v
and
\widetilde{\varphi}
except the mismatched term involving
\widetilde{\varphi}-\varphi
. This is easy to see because the only term containing 5 derivatives is the one on the left side of (
4.27
). Therefore, a straightforward
L^{2}
estimate for (
4.27
) gives us the energy estimate

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\frac{1}{2}\|\nabla^{\widetilde{\varphi}}\times v\|_{3}^{2}\leq P(\|v\|_{4},|\widetilde{\psi}|_{4},\|\mathcal{F}^{\prime}(q)\partial q\|_{\infty},\|\mathcal{F}^{\prime}(q)\partial^{2}q\|_{1},\kappa|\overline{\nabla}\partial_{t}\psi|_{4}),

(4.28)

where the mismatched term is controlled by using mollifier property (
3.10
) and
\varphi(t,x)=x_{3}+\chi(x_{3})\psi(t,x^{\prime})
.

Similarly, we replace
\partial^{3}
by
\partial_{t}^{k}\partial^{3-k}
for
0\leq k\leq 3
to get

\rho D_{t}^{\widetilde{\varphi}}\left(\partial^{3-k}\partial_{t}^{k}(\nabla^{\widetilde{\varphi}}\times v)_{i}\right)=-\epsilon^{ijk}\partial_{t}^{k}\partial^{3-k}(\rho\partial^{\widetilde{\varphi}}_{j}v^{l}\partial^{\widetilde{\varphi}}_{l}v_{k})-\epsilon^{ijk}\partial_{t}^{k}\partial^{3-k}(\rho\partial^{\widetilde{\varphi}}_{j}\partial_{t}(\widetilde{\varphi}-\varphi)\partial^{\widetilde{\varphi}}_{3}v_{k})-[\partial_{t}^{k}\partial^{3-k},\rho D_{t}^{\widetilde{\varphi}}](\nabla^{\widetilde{\varphi}}\times v)_{i},

(4.29)

and thus

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\frac{1}{2}\|\partial_{t}^{k}(\nabla^{\widetilde{\varphi}}\times v)\|_{3-k}^{2}\leq P(E^{\kappa}(t)).

(4.30)

Then we need to estimate the commutator
\|[\partial_{t}^{k},\nabla^{\widetilde{\varphi}}\times]v\|_{3-k}
to get the control of
\|\nabla^{\widetilde{\varphi}}\times\partial_{t}^{k}v\|_{3-k}
. Similarly, as in the control of divergence, we know the highest order term in the commutator should be
\|(-\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi})^{-1}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{k}\widetilde{\varphi}\partial_{3}v\|_{3-k}\lesssim\|\partial v\|_{3-k}\|\overline{\partial}{}\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{3-k}\partial_{t}^{k}\widetilde{\varphi}\|_{0}\leq\|\partial v\|_{3-k}|\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{k}\widetilde{\psi}|_{3-k}
. So we have the following conclusion

\|\nabla^{\widetilde{\varphi}}\times\partial_{t}^{k}v\|_{3-k}^{2}\leq|\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{k}\widetilde{\psi}|_{3-k}^{2}+\mathcal{P}_{0}^{\kappa}+\int_{0}^{T}P(E^{\kappa}(t))\,\mathrm{d}t.

(4.31)

Combining (
4.22
), (
4.24
), (
4.25
), (
4.28
), (
4.31
) and the argument in Section
4.2
, it remains to control the tangential derivatives of
v
and full time derivatives of
q
, namely
\|\mathcal{F}^{\prime}(q)\partial_{t}^{4}\check{q}\|_{0}
.

4.4
The
\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}
-differentiated equations

By the div-curl analysis, the crucial step is to study the higher order tangential energy estimate of (
3.11
). In particular, we define the following tangential derivatives

\mathcal{T}_{0}=\partial_{t},\quad\mathcal{T}_{1}=\partial_{1},\quad\mathcal{T}_{2}=\partial_{2},\quad\mathcal{T}_{3}=\omega(x_{3})\partial_{3},

(4.32)

where
\omega\in C^{\infty}(-b,0)
is assumed to be bounded, comparable to
|x_{3}|
when
-2\leq x_{3}\leq 0
and vanishing on
\Sigma
. This requires us to commute
\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}
with (
3.11
), where
\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}:=\mathcal{T}_{0}^{\alpha_{0}}\mathcal{T}_{1}^{\alpha_{1}}\mathcal{T}_{2}^{\alpha_{2}}\mathcal{T}_{3}^{\alpha_{3}}
, and
|\alpha|\leq 4
.

Remark 4.1
.

We need the tangential derivative
\mathcal{T}_{3}=\omega(x_{3})\partial_{3}
to control the
(\partial_{3}\varphi)^{-1}(v\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{N}}-\partial_{t}\varphi)\partial_{3}
in the material derivative
D_{t}^{\widetilde{\varphi}}
. We do not include it in
E^{\kappa}(t)
as
\omega
is comparable to 1. However, we still need the estimates of
\mathcal{T}_{3}
in the reduction of
\check{q}
.

We will not directly commute
\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}
with
\nabla^{\widetilde{\varphi}}
. Instead, for
i=1,2,3
, we observe that

\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}\partial^{\widetilde{\varphi}}_{i}f=\partial^{\widetilde{\varphi}}_{i}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}f-\partial^{\widetilde{\varphi}}_{3}f\partial^{\widetilde{\varphi}}_{i}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}\widetilde{\varphi}+\mathfrak{C}_{i}^{\prime}(f),

(4.33)

where for
i=1,2
,

\displaystyle\mathfrak{C}_{i}^{\prime}(f)=-\left[\mathcal{T}^{\alpha},\frac{\partial_{i}\widetilde{\varphi}}{\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}},\partial_{3}f\right]-\partial_{3}f\left[\mathcal{T}^{\alpha},\partial_{i}\widetilde{\varphi},\frac{1}{\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}}\right]-\partial_{i}\widetilde{\varphi}\partial_{3}f\left[\mathcal{T}^{\alpha-\gamma},\frac{1}{(\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi})^{2}}\right]\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}-\frac{\partial_{i}\widetilde{\varphi}}{\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}}[\mathcal{T}^{\alpha},\partial_{3}]f+\frac{\partial_{i}\widetilde{\varphi}}{(\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi})^{2}}\partial_{3}f[\mathcal{T}^{\alpha},\partial_{3}]\widetilde{\varphi},

(4.34)

with
|\gamma|=1
, and

\displaystyle\mathfrak{C}_{3}^{\prime}(f)=\left[\mathcal{T}^{\alpha},\frac{1}{\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}},\partial_{3}f\right]+\partial_{3}f\left[\mathcal{T}^{\alpha-\gamma},\frac{1}{(\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi})^{2}}\right]\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}+\frac{1}{\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}}[\mathcal{T}^{\alpha},\partial_{3}]f-\frac{1}{(\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi})^{2}}\partial_{3}f[\mathcal{T}^{\alpha},\partial_{3}]\widetilde{\varphi}.

(4.35)

Since
\partial^{\widetilde{\varphi}}_{i}
and
\partial^{\widetilde{\varphi}}_{3}
commute, the identity (
4.33
) implies

\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}\partial^{\widetilde{\varphi}}_{i}f=\partial^{\widetilde{\varphi}}_{i}(\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}f-\partial^{\widetilde{\varphi}}_{3}f\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}\widetilde{\varphi})+\underbrace{\partial^{\widetilde{\varphi}}_{3}\partial^{\widetilde{\varphi}}_{i}f\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}\widetilde{\varphi}+\mathfrak{C}_{i}^{\prime}(f)}_{:=\mathfrak{C}_{i}(f)}.

(4.36)

The quantity
\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}f-\partial^{\widetilde{\varphi}}_{3}f\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}\widetilde{\varphi}
is the so-called Alinhac good unknown associated with
f
. It was first observed by Alinhac
[
7
]
that the top order term of
\widetilde{\varphi}
does not appear when we use the above good unknown. It is not hard to see that we can obtain the control of
\|\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}f\|_{0}
from that of
\|\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}f-\partial^{\widetilde{\varphi}}_{3}f\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}\widetilde{\varphi}\|_{0}
. In particular,

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}f\|_{0}\leq\|\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}f-\partial^{\widetilde{\varphi}}_{3}f\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}\widetilde{\varphi}\|_{0}+\|\partial^{\widetilde{\varphi}}_{3}f\|_{\infty}\|\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}\widetilde{\varphi}\|_{0}.

(4.37)

In addition to this, we need to commute
\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}
with

D_{t}^{\widetilde{\varphi}}=\partial_{t}+\overline{v}\cdot\overline{\nabla}+\frac{1}{\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}}(v\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{N}}-\partial_{t}\varphi)\partial_{3}.

A direct computation yields:

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}D_{t}^{\widetilde{\varphi}}f

\displaystyle=\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}\partial_{t}f+\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}(\overline{v}\cdot\overline{\partial}{}f)+\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}\left(\frac{1}{\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}}(v\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{N}}-\partial_{t}\varphi)\partial_{3}f\right)

\displaystyle=D_{t}^{\widetilde{\varphi}}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}f+(v\cdot\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}\widetilde{\mathbf{N}}-\partial_{t}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}\varphi)\partial^{\widetilde{\varphi}}_{3}f-\partial^{\widetilde{\varphi}}_{3}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}\widetilde{\varphi}(v\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{N}}-\partial_{t}\varphi)\partial^{\widetilde{\varphi}}_{3}f+\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}(f),

(4.38)

where

\displaystyle\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}(f)=[\mathcal{T}^{\alpha},\overline{v}]\cdot\overline{\partial}{}f+\left[\mathcal{T}^{\alpha},\frac{1}{\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}}(v\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{N}}-\partial_{t}\varphi),\partial_{3}f\right]+\left[\mathcal{T}^{\alpha},v\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{N}}-\partial_{t}\varphi,\frac{1}{\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}}\right]\partial_{3}f+\frac{1}{\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}}[\mathcal{T}^{\alpha},v]\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{N}}\partial_{3}f

\displaystyle-(v\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{N}}-\partial_{t}\varphi)\partial_{3}f\left[\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha-\gamma},\frac{1}{(\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi})^{2}}\right]\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}+\frac{1}{\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}}(v\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{N}}-\partial_{t}\varphi)[\mathcal{T}^{\alpha},\partial_{3}]f+(v\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{N}}-\partial_{t}\varphi)\frac{\partial_{3}f}{(\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi})^{2}}[\mathcal{T}^{\alpha},\partial_{3}]\widetilde{\varphi},

(4.39)

with
|\gamma|=1
.

Since
v\cdot\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}\widetilde{\mathbf{N}}=-v_{1}\partial_{1}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}\widetilde{\varphi}-v_{2}\partial_{2}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}\widetilde{\varphi}
, then we must have

\displaystyle(v\cdot\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}\widetilde{\mathbf{N}}-\partial_{t}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}\varphi)\partial^{\widetilde{\varphi}}_{3}f-\partial^{\widetilde{\varphi}}_{3}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}\widetilde{\varphi}(v\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{N}}-\partial_{t}\varphi)\partial^{\widetilde{\varphi}}_{3}f

(4.40)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(v\cdot\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}\widetilde{\mathbf{N}}-\partial_{t}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}\widetilde{\varphi})\partial^{\widetilde{\varphi}}_{3}f-\partial^{\widetilde{\varphi}}_{3}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}\widetilde{\varphi}(v\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{N}}-\partial_{t}\varphi)\partial^{\widetilde{\varphi}}_{3}f+\partial_{t}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}(\widetilde{\varphi}-\varphi)\partial^{\widetilde{\varphi}}_{3}f

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\partial^{\widetilde{\varphi}}_{3}f\left(\partial_{t}+\overline{v}\cdot\overline{\nabla}+(v\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{N}}-\partial_{t}\varphi)\partial^{\widetilde{\varphi}}_{3}\right)\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}\widetilde{\varphi}+\underbrace{\partial_{t}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}(\widetilde{\varphi}-\varphi)\partial^{\widetilde{\varphi}}_{3}f}_{:=\mathfrak{E}(f)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\partial^{\widetilde{\varphi}}_{3}fD_{t}^{\widetilde{\varphi}}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}\widetilde{\varphi}+\mathfrak{E}(f).

Thus,

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}D_{t}^{\widetilde{\varphi}}f

\displaystyle=D_{t}^{\widetilde{\varphi}}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}f-\partial^{\widetilde{\varphi}}_{3}fD_{t}^{\widetilde{\varphi}}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}\widetilde{\varphi}+\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}(f)+\mathfrak{E}(f)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle D_{t}^{\widetilde{\varphi}}\left(\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}f-\partial^{\widetilde{\varphi}}_{3}f\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}\widetilde{\varphi}\right)+\mathfrak{D}(f)+\mathfrak{E}(f),

(4.41)

where
\mathfrak{D}(f)=(D_{t}^{\widetilde{\varphi}}\partial^{\widetilde{\varphi}}_{3}f)\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}\widetilde{\varphi}+\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}(f)
.

Let

\displaystyle\mathbf{V}_{i}:=\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}v_{i}-\partial^{\widetilde{\varphi}}_{3}v_{i}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}\widetilde{\varphi},\quad\mathbf{Q}:=\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}\check{q}-\partial^{\widetilde{\varphi}}_{3}\check{q}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}\widetilde{\varphi}

(4.42)

respectively be the Alinhac good unknowns of
v
and
\check{q}
. Motivated by (
4.36
) and (
4.41
), we take
\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}
to the first two equations of (
1.5
) to obtain

\displaystyle\rho D_{t}^{\widetilde{\varphi}}\mathbf{V}_{i}+\partial^{\widetilde{\varphi}}_{i}\mathbf{Q}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{R}^{1}_{i},

(4.43)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{\prime}(q)D_{t}^{\widetilde{\varphi}}\mathbf{Q}+\nabla^{\widetilde{\varphi}}\cdot\mathbf{V}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{R}^{2}-\mathfrak{C}_{i}(v^{i}),

(4.44)

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}^{1}_{i}:=

\displaystyle-[\mathcal{T}^{\alpha},\rho]D_{t}^{\widetilde{\varphi}}v_{i}-\rho\left(\mathfrak{D}(v_{i})+\mathfrak{E}(v_{i})\right)-\mathfrak{C}_{i}(\check{q}),

(4.45)

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}^{2}:=

\displaystyle-[\mathcal{T}^{\alpha},\mathcal{F}^{\prime}(q)]D_{t}^{\widetilde{\varphi}}\check{q}-\mathcal{F}^{\prime}(q)\left(\mathfrak{D}(\check{q})+\mathfrak{E}(\check{q})\right)+\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}(\mathcal{F}^{\prime}(q)gv_{3}).

(4.46)

In addition, since
\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}
reduces to
\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}
on
\Sigma
and
\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\widetilde{N}=(-\partial_{1}\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\widetilde{\psi},-\partial_{2}\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\widetilde{\psi},0)^{\top}
, the
\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}
-differentiated kinematic boundary condition then reads

\partial_{t}\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\psi+(\overline{v}\cdot\overline{\nabla})\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\widetilde{\psi}-\mathbf{V}\cdot\widetilde{N}=\mathcal{S}_{1}\quad\text{on}\,\,\Sigma,\quad\text{ and }\mathbf{V}_{3}=0\quad\text{on}\,\,\Sigma_{b},

(4.47)

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{1}:=\partial_{3}v\cdot\widetilde{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\widetilde{\psi}+[\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha},v\cdot,N].

(4.48)

Also, since
\check{q}=q+g\widetilde{\psi}
and
\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}|_{\Sigma}=1
, we have
\mathbf{Q}|_{\Sigma}=(\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\check{q}-\partial_{3}\check{q}\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\widetilde{\psi})|_{\Sigma}=\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}q+g\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\widetilde{\psi}-(\partial_{3}q+g)\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\widetilde{\psi}=\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}q-\partial_{3}q\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\widetilde{\psi}
, and thus the boundary condition of
\mathbf{Q}
on
\Sigma
reads:

\displaystyle\mathbf{Q}=-\sigma\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\overline{\nabla}\cdot\left(\frac{\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}}{\sqrt{1+|\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}|^{2}}}\right)+\kappa^{2}(1-\overline{\Delta}{})\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}(v\cdot\widetilde{N})-\partial_{3}q\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\widetilde{\psi}.

(4.49)

4.5
Tangential energy estimate with full spatial derivatives

In this subsection, we study the spatially-differentiated equations, i.e., the equations obtained by commuting
\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}
,
\alpha_{0}=0
, and
|\alpha|=4
, with (
3.11
). We aim to prove the following estimate:

Proposition 4.3
.

For
\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}
with multi-index
\alpha
satisfying
\alpha_{0}=0
and
|\alpha|=4
, we have

\|\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}v(T)\|_{0}^{2}+\left\|\sqrt{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}(q)}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}\check{q}(T)\right\|_{0}^{2}+\left|\sqrt{\sigma}\overline{\nabla}\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\Lambda_{\kappa}\psi(T)\right|_{0}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}\left|\kappa\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\partial_{t}\psi(t)\right|_{1}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{0}^{\kappa}+\int_{0}^{T}P(E^{\kappa}(t))\,\mathrm{d}t.

(4.50)

We will not directly consider the
\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}
-differentiated variables but use Alinhac good unknowns to get rid of higher order terms of
\widetilde{\psi}
. Invoking Lemma
A.2
and Theorem
A.3
, testing (
4.43
) with
\mathbf{V}
and then integrating over
\Omega
with respect to the measure
\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}\,\mathrm{d}x
, we get

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\Omega}\rho|\mathbf{V}|^{2}\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}\,\mathrm{d}x=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega}\rho|\mathbf{V}|^{2}\partial_{3}\partial_{t}(\widetilde{\varphi}-\varphi)\,\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Omega}\mathbf{Q}(\nabla^{\widetilde{\varphi}}\cdot\mathbf{V})\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}\,\mathrm{d}x-\int_{\Sigma}\mathbf{Q}(\mathbf{V}\cdot\widetilde{N})\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}+\int_{\Omega}\mathbf{V}\cdot\mathcal{R}^{1}\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}\,\mathrm{d}x,

(4.51)

where the boundary integral on
\Sigma_{b}
vanishes thanks to
\mathbf{V}_{3}|_{\Sigma}=0
. From now on, we will no longer write any boundary integral on
\Sigma_{b}
due to the same reason.
Before estimating the integrals in (
4.51
),
we record some important properties that Alinhac good unknowns enjoy.

Lemma 4.4
.

Let
\mathbf{F}:=\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}f-\partial^{\widetilde{\varphi}}_{3}f\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}\widetilde{\varphi}
with
|\alpha|=4
and
\alpha_{0}=0
be the Alinhac good unknowns associated with the smooth function
f
.
Suppose that
\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}\geq c_{0}>0
, then

\|\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}f\|_{0}\leq\|\mathbf{F}\|_{0}+P\left(c_{0}^{-1},|\widetilde{\psi}|_{4}\right)\|\partial_{3}f\|_{\infty}.

(4.52)

Furthermore, let
\mathfrak{C}(f)
,
\mathfrak{D}(f)
, and
\mathfrak{E}(f)
be the remainder terms defined respectively in (
4.36
), (
4.40
), and (
4.41
). Then

\displaystyle\|\mathfrak{C}_{i}(f)\|_{0}\leq

\displaystyle~P\left(c_{0}^{-1},|\widetilde{\psi}|_{4}\right)\cdot\|f\|_{4},\quad i=1,2,3,

(4.53)

\displaystyle\|\mathfrak{D}(f)\|_{0}\leq

\displaystyle~P\left(c_{0}^{-1},|\widetilde{\psi}|_{4},|\partial_{t}\widetilde{\psi}|_{3}\right)\cdot\left(\|f\|_{4}+\|\partial_{t}f\|_{3}\right),

(4.54)

\displaystyle\|\mathfrak{E}(f)\|_{0}\leq

\displaystyle~\kappa|\overline{\nabla}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}\partial_{t}\psi|_{0}\|\partial f\|_{\infty}.

(4.55)

Proof.

Since
\partial^{\widetilde{\varphi}}_{3}=(\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi})^{-1}\partial_{3}
,
we have

\displaystyle\|\partial^{\widetilde{\varphi}}_{3}f\|_{\infty}\|\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}\widetilde{\varphi}\|_{0}\leq P\left(c_{0}^{-1},|\widetilde{\psi}|_{4}\right)\|\partial_{3}f\|_{\infty},

(4.56)

and so (
4.52
) follows from (
4.37
).
Also, the estimates (
4.53
) and (
4.54
) follow from the definition of
\mathfrak{C}(f)
and
\mathfrak{D}(f)
, (
1.9
), (
3.7
) in Lemma
3.1
, and the Sobolev inequalities.
To establish (
4.55
), we notice that

\displaystyle\|\mathfrak{E}(f)\|_{0}\leq\|\partial_{t}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}(\widetilde{\varphi}-\varphi)\|_{0}\|\partial^{\widetilde{\varphi}}_{3}f\|_{\infty}+\|\partial^{\widetilde{\varphi}}_{3}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}\widetilde{\varphi}\|_{0}\|\partial_{t}(\widetilde{\varphi}-\varphi)\|_{\infty}\|\partial^{\widetilde{\varphi}}_{3}f\|_{\infty}.

Thus, it suffices to control the leading order terms
\|\partial_{t}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}(\widetilde{\varphi}-\varphi)\|_{0}
and
\|\partial^{\widetilde{\varphi}}_{3}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}\widetilde{\varphi}\|_{0}
. We have

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}(\widetilde{\varphi}-\varphi)=

\displaystyle~\partial_{t}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}\left(\chi(x_{3})\widetilde{\psi}-\chi(x_{3})\psi\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\chi(x_{3})\partial_{t}\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}(\widetilde{\psi}-\psi)+\left[\mathcal{T}^{\alpha},\chi(x_{3})\right]\partial_{t}(\widetilde{\psi}-\psi).

The
L^{2}
-norm of the second term can be controlled by the RHS of (
4.55
) thanks to (
1.9
).
By (
3.6
) in Lemma
3.1
, we have

\displaystyle|\partial_{t}\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}(\widetilde{\psi}-\psi)|_{0}\leq\kappa|\partial_{t}\psi|_{5}.

Also,

\displaystyle\partial^{\widetilde{\varphi}}_{3}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}\widetilde{\varphi}=\partial^{\widetilde{\varphi}}_{3}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}\left(\chi(x_{3})\widetilde{\psi}\right)=\left(\partial^{\widetilde{\varphi}}_{3}\chi(x_{3})\right)\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}\widetilde{\psi}+\left(\partial^{\widetilde{\varphi}}_{3}[\mathcal{T}^{\alpha},\chi(x_{3})]\right)\widetilde{\psi},

and so
\|\partial^{\widetilde{\varphi}}_{3}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}\widetilde{\varphi}\|_{0}
can be controlled by the RHS of (
4.55
).
∎

Remark 4.2
.

The appearance of
\mathfrak{E}(f)
is a consequence of the tangential smoothing. This estimate of
\|\mathfrak{E}(f)\|_{0}
yields a top order term
\kappa|\partial_{t}\psi|_{5}
, which can only be controlled by the energy contributed by the artificial viscosity. In other words, the artificial viscosity compensates for the loss of symmetry in the
\kappa
-equations.

4.5.1
Control of
\int_{\Omega}\rho|\mathbf{V}|^{2}\partial_{3}\partial_{t}(\widetilde{\varphi}-\varphi)\,\mathrm{d}x
: The integral contains the mismatched term.

We have

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\rho|\mathbf{V}|^{2}\partial_{3}\partial_{t}(\widetilde{\varphi}-\varphi)\,\mathrm{d}x\leq\|\rho\|_{\infty}\|\mathbf{V}\|_{0}^{2}\|\partial_{3}\partial_{t}(\widetilde{\varphi}-\varphi)\|_{\infty}\lesssim\sqrt{\kappa}\|\mathbf{V}\|_{0}^{2}|\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}\psi|_{0.5}.

(4.57)

4.5.2
Control of
\int_{\Omega}\mathbf{V}\cdot\mathcal{R}^{1}\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}\,\mathrm{d}x
: Error terms

We have

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\mathbf{V}\cdot\mathcal{R}^{1}\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}\,\mathrm{d}x\leq\|\mathbf{V}\|_{0}\|\mathcal{R}^{1}\|_{0}\|\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}\|_{\infty},

(4.58)

where the
L^{2}
-norm of
\mathcal{R}^{1}
is directly controlled by using (
4.45
) and (
4.53
)–(
4.55
):

\|\mathcal{R}^{1}\|_{0}\leq P(\|\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}\|_{\infty},|\widetilde{\psi}|_{4},|\partial_{t}\widetilde{\psi}|_{3})\left(\kappa|\overline{\nabla}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}\partial_{t}\psi|_{0}\|v\|_{4}+{\|v\|_{4}+\|\partial_{t}v\|_{3}+\|\check{q}\|_{4}}\right),

(4.59)

where the term containing
\kappa|\overline{\nabla}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}\partial_{t}\psi|_{0}
should be controlled under time integral as we will get
L_{t}^{2}H_{x^{\prime}}^{1}([0,T]\times\Sigma)
bound for
\kappa\partial_{t}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}\psi
later.

4.5.3
Control of
\int_{\Omega}\mathbf{Q}(\nabla^{\widetilde{\varphi}}\cdot\mathbf{V})\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}\,dx
: Tangential energy for
\mathbf{Q}

Equation (
4.44
) indicates that

\int_{\Omega}\mathbf{Q}(\nabla^{\widetilde{\varphi}}\cdot\mathbf{V})\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}\,\mathrm{d}x=-\int_{\Omega}\mathcal{F}^{\prime}(q)\mathbf{Q}(D_{t}^{\widetilde{\varphi}}\mathbf{Q})\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}\,\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Omega}\mathbf{Q}(\mathcal{R}^{2}-\mathfrak{C}_{i}(v^{i}))\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}\,\mathrm{d}x.

(4.60)

For the second term on the RHS of (
4.60
), we invoke the second inequality in (
1.30
) and then apply it to the definition of
\mathcal{R}^{2}
in (
4.46
) to get:

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\mathbf{Q}\mathcal{R}^{2}\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}\,\mathrm{d}x\leq\|\sqrt{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}(q)}\mathbf{Q}\|_{0}\|\mathcal{R}^{2}\|_{0}\|\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}\|_{\infty}.

(4.61)

In other words, we “borrow” one
\sqrt{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}(q)}
from
\mathcal{R}^{2}
and attach it to
\mathbf{Q}
. Thanks to (
4.53
)-(
4.55
),
we control the
L^{2}
-norm of the rest of terms in
\mathcal{R}^{2}
directly by

P(\|\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}\|_{\infty},|\widetilde{\psi}|_{4},|\partial_{t}\widetilde{\psi}|_{3})\left(\kappa|\overline{\nabla}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}\partial_{t}\psi|_{0}\left\|\sqrt{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}(q)}\check{q}\right\|_{4}+\left\|\sqrt{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}(q)}\check{q}\right\|_{4}+\left\|\sqrt{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}(q)}\partial_{t}\check{q}\right\|_{3}+\left\|\sqrt{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}(q)}gv_{3}\right\|_{3}\right),

(4.62)

where the term containing
\kappa|\overline{\nabla}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}\partial_{t}\psi|_{0}
should be controlled under time integral as we will get
L_{t}^{2}H_{x^{\prime}}^{1}([0,T]\times\Sigma)
bound for
\kappa\partial_{t}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}\psi
later. Then the contribution of
\mathfrak{C}_{i}(v^{i})
is controlled by

-\int_{\Omega}\mathbf{Q}(\mathfrak{C}_{i}(v^{i}))\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}\,\mathrm{d}x\leq P(|\widetilde{\psi}|_{4},|\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}|_{W^{1,\infty}})|\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}\widetilde{\psi}|_{0}\|v\|_{4}\|\mathbf{Q}\|_{0}.

(4.63)

Here,
\|\mathbf{Q}\|_{0}
contributes to
\|\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\check{q}\|_{0}
and
\|\partial^{\widetilde{\varphi}}_{3}\check{q}\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\widetilde{\psi}\|_{0}
. The first term
\|\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\check{q}\|_{0}
is not weighted by
\sqrt{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}(q)}
and thus cannot be controlled directly by (
4.50
). Fortunately, we can overcome this issue by invoking (
4.17
). Similarly,
\|\partial^{\widetilde{\varphi}}_{3}\check{q}\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\widetilde{\psi}\|_{0}\leq\|\partial^{\widetilde{\varphi}}_{3}\check{q}\|_{\infty}\|\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\widetilde{\psi}\|_{0}
, where we use (
4.20
) to treat
\|\partial^{\widetilde{\varphi}}_{3}\check{q}\|_{\infty}
, and so this can be controlled uniformly as
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}(q)\to 0
.

Furthermore, invoking the integration by parts formula (
A.8
), the first integral on the RHS of (
4.60
) becomes

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\mathcal{F}^{\prime}(q)\mathbf{Q}(D_{t}^{\widetilde{\varphi}}\mathbf{Q})\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}\,\mathrm{d}x=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{\Omega}\mathcal{F}^{\prime}(q)|\mathbf{Q}|^{2}\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}\,\mathrm{d}x+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega}(D_{t}^{\widetilde{\varphi}}\mathcal{F}^{\prime}(q))|\mathbf{Q}|^{2}\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}\,\mathrm{d}x

(4.64)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega}(\nabla^{\widetilde{\varphi}}\cdot v)\mathcal{F}^{\prime}(q)|\mathbf{Q}|^{2}\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}\,\mathrm{d}x+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega}\mathcal{F}^{\prime}(q)|\mathbf{Q}|^{2}\partial_{3}\partial_{t}(\widetilde{\varphi}-\varphi)\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left\|\sqrt{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}(q)}\mathbf{Q}\right\|_{0}^{2}+\|\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}\|_{\infty}\|\sqrt{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}(q)}\mathbf{Q}\|_{0}^{2}\left(\|\partial v\|_{\infty}+\kappa|\overline{\nabla}\partial_{t}\psi|_{0.5}\right).

4.5.4
Control of
-\int_{\Sigma}\mathbf{Q}(\mathbf{V}\cdot\widetilde{N})\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}
: Boundary energy contributed by surface tension and artificial viscosity

Note that
\mathcal{T}_{3}=\vec{0}
on
\Sigma
implies the corresponding good unknown
\mathbf{Q}=0
on
\Sigma
, so it suffices to consider the case
\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}=\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}
when analyzing the boundary integral. Using (
4.47
), we have

\displaystyle-\int_{\Sigma}\mathbf{Q}(\mathbf{V}\cdot\widetilde{N})\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}=-\int_{\Sigma}\mathbf{Q}\left(\partial_{t}\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\psi+(\overline{v}\cdot\overline{\nabla})\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\widetilde{\psi}-\mathcal{S}_{1}\right)\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}.

(4.65)

The first term is expected to contribute to two coercive terms if we invoke the boundary condition (
4.49
) of
\mathbf{Q}
:

\displaystyle I_{1}:=-\int_{\Sigma}\mathbf{Q}\partial_{t}\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}=

\displaystyle\sigma\int_{\Sigma}\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\overline{\nabla}\cdot\left(\frac{\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}}{\sqrt{1+|\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}|^{2}}}\right)\partial_{t}\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}-\kappa^{2}\int_{\Sigma}\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}(1-\overline{\Delta}{})\partial_{t}\psi\cdot\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\partial_{t}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}+\int_{\Sigma}\partial_{3}q\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\widetilde{\psi}\partial_{t}\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}

(4.66)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle:\text{ ST}_{1}+\text{ ST}_{2}+\text{ RT}.

Since
1-\overline{\Delta}{}=\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{2}
, where
\langle\cdot\rangle
denotes the Japanese bracket, we find the term
\text{ ST}_{2}
gives us
\sqrt{\kappa}
-weighted enhanced energy after integration by parts :

\text{ ST}_{2}=-\kappa^{2}\int_{\Sigma}\left|\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle\partial_{t}\psi\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}=-\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left|\kappa\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\partial_{t}\psi\right|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{x^{\prime}}^{1}}^{2}.

(4.67)

In the control of
\text{ ST}_{1}
, we will repeatedly use

\overline{\partial}{}\left(\frac{1}{|\widetilde{N}|}\right)=\frac{\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}\cdot\overline{\partial}{}\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}}{|\widetilde{N}|^{3}},

(4.68)

where
|\widetilde{N}|=\sqrt{1+|\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}|^{2}}
denotes the length of the smoothed normal vector
\widetilde{N}=(-\overline{\partial}{}_{1}\widetilde{\psi},-\overline{\partial}{}_{2}\widetilde{\psi},1)^{\top}
.
Now we integrate
\overline{\nabla}\cdot
by parts in
\text{ ST}_{1}
to get

\displaystyle\text{ ST}_{1}=

\displaystyle-\sigma\int_{\Sigma}\frac{\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}}{|\widetilde{N}|}\cdot\partial_{t}\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\overline{\nabla}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}+\sigma\int_{\Sigma}\frac{\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}\cdot\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}}{|\widetilde{N}|^{3}}\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}\cdot\partial_{t}\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\overline{\nabla}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}

(4.69)

\displaystyle-\sigma\int_{\Sigma}\left(\left[\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha-\alpha^{\prime}},\frac{1}{|\widetilde{N}|}\right]\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha^{\prime}}\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}+\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}\left[\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha-\alpha^{\prime}},\frac{1}{|\widetilde{N}|^{3}}\right](\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}\cdot\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha^{\prime}}\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi})-\frac{1}{|\widetilde{N}|^{3}}\left[\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha-\alpha^{\prime}},\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}\right]\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha^{\prime}}\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}\right)\cdot\partial_{t}\overline{\nabla}\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle:\text{ ST}_{11}+\text{ ST}_{12}+\text{ ST}_{13},

where
\alpha^{\prime}
is a multi-index with
|\alpha^{\prime}|=1
.

The first two terms in (
4.69
) are expected to produce the energy contributed by the surface tension. Before that, we need to move one mollifier from the top order term of
\widetilde{\psi}=\Lambda_{\kappa}^{2}\psi
to the top order term of
\psi
by using the self-adjointness of
\Lambda_{\kappa}
in
L^{2}(\Sigma)
:

\displaystyle\text{ ST}_{11}+\text{ ST}_{12}=

\displaystyle-\sigma\int_{\Sigma}\dfrac{\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\overline{\nabla}\Lambda_{\kappa}\psi\cdot\partial_{t}\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\overline{\nabla}\Lambda_{\kappa}\psi}{|\widetilde{N}|}-\dfrac{(\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}\cdot\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\overline{\nabla}\Lambda_{\kappa}\psi)(\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}\cdot\partial_{t}\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\overline{\nabla}\Lambda_{\kappa}\psi)}{|\widetilde{N}|^{3}}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}

(4.70)

\displaystyle-\sigma\int_{\Sigma}\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\overline{\nabla}\Lambda_{\kappa}\psi\cdot\left(\left[\Lambda_{\kappa},\frac{1}{|\widetilde{N}|}\right]\overline{\nabla}\partial_{t}\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\psi\right)\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}+\sigma\int_{\Sigma}\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\overline{\nabla}_{i}\Lambda_{\kappa}\psi\cdot\left(\left[\Lambda_{\kappa},\frac{\overline{\nabla}_{i}\widetilde{\psi}\overline{\nabla}_{j}\widetilde{\psi}}{|\widetilde{N}|^{3}}\right]\overline{\nabla}_{j}\partial_{t}\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\psi\right)\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle\text{ ST}_{10}+\text{ ST}_{11}^{R}+\text{ ST}_{12}^{R}.

Then we find

\displaystyle\text{ ST}_{10}=

\displaystyle-\frac{\sigma}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{\Sigma}\frac{|\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\overline{\nabla}\Lambda_{\kappa}\psi|^{2}}{\sqrt{1+|\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}|^{2}}}-\frac{|\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}\cdot\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\overline{\nabla}\Lambda_{\kappa}\psi|^{2}}{{\sqrt{1+|\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}|^{2}}}^{3}}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}

(4.71)

\displaystyle+\frac{\sigma}{2}\int_{\Sigma}\partial_{t}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{1+|\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}|^{2}}}\right)\left|\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\overline{\nabla}\Lambda_{\kappa}\psi\right|^{2}-\partial_{t}\left(\frac{1}{{\sqrt{1+|\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}|^{2}}}^{3}}\right)\left|\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}\cdot\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\overline{\nabla}\Lambda_{\kappa}\psi\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}.

(4.72)

To deal with the first term in
\text{ ST}_{10}
, we plug
\mathbf{a}=\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\overline{\nabla}\Lambda_{\kappa}\psi
into the following inequality which can be proved by direct calculation:

\frac{|\mathbf{a}|^{2}}{\sqrt{1+|\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}|^{2}}}-\frac{|\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}\cdot\mathbf{a}|^{2}}{{\sqrt{1+|\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}|^{2}}}^{3}}\geq\frac{|\mathbf{a}|^{2}}{{\sqrt{1+|\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}|^{2}}}^{3}},

(4.73)

in order to get

\int_{0}^{T}\text{ ST}_{10}\,\mathrm{d}t+\frac{\sigma}{2}\int_{\Sigma}\frac{|\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\overline{\nabla}\Lambda_{\kappa}\psi|^{2}}{{\sqrt{1+|\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}|^{2}}}^{3}}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}\leq P(|\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}_{0}|_{L^{\infty}})\left|\sqrt{\sigma}\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\overline{\nabla}\Lambda_{\kappa}\psi_{0}\right|_{0}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}\eqref{ST10R}\,\mathrm{d}t,

(4.74)

where the terms in (
4.72
) can be controlled directly:

\eqref{ST10R}\leq P(|\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}|_{L^{\infty}})|\partial_{t}\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}|_{L^{\infty}}\left|\sqrt{\sigma}\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\overline{\nabla}\Lambda_{\kappa}\psi\right|_{0}^{2}.

(4.75)

To finish the control of
\text{ ST}_{1}
, it remains to control
\text{ ST}_{13}
and
\text{ ST}_{11}^{R},\text{ ST}_{12}^{R}
. The last two terms can be controlled by using the mollifier property (
3.10
) and the
\kappa
-weighted energy contributed by the artificial viscosity. For
\text{ ST}_{11}^{R}
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\text{ ST}_{11}^{R}\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\left|\sqrt{\sigma}\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\Lambda_{\kappa}\psi\right|_{0}P\left(|\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}|_{\infty}\right)|\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}|_{W^{1,\infty}}\left|\kappa\partial_{t}\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\psi\right|_{\dot{H}^{1}}\,\mathrm{d}t

(4.76)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\varepsilon\left|\kappa\partial_{t}\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\psi\right|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{x^{\prime}}^{1}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}P\left(|\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}|_{\infty}\right)|\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}|_{W^{1,\infty}}^{2}\left|\sqrt{\sigma}\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\Lambda_{\kappa}\psi\right|_{0}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t.

Also,
\text{ ST}_{12}^{R}
can be controlled similarly.

As for
\text{ ST}_{13}
in (
4.69
), we find that all three commutators have similar structures and the same leading order terms, so we only show the analysis of the first commutator. Note that the leading order term in
[\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha-\alpha^{\prime}},|\widetilde{N}|^{-1}]\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha^{\prime}}\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}
appears when
\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha-\alpha^{\prime}}
falls on
|\widetilde{N}|^{-1}
or
\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha^{\prime\prime}}
falls on
|\widetilde{N}|^{-1}
and
\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha-\alpha^{\prime}-\alpha^{\prime\prime}}
falls on
\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha^{\prime}}\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}
for some
|\alpha^{\prime\prime}|=1
. In either of the two cases, the top-order term contributes to the following integral:

-\sigma\int_{\Sigma}|\widetilde{N}|^{-3}\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}~\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha-\alpha^{\prime}}\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}~\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha^{\prime}}\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}\cdot\partial_{t}\overline{\nabla}\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}.

(4.77)

We integrate one
\overline{\nabla}
by parts to get

\sigma\int_{\Sigma}|\widetilde{N}|^{-3}\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}~\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha-\alpha^{\prime}}\overline{\nabla}^{2}\widetilde{\psi}~\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha^{\prime}}\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}~\partial_{t}\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}

modulo lower order terms,
and then we move one
\Lambda_{\kappa}
from
\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha-\alpha^{\prime}}\overline{\nabla}^{2}\widetilde{\psi}
to
\partial_{t}\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\psi
such that the main term is directly controlled as:

\sigma\int_{\Sigma}|\widetilde{N}|^{-3}\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}~\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha-\alpha^{\prime}}\overline{\nabla}^{2}\Lambda_{\kappa}\psi~\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha^{\prime}}\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}~\partial_{t}\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\Lambda_{\kappa}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}\lesssim P(|\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}|_{\infty})|\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}|_{W^{1,\infty}}\left|\sqrt{\sigma}\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\overline{\nabla}\Lambda_{\kappa}\psi\right|_{0}\left|\sqrt{\sigma}\partial_{t}\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\Lambda_{\kappa}\psi\right|_{0},

(4.78)

where the last term will be controlled in
\partial_{t}\overline{\partial}{}^{3}
-estimates. Besides, we have to analyze the commutator involving
\Lambda_{\kappa}
:

\sigma\int_{\Sigma}\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha-\alpha^{\prime}}\overline{\nabla}^{2}\Lambda_{\kappa}\psi\left(\left[\Lambda_{\kappa},P(\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi})\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha^{\prime}}\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}\right]\partial_{t}\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\psi\right)\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime},

(4.79)

which is controlled under the time integral:

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\eqref{ST132}\,\mathrm{d}t\lesssim

\displaystyle\sqrt{\sigma}\int_{0}^{T}|\sqrt{\sigma}\overline{\nabla}\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\Lambda_{\kappa}\psi|_{0}\cdot\kappa|\overline{\partial}{}\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}|_{W^{1,\infty}}P(|\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}|_{W^{1,\infty}})|\partial_{t}\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\psi|_{0}\,\mathrm{d}t

(4.80)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\varepsilon\left|\kappa\partial_{t}\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\psi\right|_{L_{t}^{2}L_{x^{\prime}}^{2}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}\left|\sqrt{\sigma}\overline{\nabla}\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\Lambda_{\kappa}\psi\right|_{0}^{2}|\sqrt{\sigma}\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}|_{3.5}^{2}P(|\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}|_{2.5})\,\mathrm{d}t.

Therefore,

\int_{0}^{T}(\text{ ST}_{1}+\text{ ST}_{2})\,\mathrm{d}t+\left|\kappa\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\partial_{t}\psi\right|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{x^{\prime}}^{1}}^{2}+\frac{\sigma}{2}\left|\overline{\nabla}\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\Lambda_{\kappa}\psi(T)\right|_{0}^{2}\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{0}^{\kappa}+\int_{0}^{T}P(E^{\kappa}(t))\,\mathrm{d}t,

(4.81)

where we have chosen
\varepsilon>0
that appears above to be suitably small such that all
\varepsilon
-terms are absorbed by the
\kappa
-weighted energy.

To finish the control of
I_{1}
defined in (
4.66
), it remains to control the term
RT
. Note that when we drop the mollifier and have the Rayleigh-Taylor sign condition
-\partial_{3}q\geq\frac{c_{0}}{2}>0
assumed on
\Sigma
,
RT
should directly give us the non-
\sigma
-weighted boundary energy. But since we are now solving the gravity-capillary water wave system for fixed
\sigma>0
instead of taking vanishing surface tension limit, we cannot assume
-\partial_{3}q\geq\frac{c_{0}}{2}>0
on
\Sigma
. Thus this term is controlled by the surface tension energy after moving one
\Lambda_{\kappa}
:

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\text{ RT}\,\mathrm{d}t=

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Sigma}\partial_{3}q\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\Lambda_{\kappa}\psi\cdot\partial_{t}\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\Lambda_{\kappa}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}\,\mathrm{d}t-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Sigma}\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\Lambda_{\kappa}\psi\cdot\left(\left[\Lambda_{\kappa},\partial_{3}q\right]\partial_{t}\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\psi\right)\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}\,\mathrm{d}t

(4.82)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}|\partial q|_{L^{\infty}}\left|\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\Lambda_{\kappa}\psi\right|_{0}\left|\partial_{t}\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\Lambda_{\kappa}\psi\right|_{0}\,\mathrm{d}t+\varepsilon\left|\kappa\partial_{t}\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\psi\right|_{L_{t}^{2}L_{x^{\prime}}^{2}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}|\partial q|_{W^{1,\infty}}^{2}\left|\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\Lambda_{\kappa}\psi\right|_{0}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\varepsilon\left|\kappa\partial_{t}\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\psi\right|_{L_{t}^{2}L_{x^{\prime}}^{2}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}P\left(\|\check{q}\|_{4},\left|\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\Lambda_{\kappa}\psi\right|_{0},\left|\partial_{t}\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\Lambda_{\kappa}\psi\right|_{0}\right)\,\mathrm{d}t,

where the term
\left|\partial_{t}\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\Lambda_{\kappa}\psi\right|_{0}
is the energy term obtained in
\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha-\alpha^{\prime}}\partial_{t}
-estimates for
|\alpha^{\prime}|=1
.

Remark 4.3
.

The RHS of (
4.82
) is not uniform in
\sigma
. However, as mentioned earlier,
-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Sigma}\partial_{3}q\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\Lambda_{\kappa}\psi\cdot\partial_{t}\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\Lambda_{\kappa}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}\,\mathrm{d}t
contributes to a non-
\sigma
-weighted energy term
\int_{\Sigma}(-\partial_{3}q)|\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\Lambda_{\kappa}\psi|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t
provided the Rayleigh-Taylor sign condition holds. We shall revisit the control of RT in Section
7
, where the zero surface tension limit is considered.

Combining this with (
4.81
), we get the estimate for
I_{1}

\int_{0}^{T}I_{1}\,\mathrm{d}t+\left|\kappa\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\partial_{t}\psi\right|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{x^{\prime}}^{1}}^{2}+\frac{\sigma}{2}\left|\overline{\nabla}\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\Lambda_{\kappa}\psi(T)\right|_{0}^{2}\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{0}^{\kappa}+\int_{0}^{T}P(E^{\kappa}(t))\,\mathrm{d}t,

(4.83)

after choosing
\varepsilon>0
that appears above to be suitably small.

The second term in (
4.65
) gives

\displaystyle I_{2}:=-\int_{\Sigma}\mathbf{Q}(\overline{v}\cdot\overline{\nabla})\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\widetilde{\psi}=

\displaystyle\sigma\int_{\Sigma}\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\overline{\nabla}\cdot\left(\frac{\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}}{\sqrt{1+|\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}|^{2}}}\right)(\overline{v}\cdot\overline{\nabla})\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\widetilde{\psi}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}-\kappa^{2}\int_{\Sigma}\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}(1-\overline{\Delta}{})\partial_{t}\psi\cdot(\overline{v}\cdot\overline{\nabla})\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\widetilde{\psi}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}

(4.84)

\displaystyle+\int_{\Sigma}\partial_{3}q\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\widetilde{\psi}(\overline{v}\cdot\overline{\nabla})\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\widetilde{\psi}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle:I_{21}+I_{22}+I_{23},

where we find that
I_{22},I_{23}
can be directly controlled as follows:

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}I_{22}\,\mathrm{d}t\overset{\overline{\nabla}}{=}

\displaystyle-\kappa^{2}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Sigma}\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\overline{\nabla}\partial_{t}\psi\cdot\overline{\nabla}\left((\overline{v}\cdot\overline{\nabla})\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\widetilde{\psi}\right)\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}\,\mathrm{d}t-\kappa^{2}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Sigma}\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\partial_{t}\psi\cdot(\overline{v}\cdot\overline{\nabla})\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\widetilde{\psi}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}\,\mathrm{d}t

(4.85)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\left|\kappa\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\partial_{t}\psi\right|_{0}|\overline{\nabla}\overline{v}|_{\infty}\left|\kappa\overline{\nabla}^{2}\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\widetilde{\psi}\right|_{0}\,\mathrm{d}t+\kappa\int_{0}^{T}\left|\kappa\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\partial_{t}\psi\right|_{0}|\overline{v}|_{\infty}\left|\overline{\nabla}\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\widetilde{\psi}\right|_{0}\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\varepsilon\left|\kappa\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\partial_{t}\psi\right|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{x^{\prime}}^{1}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}|\overline{v}|_{W^{1,\infty}}^{2}\left|\overline{\nabla}\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\Lambda_{\kappa}\psi\right|_{0}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\lesssim\varepsilon\left|\kappa\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\partial_{t}\psi\right|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{x^{\prime}}^{1}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}P(E^{\kappa}(t))\,\mathrm{d}t,

where we use the mollifier property (
3.4
) to control
|\kappa\overline{\nabla}^{2}\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\widetilde{\psi}|_{0}\lesssim\kappa\cdot\kappa^{-1}|\overline{\nabla}\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\Lambda_{\kappa}\psi|_{0}
. This step also shows why the power of
\kappa
must be 2 in the artificial viscosity, otherwise, the control of
I_{22}
is not uniform in
\kappa
. For
I_{23}
we integrate
\overline{v}\cdot\overline{\nabla}
by parts to get

I_{23}=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Sigma}\overline{\nabla}\cdot(\overline{v}~\partial_{3}q)|\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\widetilde{\psi}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}\lesssim P(E^{\kappa}(t)).

(4.86)

The control of
I_{21}
is analogous to
\text{ ST}_{1}
. Following (
4.69
), we have

\displaystyle I_{21}=

\displaystyle-\sigma\int_{\Sigma}\left(\frac{\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}}{|\widetilde{N}|}-\frac{\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}\cdot\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}}{|\widetilde{N}|^{3}}\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}\right)\cdot(\overline{v}\cdot\overline{\nabla})\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}

(4.87)

\displaystyle-\sigma\int_{\Sigma}\left(\left[\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha-\alpha^{\prime}},\frac{1}{|\widetilde{N}|}\right]\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha^{\prime}}\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}+\left[\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha-\alpha^{\prime}},\frac{1}{|\widetilde{N}|^{3}}\right](\overline{\nabla}\psi\cdot\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha^{\prime}}\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi})-\frac{1}{|\widetilde{N}|^{3}}\left[\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha-\alpha^{\prime}},\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}\right]\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha^{\prime}}\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}\right)\cdot(\overline{v}\cdot\overline{\nabla})\overline{\nabla}\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\widetilde{\psi}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle:I_{211}+I_{212},

where
I_{212}
can be directly controlled if we integrate
\overline{v}\cdot\overline{\nabla}
by parts:

I_{212}\lesssim P(|\widetilde{\psi}|_{4})|\overline{v}|_{W^{1,\infty}}\left|\sqrt{\sigma}\overline{\nabla}\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\widetilde{\psi}\right|_{0}^{2}\leq P(E^{\kappa}(t)).

(4.88)

For
I_{211}
, we integrate
\overline{v}\cdot\overline{\nabla}
by parts and use the symmetric structure to see

\displaystyle I_{211}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle L}}{{=}}-\frac{\sigma}{2}\int_{\Sigma}(\overline{\nabla}\cdot\overline{v})\left(\frac{|\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}|^{2}}{|\widetilde{N}|}-\frac{|\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}\cdot\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}|^{2}}{|\widetilde{N}|^{3}}\right)\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}\lesssim P(|\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}|_{\infty})|\overline{v}|_{W^{1,\infty}}\left|\sqrt{\sigma}\overline{\nabla}\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\widetilde{\psi}\right|_{0}^{2}.

(4.89)

Therefore, plugging (
4.85
)-(
4.89
) into (
4.84
), we get the estimates for
I_{2}
:

\int_{0}^{T}I_{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\lesssim\varepsilon\left|\kappa\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\partial_{t}\psi\right|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{x^{\prime}}^{1}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}P(E^{\kappa}(t)).

(4.90)

It remains to control the term involving
\mathcal{S}_{1}
which reads

\displaystyle I_{3}:=

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma}\mathbf{Q}\mathcal{S}_{1}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}=\int_{\Sigma}\mathbf{Q}\left(\partial_{3}v\cdot\widetilde{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\widetilde{\psi}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\beta_{1}|+|\beta_{2}|=4\\
|\beta_{1}|,|\beta_{2}|>0\end{subarray}}\overline{\partial}{}^{\beta_{1}}v\cdot\overline{\partial}{}^{\beta_{2}}\widetilde{N}\right)\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}

(4.91)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma}\left(\sigma\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\mathcal{H}+\kappa^{2}(1-\overline{\Delta}{})\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\partial_{t}\psi-\partial_{3}q\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\widetilde{\psi}\right)\left(\partial_{3}v\cdot\widetilde{N}\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\widetilde{\psi}+\sum_{|\beta_{1}|=1,|\beta_{2}|=3}\overline{\partial}{}^{\beta_{1}}v\cdot\overline{\partial}{}^{\beta_{2}}\widetilde{N}\right)\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}

\displaystyle+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\beta_{1}|+|\beta_{2}|=4\\
|\beta_{1}|\geq 1,1\leq|\beta_{2}|\leq 2\end{subarray}}\int_{\Sigma}(\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}q-\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\widetilde{\psi}\partial_{3}q)(\overline{\partial}{}^{\beta_{1}}v\cdot\overline{\partial}{}^{\beta_{2}}\widetilde{N})\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle:I_{31}+I_{32},

where we use the definition of
\mathbf{Q}
in
I_{32}
and invoke the Dirichlet boundary condition (
4.49
) for
\mathbf{Q}
in
I_{31}
such that the
L^{2}(\Sigma)
bound of
\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}v
and non-
\sigma
-weighted
\overline{\nabla}\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\psi
with
|\alpha|=4
can be avoided on
\Sigma
.

The term
I_{32}
can be directly controlled as:

I_{32}\lesssim\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\beta_{1}|+|\beta_{2}|=4\\
|\beta_{1}|\geq 1,1\leq|\beta_{2}|\leq 2\end{subarray}}|\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}q|_{-\frac{1}{2}}\left|\overline{\partial}{}^{\beta_{1}}\overline{v}\cdot\overline{\nabla}^{\beta_{2}}\overline{\partial}{}\widetilde{\psi}\right|_{\frac{1}{2}}+\left|\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\widetilde{\psi}\partial_{3}q\right|_{0}\left|\overline{\partial}{}^{\beta_{1}}\overline{v}\cdot\overline{\nabla}^{\beta_{2}}\overline{\partial}{}\widetilde{\psi}\right|_{0}\lesssim\|q\|_{4}\|v\|_{4}|\widetilde{\psi}|_{3.5}+|\partial q|_{L^{\infty}}\|v\|_{3.5}|\widetilde{\psi}|_{3}|\widetilde{\psi}|_{4}.

(4.92)

For
I_{31}
, we invoke
\mathcal{H}=-\overline{\nabla}\cdot(\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}/|\widetilde{N}|)
and then integrate
\overline{\nabla}\cdot
by parts in the mean curvature term and integrate one tangential derivative by parts in the viscosity term to get:

I_{31}\lesssim P(|\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}|_{\infty})|\partial v|_{\infty}\left(\left|\sqrt{\sigma}\overline{\nabla}\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\widetilde{\psi}\right|_{4}^{2}|\partial v|_{\infty}+\left|\kappa\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\partial_{t}\psi\right|_{1}\left|\kappa\overline{\nabla}\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\widetilde{\psi}\right|_{0}\right)+|\partial q|_{L^{\infty}}|\widetilde{\psi}|_{4}^{2}|\overline{\partial}{}v|_{\infty},

(4.93)

and thus yields

\int_{0}^{T}I_{31}\,\mathrm{d}t\lesssim\varepsilon\left|\kappa\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\partial_{t}\psi\right|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{x^{\prime}}^{1}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}P(E^{\kappa}(t))\,\mathrm{d}t,

(4.94)

which together with (
4.92
) gives the bound for
I_{3}
:

\int_{0}^{T}I_{3}\,\mathrm{d}t\leq\varepsilon\left|\kappa\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\partial_{t}\psi\right|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{x^{\prime}}^{1}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}P(E^{\kappa}(t))\,\mathrm{d}t.

(4.95)

Combining (
4.65
), (
4.66
), (
4.83
), (
4.84
), (
4.90
), (
4.91
), (
4.95
), we get the estimates for the boundary integral after choosing
\varepsilon>0
suitably small:

-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Sigma}\mathbf{Q}(\mathbf{V}\cdot\widetilde{N})\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}+\left|\kappa\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\partial_{t}\psi\right|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{x^{\prime}}^{1}([0,T]\times\Sigma)}^{2}+\frac{\sigma}{2}\left|\overline{\nabla}\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\Lambda_{\kappa}\psi(T)\right|_{0}^{2}\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{0}^{\kappa}+\int_{0}^{T}P(E^{\kappa}(t))\,\mathrm{d}t.

(4.96)

Plugging the estimates (
4.57
)-(
4.60
), (
4.64
) and (
4.96
) into (
4.51
) and using
\rho\gtrsim 1,\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}\gtrsim 1
, we get the estimates for the good unknowns:

\|\mathbf{V}(T)\|_{0}^{2}+\left\|\sqrt{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}(q)}\mathbf{Q}(T)\right\|_{0}^{2}+\left|\sqrt{\sigma}\overline{\nabla}\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\Lambda_{\kappa}\psi(T)\right|_{0}^{2}+\left|\kappa\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\partial_{t}\psi\right|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{x^{\prime}}^{1}([0,T]\times\Sigma)}^{2}\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{0}^{\kappa}+\int_{0}^{T}P(E^{\kappa}(t))\,\mathrm{d}t.

(4.97)

Finally, using the definition
\mathbf{V}=\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}v-\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}\widetilde{\varphi}\partial^{\widetilde{\varphi}}_{3}v
, we can replace
\|\mathbf{V}\|_{0}
by
\|\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}v\|_{0}
because their difference, namely
\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}\widetilde{\varphi}\partial^{\widetilde{\varphi}}_{3}v
, is bounded by
\mathcal{P}_{0}^{\kappa}+\int_{0}^{T}P(E^{\kappa}(t))\,\mathrm{d}t
. Indeed, using
\widetilde{\varphi}(t,x)=x_{3}+\chi(x_{3})\widetilde{\psi}(t,x^{\prime})
we only need to investigate the case
\mathcal{T}=\overline{\partial}{}
because the weighted derivative
\mathcal{T}=\omega(x_{3})\partial_{3}
only falls on
\chi(x_{3})
and
x_{3}
instead of
\widetilde{\psi}
. So we have
\|\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\widetilde{\varphi}\|_{0}\lesssim|\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\widetilde{\psi}|_{0}
which is already bounded by the surface tension energy and thus by
\mathcal{P}_{0}^{\kappa}+\int_{0}^{T}P(E^{\kappa}(t))\,\mathrm{d}t
according to (
4.97
). Since
\|\partial^{\widetilde{\varphi}}_{3}v\|_{\infty}\leq\|v\|_{3}\|\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}\|_{\infty}\leq\mathcal{P}_{0}^{\kappa}+\int_{0}^{T}P(E^{\kappa}(t))\,\mathrm{d}t
, we have

\|\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}v(T)\|_{0}^{2}+\left\|\sqrt{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}(q)}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}\check{q}(T)\right\|_{0}^{2}+\left|\sqrt{\sigma}\overline{\nabla}\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\Lambda_{\kappa}\psi(T)\right|_{0}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}\left|\kappa\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\partial_{t}\psi(t)\right|_{1}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{0}^{\kappa}+\int_{0}^{T}P(E^{\kappa}(t))\,\mathrm{d}t.

(4.98)

We remark here that we can employ the same analysis to prove the tangential estimates with mixed spatial-time derivatives.

Proposition 4.5
.

Let
\alpha
be the multi-index satisfying
1\leq\alpha_{0}\leq 3
and
|\alpha|=4
, we have:

\|\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}v(T)\|_{0}^{2}+\left\|\sqrt{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}(q)}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}\check{q}(T)\right\|_{0}^{2}+\left|\sqrt{\sigma}\overline{\nabla}\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}\Lambda_{\kappa}\psi(T)\right|_{0}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}\left|\kappa\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}\partial_{t}\psi(t)\right|_{1}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{0}^{\kappa}+\int_{0}^{T}P(E^{\kappa}(t))\,\mathrm{d}t.

(4.99)

4.6
Tangential energy estimate with time derivatives

In this subsection, we study the time-differentiated equations, i.e., the equations obtained by commuting
\partial_{t}^{4}
with (
3.11
). We aim to prove:

Proposition 4.6
.

We have

\|\partial_{t}^{4}v(T)\|_{0}^{2}+\left\|\sqrt{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}(q)}\partial_{t}^{4}\check{q}(T)\right\|_{0}^{2}+\left|\sqrt{\sigma}\overline{\nabla}\partial_{t}^{4}\Lambda_{\kappa}\psi(T)\right|_{0}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}\left|\kappa\partial_{t}^{5}\psi(t)\right|_{1}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{0}^{\kappa}+\int_{0}^{T}P(E^{\kappa}(t))\,\mathrm{d}t.

(4.100)

Although the proof appears to be similar to what has been done in the previous subsection, it should be mentioned that we only have
L^{2}(\Omega)
-regularity for the full-time derivatives of
v
and
q
, and thus we do not have any information about their boundary regularity. When the full-time derivatives of
v
and
q
appear on the boundary, we use either the artificial viscosity or Euler equations to reduce a time derivative to a spatial derivative.

4.6.1
Alinhac good unknowns for full-time derivatives

To begin with, we still introduce the Alinhac good unknowns of
v,q
with respect to
\partial_{t}^{4}
. Using the same notation as before, we define

\displaystyle\mathbf{V}_{i}:=\partial_{t}^{4}v_{i}-\partial^{\widetilde{\varphi}}_{3}v_{i}\partial_{t}^{4}\widetilde{\varphi},\quad\mathbf{Q}:=\partial_{t}^{4}\check{q}-\partial^{\widetilde{\varphi}}_{3}\check{q}\partial_{t}^{4}\widetilde{\varphi}.

(4.101)

Parallel to (
4.36
) , we have

\partial_{t}^{4}(\nabla^{\widetilde{\varphi}}_{i}f)=\nabla^{\widetilde{\varphi}}_{i}\mathbf{F}+\mathfrak{C}_{i}(f),

(4.102)

where
\mathfrak{C}_{i}(f):=\partial^{\widetilde{\varphi}}_{3}\partial^{\widetilde{\varphi}}_{i}f\partial_{t}^{4}\widetilde{\varphi}+\mathfrak{C}^{\prime}_{i}(f)
and

\displaystyle\mathfrak{C}_{i}^{\prime}(f)=

\displaystyle-\left[\partial_{t}^{4},\frac{\partial_{i}\widetilde{\varphi}}{\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}},\partial_{3}f\right]-\partial_{3}f\left[\partial_{t}^{4},\partial_{i}\widetilde{\varphi},\frac{1}{\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}}\right]+\partial_{i}\widetilde{\varphi}\partial_{3}f\left[\partial_{t}^{3},\frac{1}{(\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi})^{2}}\right]\partial_{t}\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi},~~i=1,2

(4.103)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{C}_{3}^{\prime}(f)=

\displaystyle\left[\partial_{t}^{4},\frac{1}{\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}},\partial_{3}f\right]+\partial_{3}f\left[\partial_{t}^{3},\frac{1}{(\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi})^{2}}\right]\partial_{t}\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}.

(4.104)

Then we take
\partial_{t}^{4}
to the first two equations of (
1.5
) to obtain

\displaystyle\rho D_{t}^{\widetilde{\varphi}}\mathbf{V}_{i}+\nabla^{\widetilde{\varphi}}_{i}\mathbf{Q}=

\displaystyle~\mathcal{R}^{1}_{i},

(4.105)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{\prime}(q)D_{t}^{\widetilde{\varphi}}\mathbf{Q}+\nabla^{\widetilde{\varphi}}\cdot\mathbf{V}=

\displaystyle~\mathcal{R}^{2}-\mathfrak{C}^{i}(v_{i}),

(4.106)

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}^{1}_{i}:=

\displaystyle-[\partial_{t}^{4},\rho]D_{t}^{\widetilde{\varphi}}v_{i}-\rho\left(\mathfrak{D}(v_{i})+\mathfrak{E}(v_{i})\right)-\mathfrak{C}_{i}(\check{q}),

(4.107)

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}^{2}:=

\displaystyle-[\partial_{t}^{4},\mathcal{F}^{\prime}(q)]D_{t}^{\widetilde{\varphi}}\check{q}-\mathcal{F}^{\prime}(q)\left(\mathfrak{D}(\check{q})+\mathfrak{E}(\check{q})\right)+\partial_{t}^{4}(\mathcal{F}^{\prime}(q)gv_{3}),

(4.108)

and the commutators
\mathfrak{D}(f),\mathfrak{e}(f)
are defined in the same way as in (
4.39
) and (
4.40
) by replacing
\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}
with
\partial_{t}^{4}
and replacing
\overline{\partial}{}
with
\partial_{t}
. The last two terms in (
4.39
) vanish because
\partial_{t}^{4}
directly commutes with
\partial_{3}
. Analogous to Lemma
4.4
, we list the estimates for commutators
\mathfrak{C},\mathfrak{D},\mathfrak{e}
.

Lemma 4.7
.

Let
\mathbf{F}:=\partial_{t}^{4}f-\partial^{\widetilde{\varphi}}_{3}f\partial_{t}^{4}\widetilde{\varphi}
be the Alinhac good unknowns of
f
with respect to
\partial_{t}^{4}
. Assuming that
\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}\geq c_{0}>0
, then

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}^{4}f\|_{0}\leq

\displaystyle~\|\mathbf{F}\|_{0}+c_{0}^{-1}\|\partial_{3}f\|_{\infty}|\partial_{t}^{4}\widetilde{\psi}|_{0},

(4.109)

\displaystyle\|\mathfrak{C}_{i}(f)\|_{0}\leq

\displaystyle~P\left(c_{0}^{-1},|\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}|_{\infty},\sum_{k=1}^{3}|\overline{\nabla}\partial_{t}^{k}\widetilde{\psi}|_{3-k}\right)\cdot\left(\|\partial f\|_{\infty}+\sum_{k=1}^{3}\|\partial_{t}^{k}f\|_{4-k}\right),\quad i=1,2,3,

(4.110)

\displaystyle\|\mathfrak{D}(f)\|_{0}\leq

\displaystyle~P\left(c_{0}^{-1},|\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}|_{\infty},\sum_{k=1}^{3}|\overline{\nabla}\partial_{t}^{k}\widetilde{\psi}|_{3-k}\right)\cdot\left(\|\partial f\|_{\infty}+\sum_{k=1}^{3}\|\partial_{t}^{k}f\|_{4-k}\right),

(4.111)

\displaystyle\|\mathfrak{E}(f)\|_{0}\leq

\displaystyle~\kappa|\overline{\nabla}\partial_{t}^{5}\psi|_{0}\|\partial f\|_{\infty}.

(4.112)

The
\partial_{t}^{4}
-differentiated kinematic boundary condition now reads

\partial_{t}^{5}\psi+(\overline{v}\cdot\overline{\nabla})\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\widetilde{\psi}-\mathbf{V}\cdot\widetilde{N}=\mathcal{S}_{1}^{*},\quad\text{on}\,\,\Sigma,

(4.113)

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{1}^{*}:=\partial_{3}v\cdot\widetilde{N}\partial_{t}^{4}\widetilde{\psi}+\sum_{1\leq\beta\leq 3}\binom{4}{\beta}\partial_{t}^{\beta}v\cdot\partial_{t}^{4-\beta}\widetilde{N}.

(4.114)

Also, since
\mathbf{Q}|_{\Sigma}=\partial_{t}^{4}q-\partial^{\widetilde{\varphi}}_{3}q\partial_{t}^{4}\widetilde{\psi}
, the boundary condition of
\mathbf{Q}
on
\Sigma
reads

\displaystyle\mathbf{Q}=-\sigma\partial_{t}^{4}\overline{\nabla}\cdot\left(\frac{\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}}{\sqrt{1+|\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}|^{2}}}\right)+\kappa^{2}(1-\overline{\Delta}{})\partial_{t}^{5}\psi-\partial_{3}q\partial_{t}^{4}\widetilde{\psi}.

(4.115)

4.6.2
Energy estimates for the full-time derivatives

Replacing
\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}
by
\partial_{t}^{4}
in (
4.51
), we have

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega}\rho|\mathbf{V}|^{2}\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}\,\mathrm{d}x=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega}\rho|\mathbf{V}|^{2}\partial_{3}\partial_{t}(\widetilde{\varphi}-\varphi)\,\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Omega}\mathbf{Q}(\nabla^{\widetilde{\varphi}}\cdot\mathbf{V})\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}\,\mathrm{d}x-\int_{\Sigma}\mathbf{Q}(\mathbf{V}\cdot\widetilde{N})\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}+\int_{\Omega}\mathbf{V}\cdot\mathcal{R}^{1}\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}\,\mathrm{d}x.,

(4.116)

where the first term and the last term are controlled in the same way as (
4.57
)-(
4.59
), so we omit the details. As for the second term, we follow (
4.60
)-(
4.64
) to get

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\mathbf{Q}(\nabla^{\widetilde{\varphi}}\cdot\mathbf{V})\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}\,\mathrm{d}x

(4.117)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\underbrace{-\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}^{4}\check{q}\mathfrak{C}_{i}(v^{i})\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}\,\mathrm{d}x}_{=:I_{0}^{*}}+\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}^{4}\widetilde{\varphi}\partial^{\widetilde{\varphi}}_{3}\check{q}\mathfrak{C}_{i}(v^{i})\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}\,\mathrm{d}x-\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left\|\sqrt{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}(q)}\mathbf{Q}\right\|_{0}^{2}

\displaystyle+\left\|\sqrt{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}(q)}\mathbf{Q}\right\|_{0}^{2}(\|\partial v\|_{\infty}+\kappa|\overline{\nabla}\partial_{t}\psi|_{0.5})+\left\|\sqrt{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}(q)}\mathbf{Q}\right\|_{0}\|\mathcal{R}^{2}\|_{0}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle I_{0}^{*}-\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left\|\sqrt{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}(q)}\mathbf{Q}\right\|_{0}^{2}+\left\|\sqrt{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}(q)}\partial_{t}^{4}\check{q}\right\|_{0}^{2}\left(\|\partial v\|_{\infty}+\kappa|\overline{\nabla}\partial_{t}\psi|_{0.5}+|\kappa\overline{\nabla}\partial_{t}^{5}\psi|_{0}\right)

\displaystyle+P\left(c_{0}^{-1},|\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}|_{\infty},\sum_{k=1}^{3}|\overline{\nabla}\partial_{t}^{k}\widetilde{\psi}|_{3-k}\right)|\partial_{t}^{4}\widetilde{\psi}|_{0}|\left\|\sqrt{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}(q)}\partial_{t}^{4}\check{q}\right\|_{0}\left(\|\partial v,\partial q\|_{\infty}+\sum_{k=1}^{3}\|\partial_{t}^{k}\check{q},\partial_{t}^{k}v\|_{4-k}+\|\mathcal{F}^{\prime}(q)\partial_{t}^{4}v_{3}\|_{0}\right).

At this point, we are not able to control
I_{0}^{*}:=-\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}^{4}q\mathfrak{C}_{i}(v^{i})\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}\,\mathrm{d}x
as in (
4.63
) since this requires the bound for
\|\partial_{t}^{4}q\|_{0}
. We can only obtain the control of
\|\sqrt{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}(q)}\partial_{t}^{4}q\|_{0}
from the energy estimate because we can no longer use the momentum equation to reduce
\partial_{t}^{4}q
due to lack of spatial derivatives.
Although the method in (
4.63
) is still valid here when we prove the well-posedness provided that
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}(q)
is bounded from below, we would like to show that our estimate can be adjusted to be uniform in
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}(q)
.
To achieve this, we find that the problematic terms in
\mathfrak{C}_{i}(v^{i})
can be exactly canceled by the boundary error term
\mathcal{S}_{1}
defined in (
4.114
). Therefore, this term should be controlled together with the boundary integral if we want our energy estimates to be uniform in the Mach number.

Next, we analyze the boundary integral. Most of the steps are parallel to Section
4.5.4
if we replace
\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}
by
\partial_{t}^{4}
, so we will omit the details of those repeated steps but only list the different steps. Plugging the boundary conditions (
4.113
) and (
4.115
) into
-\int_{\Sigma}\mathbf{Q}(\mathbf{V}\cdot\widetilde{N})\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}
, we get

\displaystyle-\int_{\Sigma}\mathbf{Q}(\mathbf{V}\cdot\widetilde{N})\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}=-\int_{\Sigma}\mathbf{Q}\partial_{t}^{5}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}-\int_{\Sigma}\mathbf{Q}(\overline{v}\cdot\overline{\nabla})\partial_{t}^{4}\widetilde{\psi}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}+\int_{\Sigma}\mathbf{Q}\mathcal{S}_{1}^{*}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}=:I_{1}^{*}+I_{2}^{*}+I_{3}^{*},

(4.118)

and
I_{1}^{*}
is further divided into three parts:

\displaystyle I_{1}^{*}:=-\int_{\Sigma}\mathbf{Q}\partial_{t}^{5}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}=

\displaystyle\sigma\int_{\Sigma}\partial_{t}^{4}\overline{\nabla}\cdot\left(\frac{\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}}{\sqrt{1+|\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}|^{2}}}\right)\partial_{t}^{5}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}-\kappa^{2}\int_{\Sigma}\partial_{t}^{4}(1-\overline{\Delta}{})\partial_{t}\psi\cdot\partial_{t}^{5}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}+\int_{\Sigma}\partial_{3}q\partial_{t}^{4}\widetilde{\psi}\partial_{t}^{5}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}

(4.119)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle:\text{ ST}_{1}^{*}+\text{ ST}_{2}^{*}+\text{ RT}^{*}.

Mimicing the steps (
4.67
)-(
4.81
), we can get the bounds for
\text{ ST}_{1}^{*},\text{ ST}_{2}^{*}
:

\int_{0}^{T}\text{ ST}_{1}^{*}+\text{ ST}^{*}_{2}\,\mathrm{d}t+\left|\kappa\partial_{t}^{5}\psi\right|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{x^{\prime}}^{1}}^{2}+\frac{\sigma}{2}\left|\overline{\nabla}\partial_{t}^{4}\Lambda_{\kappa}\psi(T)\right|_{0}^{2}\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{0}^{\kappa}+\int_{0}^{T}P(E^{\kappa}(t))\,\mathrm{d}t.

(4.120)

Remark 4.4
.

Parallel to the remark after (
4.82
),
-\int_{0}^{T}\text{ RT}^{*}\,\mathrm{d}t
would contribute to the non-
\sigma
-weighted energy
\int_{\Sigma}(-\partial_{3}q)|\partial_{t}^{4}\Lambda_{\kappa}\psi|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t
if the Rayleigh-Taylor sign condition holds. This will be revisited in Section
7
.

As for
\text{ RT}^{*}
, if we still follow (
4.82
) to get:

\int_{0}^{T}\text{ RT}^{*}\,\mathrm{d}t\lesssim\varepsilon\left|\kappa\partial_{t}^{5}\psi\right|_{L_{t}^{2}L_{x^{\prime}}^{2}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}P\left(\|\check{q}\|_{4},\left|\partial_{t}^{4}\Lambda_{\kappa}\psi\right|_{0},\left|\partial_{t}^{5}\Lambda_{\kappa}\psi\right|_{0}\right)\,\mathrm{d}t,

then we find that the term
|\partial_{t}^{5}\Lambda_{\kappa}\psi|_{0}
is not included in
E^{\kappa}(t)
because there is no spatial derivative here. To overcome this, we invoke the kinematic boundary condition
\partial_{t}\psi=-\overline{v}\cdot\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}+v_{3}
and take
\partial_{t}^{4}
to get

\partial_{t}^{5}\psi=-(\overline{v}\cdot\overline{\nabla})\partial_{t}^{4}\widetilde{\psi}+\partial_{t}^{4}v_{3}-[\partial_{t}^{4},\overline{v}\cdot]\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}=-(\overline{v}\cdot\overline{\nabla})\partial_{t}^{4}\widetilde{\psi}+\partial_{t}^{4}v\cdot\widetilde{N}-[\partial_{t}^{4},\overline{v}\cdot,\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}],

(4.121)

and thus

\displaystyle\text{ RT}^{*}=

\displaystyle-\int_{\Sigma}\partial_{3}q\partial_{t}^{4}\widetilde{\psi}(\overline{v}\cdot\overline{\nabla})\partial_{t}^{4}\widetilde{\psi}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}+\int_{\Sigma}\partial_{3}q\partial_{t}^{4}\widetilde{\psi}\partial_{t}^{4}v\cdot\widetilde{N}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}-\int_{\Sigma}\partial_{3}q\partial_{t}^{4}\widetilde{\psi}[\partial_{t}^{4},\overline{v}\cdot,\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}]\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}

(4.122)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle:\text{ RT}_{1}^{*}+\text{ RT}_{2}^{*}+\text{ RT}_{3}^{*}.

Note that we only need to analyze the contribution of
\text{ RT}_{2}^{*}
because the contribution of the other two terms will be canceled by part of
I_{2}^{*}
and
I_{3}^{*}
. To do this, we need to derive the equation for
\partial_{t}^{4}\cdot\widetilde{N}
on
\Sigma
. Recall that

D_{t}^{\widetilde{\varphi}}\big|_{\Sigma}=\partial_{t}+(\overline{v}\cdot\overline{\nabla})+(\partial_{3}\widetilde{\psi})^{-1}\underbrace{(v\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{N}}-\partial_{t}\varphi)}_{=0\text{ on }\Sigma}\partial_{3}=\partial_{t}+(\overline{v}\cdot\overline{\nabla}),

we have the following identity by projecting the momentum equation onto the direction of
\widetilde{N}
on
\Sigma
:

\rho\partial_{t}v\cdot\widetilde{N}=-(\rho-1)g-\rho(\overline{v}\cdot\overline{\nabla})v\cdot\widetilde{N}+\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}\cdot\overline{\nabla}\check{q}-(1+|\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}|^{2})\partial_{3}\check{q},

and thus

\rho\partial_{t}^{4}v\cdot\widetilde{N}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle L}}{{=}}-\partial_{t}^{3}\rho g-\rho(\overline{v}\cdot\overline{\nabla})\partial_{t}^{3}v\cdot\widetilde{N}+\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}\cdot\overline{\nabla}\partial_{t}^{3}\check{q}-|\widetilde{N}|^{2}\partial_{3}\partial_{t}^{3}\check{q}.

(4.123)

The contribution of the first three terms in (
4.123
) can be directly controlled after integrating
\overline{\nabla}
by parts and using the Sobolev trace lemma:

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma}\rho^{-1}\partial_{3}q\partial_{t}^{4}\widetilde{\psi}\left(\rho(\overline{v}\cdot\overline{\nabla})\partial_{t}^{3}v\cdot\widetilde{N}+\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}\cdot\overline{\nabla}\partial_{t}^{3}\check{q}-\partial_{t}^{3}\rho g\right)\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}

(4.124)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle L}}{{=}}

\displaystyle-\int_{\Sigma}\rho^{-1}\overline{\nabla}\partial_{t}^{4}\widetilde{\psi}\cdot\left(\partial_{3}q\left(\rho\overline{v}\partial_{t}^{3}v\cdot\widetilde{N}+\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}\partial_{t}^{3}\check{q}\right)\right)\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}-\int_{\Sigma}\rho^{-1}\partial_{3}q\partial_{t}^{4}\widetilde{\psi}\partial_{t}^{3}\rho g\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\partial q\|_{2}\left(\left|\overline{\nabla}\partial_{t}^{4}\widetilde{\psi}\right|_{0}P\left(\|\partial_{t}^{3}v\|_{1},\|\partial_{t}^{3}\check{q}\|_{1},|\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}|_{\infty}\right)+|\partial_{t}^{4}\widetilde{\psi}|_{0}\|\mathcal{F}^{\prime}(q)\partial_{t}^{3}q\|_{1}|\rho|_{\infty}\right).

Remark 4.5
.

Note that the right side of (
4.124
) involves
|\overline{\nabla}\partial_{t}^{4}\widetilde{\psi}|_{0}
whose control relies on
\sigma^{-1}
. This is due to the lack of the Rayleigh-Taylor sign condition. When taking the zero surface tension limit, the Rayleigh-Taylor sign condition is assumed and thus the RT term can be directly controlled.

Then for the last term, we need to do the same reduction for
\partial_{t}^{4}\psi
:

\partial_{t}^{4}\psi=-(\overline{v}\cdot\overline{\nabla})\partial_{t}^{3}\widetilde{\psi}+\partial_{t}^{3}v_{3}-[\partial_{t}^{3},\overline{v}\cdot]\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}=-(\overline{v}\cdot\overline{\nabla})\partial_{t}^{3}\widetilde{\psi}+\partial_{t}^{3}v\cdot\widetilde{N}-[\partial_{t}^{3},\overline{v}\cdot,\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}].

(4.125)

Using (
4.125
) and Sobolev trace lemma, it is controlled by

|\partial_{t}^{4}\widetilde{\psi}|_{0}\lesssim P(|\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}|_{\infty},|\overline{\nabla}\partial_{t}\widetilde{\psi}|_{\infty})\left(|\overline{\nabla}\partial_{t}^{3}\widetilde{\psi}|_{0}+\|\partial_{t}^{3}v\|_{1}+\|\partial_{t}^{2}v\|_{2}+|\overline{\nabla}\partial_{t}^{2}\widetilde{\psi}|_{0}\right).

(4.126)

Now we plug the equality above into the boundary integral
-\int_{\Sigma}\rho^{-1}\partial_{3}q|\widetilde{N}|^{2}\partial_{t}^{4}\widetilde{\psi}\partial_{3}\partial_{t}^{3}\check{q}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}
. Note that the unit exterior normal vector to
\Sigma
is
(0,0,1)^{\top}
(not the Eulerian normal vector
\widetilde{N}
!), we can use the divergence theorem to rewrite the boundary integral into the interior, and integrate by parts in
\partial_{t}
to get the following estimate:

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Sigma}\rho^{-1}\partial_{3}q|\widetilde{N}|^{2}\partial_{t}^{4}\widetilde{\psi}\partial_{3}\partial_{t}^{3}\check{q}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}\,\mathrm{d}t\stackrel{{\scriptstyle L}}{{=}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Sigma}\rho^{-1}\partial_{3}q|\widetilde{N}|^{2}\Lambda_{\kappa}^{2}\left((\overline{v}\cdot\overline{\nabla})\partial_{t}^{3}\widetilde{\psi}-\partial_{t}^{3}v\cdot\widetilde{N}\right)\partial_{3}\partial_{t}^{3}\check{q}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}\,\mathrm{d}t

(4.127)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\partial_{3}\left(\rho^{-1}\partial_{3}q|\widetilde{\mathbf{N}}|^{2}\Lambda_{\kappa}^{2}\left((\overline{v}\cdot\overline{\nabla})\partial_{t}^{3}\widetilde{\varphi}-\partial_{t}^{3}v\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{N}}\right)\partial_{3}\partial_{t}^{3}\check{q}\right)\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle L}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\rho^{-1}\partial_{3}q|\widetilde{\mathbf{N}}|^{2}\Lambda_{\kappa}^{2}\left((\overline{v}\cdot\overline{\nabla})\partial_{t}^{3}\widetilde{\varphi}-\partial_{t}^{3}v\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{N}}\right)\cdot\partial_{3}^{2}\partial_{t}^{3}\check{q}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\overset{\partial_{t}}{=}

\displaystyle-\int_{\Omega}\rho^{-1}\partial_{3}q|\widetilde{\mathbf{N}}|^{2}\Lambda_{\kappa}^{2}\left((\overline{v}\cdot\overline{\nabla})\partial_{t}^{3}\widetilde{\varphi}-\partial_{t}^{3}v\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{N}}\right)\cdot\partial_{3}^{2}\partial_{t}^{2}\check{q}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\rho^{-1}\partial_{3}q|\widetilde{\mathbf{N}}|^{2}\partial_{t}\Lambda_{\kappa}^{2}\left((\overline{v}\cdot\overline{\nabla})\partial_{t}^{3}\widetilde{\varphi}-\partial_{t}^{3}v\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{N}}\right)\cdot\partial_{3}^{2}\partial_{t}^{2}\check{q}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\varepsilon\|\partial_{t}^{2}\partial^{2}\check{q}\|_{0}^{2}+\mathcal{P}_{0}^{\kappa}+\int_{0}^{T}P\left(\|\partial_{t}^{4}v\|_{0},\|\partial_{t}^{3}v\|_{1},\|\partial_{t}v\|_{\infty},\|\partial_{t}^{2}\check{q}\|_{2},|\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}|_{\infty},|\overline{\nabla}\partial_{t}^{3}\widetilde{\psi}|_{0},|\overline{\nabla}\partial_{t}^{4}\widetilde{\psi}|_{0}\right)\,\mathrm{d}t.

Combining this with (
4.120
), (
4.122
), (
4.124
) and (
4.127
), we get the estimate for
I_{1}^{*}
:

\int_{0}^{T}I_{1}^{*}\,\mathrm{d}t+\left|\kappa\partial_{t}^{5}\psi\right|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{x^{\prime}}^{1}}^{2}+\frac{\sigma}{2}\left|\overline{\nabla}\partial_{t}^{4}\Lambda_{\kappa}\psi(T)\right|_{0}^{2}\lesssim\varepsilon\|\partial_{t}^{2}\partial^{2}\check{q}\|_{0}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}\text{ RT}_{1}^{*}+\text{ RT}_{3}^{*}\,\mathrm{d}t+\mathcal{P}_{0}^{\kappa}+\int_{0}^{T}P(E^{\kappa}(t))\,\mathrm{d}t,

(4.128)

after choosing
\varepsilon>0
that appears above to be suitably small.

Next we expand
I_{2}^{*},I_{3}^{*}
defined in (
4.118
)

\displaystyle I_{2}^{*}+I_{3}^{*}=

\displaystyle-\int_{\Sigma}\partial_{t}^{4}\check{q}(\overline{v}\cdot\overline{\nabla})\partial_{t}^{4}\widetilde{\psi}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}+\int_{\Sigma}\partial_{t}^{4}\widetilde{\psi}\partial_{3}q(\overline{v}\cdot\overline{\nabla})\partial_{t}^{4}\widetilde{\psi}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}

(4.129)

\displaystyle+\int_{\Sigma}\partial_{t}^{4}\check{q}\mathcal{S}_{1}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}-\int_{\Sigma}\partial_{t}^{4}\widetilde{\psi}\partial_{3}q\partial_{3}v\cdot\widetilde{N}\partial_{t}^{4}\widetilde{\psi}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}-\int_{\Sigma}\partial_{t}^{4}\widetilde{\psi}\partial_{3}q\left(\sum_{1\leq\beta\leq 3}\binom{4}{\beta}\partial_{t}^{\beta}v\cdot\partial_{t}^{4-\beta}\widetilde{N}\right)\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}

and we find that the second term exactly cancels
\text{ RT}_{1}^{*}
and the fifth term exactly cancels
\text{ RT}_{3}^{*}
defined in (
4.122
). The first term can be controlled in the same way as
I_{21},I_{22}
defined in (
4.84
) after replacing
\overline{\partial}{}^{4}
by
\partial_{t}^{4}
. The fourth term is directly controlled by
P(E^{\kappa}(t))
by using the Sobolev trace lemma.

Hence, it suffices to analyze the third term. Using the definition of
\mathcal{S}_{1}^{*}
, we have

\int_{\Sigma}\partial_{t}^{4}\check{q}\mathcal{S}_{1}^{*}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}=\int_{\Sigma}\partial_{t}^{4}\check{q}\left(\partial_{3}v\cdot\widetilde{N}\partial_{t}^{4}\widetilde{\psi}\right)\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}-4\int_{\Sigma}\partial_{t}^{4}\check{q}\partial_{t}^{3}v\cdot\partial_{t}\widetilde{N}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}+\sum_{1\leq\beta\leq 2}\binom{4}{\beta}\int_{\Sigma}\partial_{t}^{4}\check{q}\partial_{t}^{\beta}v\cdot\overline{\nabla}\partial_{t}^{4-\beta}\widetilde{\psi}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime},

(4.130)

where the first term can be controlled by the surface tension energy after invoking (
4.115
) and integrating
\overline{\nabla}
by parts; and the last term can be controlled after integrating by part in
\partial_{t}
under time integral. But for the remaining term

I_{30}^{*}:=4\int_{\Sigma}\partial_{t}^{4}\check{q}\partial_{t}^{3}v\cdot\partial_{t}\widetilde{N}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime},

(4.131)

we have neither the
L^{2}(\Sigma)
-regularity of
\partial_{t}^{4}q
nor the possibility of integrating
\frac{1}{2}
-time derivatives by parts as in the control of (
4.91
).

Fortunately, we can still control
I_{30}

together with the interior term

I_{0}^{*}:=-\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}^{4}q\mathfrak{C}_{i}(v^{i})\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}\,\mathrm{d}x
defined in (
4.117
). In fact, invoking (
4.103
) and (
4.104
), we know
\mathfrak{C}_{i}(v^{i})
includes the following terms involving
\geq 3
time derivatives of
v^{i}
and
\geq 4
derivatives of
\widetilde{\varphi}
:

\displaystyle\partial^{\widetilde{\varphi}}_{3}\partial^{\widetilde{\varphi}}_{i}v^{i}\partial_{t}^{4}\widetilde{\varphi}

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{C}_{i}(v^{i})-\mathfrak{C}^{\prime}_{i}(v^{i}),~~i=1,2,3,

(4.132)

\displaystyle-4\partial_{t}\left(\frac{\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\widetilde{\varphi}}{\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}}\right)\partial_{t}^{3}\partial_{3}v^{i}

\displaystyle=4\partial_{t}\widetilde{\mathbf{N}}_{i}\partial^{\widetilde{\varphi}}_{3}\partial_{t}^{3}v^{i}+4\frac{\partial_{3}\partial_{t}\widetilde{\varphi}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\widetilde{\varphi}}{\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}}\partial^{\widetilde{\varphi}}_{3}\partial_{t}^{3}v^{i}\text{ from the first commutator in }\mathfrak{C}_{i}^{\prime}(v^{i})~~i=1,2,

(4.133)

\displaystyle 4\partial_{t}\left(\frac{1}{\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}}\right)\partial_{t}^{3}\partial_{3}v^{3}

\displaystyle=-4\frac{\partial_{3}\partial_{t}\widetilde{\varphi}}{\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}}\partial^{\widetilde{\varphi}}_{3}\partial_{t}^{3}v^{3}\text{ from the first commutator in }\mathfrak{C}_{3}^{\prime}(v^{3}),

(4.134)

while the terms in
\mathfrak{C}_{i}^{\prime}(v^{i})
containing only
\leq 2
time derivatives of
v^{i}
and
\leq 3
time derivatives of
\widetilde{\varphi}
are controlled directly after integrating
\partial_{t}
by parts under time integral.

The contribution of the above four terms in
I_{0}^{*}
is divided into three parts:

\displaystyle I_{00}^{*}

\displaystyle:=-4\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}^{4}\check{q}\partial_{t}\widetilde{\mathbf{N}}_{i}\partial_{3}\partial_{t}^{3}v^{i}\,\mathrm{d}x

(4.135)

\displaystyle I_{01}^{*}

\displaystyle:=-\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}^{4}\check{q}\partial_{3}(\nabla^{\widetilde{\varphi}}\cdot v)\partial_{t}^{4}\widetilde{\varphi}\,\mathrm{d}x

(4.136)

\displaystyle I_{02}^{*}

\displaystyle:=-4\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}^{4}\check{q}\left(\frac{\partial_{3}\partial_{t}\widetilde{\varphi}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\widetilde{\varphi}}{\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}}\right)\partial^{\widetilde{\varphi}}_{3}\partial_{t}^{3}v^{i}\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}\,\mathrm{d}x+4\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}^{4}\check{q}\left(\frac{\partial_{3}\partial_{t}\widetilde{\varphi}}{\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}}\right)\partial^{\widetilde{\varphi}}_{3}\partial_{t}^{3}v^{3}\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}\,\mathrm{d}x.

(4.137)

Integrating
\partial_{3}
by parts in
I_{00}^{*}
and using
N_{3}=1
, we find the boundary term exactly cancels with
I_{30}^{*}
, so we have:

\displaystyle I_{30}^{*}+I_{00}^{*}=

\displaystyle 4\int_{\Omega}\left(\partial_{t}^{4}\partial_{3}\check{q}\partial_{t}\widetilde{\mathbf{N}}+\partial_{t}^{4}\check{q}\partial_{t}\partial_{3}\widetilde{\mathbf{N}}\right)\cdot\partial_{t}^{3}v\,\mathrm{d}x
