(4.138)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{\Omega}\left(\partial_{t}^{3}\partial_{3}\check{q}\partial_{t}\widetilde{\mathbf{N}}+\partial_{t}^{3}\check{q}\partial_{t}\partial_{3}\widetilde{\mathbf{N}}\right)\cdot\partial_{t}^{3}v\,\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}^{3}\partial_{3}\check{q}\partial_{t}(\partial_{t}\widetilde{\mathbf{N}}\cdot\partial_{t}^{3}v)+\partial_{t}^{3}\check{q}\partial_{t}(\partial_{t}\partial_{3}\widetilde{\mathbf{N}}\cdot\partial_{t}^{3}v)\,\mathrm{d}x.

Under the time integral, we have the following bounds after using
\varepsilon
-Young’s inequality:

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}I_{00}^{*}+I_{30}^{*}\,\mathrm{d}t\lesssim\varepsilon\|\partial_{t}^{3}\partial\check{q}\|_{0}^{2}+\mathcal{P}_{0}^{\kappa}+\int_{0}^{T}(\|\partial_{t}^{3}\check{q}(t)\|_{0}+1)P(E^{\kappa}(t))\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\|\partial_{t}^{3}\partial\check{q}\|_{0}^{2}+\varepsilon\int_{0}^{T}\|\partial_{t}^{3}\check{q}(t)\|_{0}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t+\mathcal{P}_{0}^{\kappa}+\int_{0}^{T}P(E^{\kappa}(t))\,\mathrm{d}t.

(4.139)

Here, we still need to study
\int_{0}^{T}\|\partial_{t}^{3}\check{q}(t)\|_{0}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t
, as the reduction scheme does not apply to
\partial_{t}^{3}\check{q}(t)
due to lack of spatial derivatives. We control this term through the fundamental theorem of calculus: For each
x_{3}\in(-b,0)
, we write

\partial_{t}^{3}\check{q}(t,x^{\prime},x_{3})=\partial_{t}^{3}\check{q}(t,x^{\prime},0)+\int_{0}^{x_{3}}\partial_{3}\partial_{t}^{3}\check{q}(t,x^{\prime},z)\,dz,

and so

\left(\partial_{t}^{3}\check{q}(t,x^{\prime},x_{3})\right)^{2}\lesssim_{b}\left(\partial_{t}^{3}\check{q}(t,x^{\prime},0)\right)^{2}+\int_{0}^{x_{3}}\left(\partial_{3}\partial_{t}^{3}\check{q}(t,x^{\prime},z)\right)^{2}\,dz.

Integrating both sides with respect to the spatial variables, we obtain

\|\partial_{t}^{3}\check{q}(t)\|_{0}^{2}\lesssim_{b}|\partial_{t}^{3}\check{q}(t)|_{0}^{2}+\|\partial_{3}\partial_{t}^{3}\check{q}(t)\|_{0}^{2}.

The second term on the RHS is bounded by
E^{\kappa}(t)
. For the first term, note that

\partial_{t}^{3}\check{q}=-\sigma\partial_{t}^{3}\left(\frac{\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}}{\sqrt{1+|\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}|^{2}}}+g\widetilde{\psi}\right)+\kappa^{2}(1-\overline{\Delta}{})\partial_{t}^{4}\psi,\quad\text{on}\,\,\,\Sigma,

where

-\sigma\partial_{t}^{3}\left(\frac{\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}}{\sqrt{1+|\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}|^{2}}}\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle L}}{{=}}-\sigma\overline{\nabla}\cdot\left(\frac{\overline{\nabla}\partial_{t}^{3}\widetilde{\psi}}{\sqrt{(1+|\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}|^{2}})}-\frac{\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}\cdot\overline{\nabla}\partial_{t}^{3}\widetilde{\psi}}{{\sqrt{(1+|\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}|^{2}})}^{3}}\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}\right),

which indicates that
\partial_{t}^{3}\check{q}|_{\Sigma}
consists of non-
\kappa
-weighted terms with at most 5 derivatives on
\widetilde{\psi}
with at most 3 times derivatives, and a
\kappa
-weighted term
\kappa^{2}(1-\overline{\Delta}{})\partial_{t}^{4}\psi
. Therefore,

\int_{0}^{T}\|\partial_{t}^{3}\check{q}(t)\|_{0}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\lesssim\int_{0}^{T}P\left(\sum_{k=0}^{3}|\sqrt{\sigma}\overline{\nabla}\partial_{t}^{k}\Lambda_{\kappa}\psi(t)|_{4-k},\sum_{k=0}^{3}|\partial_{t}^{k}\Lambda_{\kappa}\psi(t)|_{4-k}\right)\,\mathrm{d}t+\int_{0}^{T}|\kappa\partial_{t}^{4}\psi(t)|_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t

By combining this with (
4.6.2
), we conclude:

\int_{0}^{T}I_{00}^{*}+I_{30}^{*}\,\mathrm{d}t\lesssim\varepsilon\|\partial_{t}^{3}\partial\check{q}\|_{0}^{2}+\varepsilon\int_{0}^{T}\|\kappa\partial_{t}^{4}\psi(t)\|_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t+\mathcal{P}_{0}^{\kappa}+\int_{0}^{T}P(E^{\kappa}(t))\,\mathrm{d}t.

(4.140)

Next, the term
I_{01}^{*}
can be directly controlled if we insert the continuity equation
\nabla^{\widetilde{\varphi}}\cdot v=-\mathcal{F}^{\prime}(q)D_{t}^{\widetilde{\varphi}}q

I_{01}^{*}\lesssim\left\|\sqrt{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}(q)}\partial_{t}^{4}\check{q}\right\|_{0}\left\|\sqrt{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}(q)}\partial_{t}q,\sqrt{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}(q)}\partial q\right\|_{W^{1,\infty}}|\partial_{t}^{4}\widetilde{\psi}|_{0}.

(4.141)

As for
I_{02}^{*}
, we note that
-\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\widetilde{\varphi}\partial^{\widetilde{\varphi}}_{3}\partial_{t}^{3}v^{i}=\partial^{\varphi}_{i}\partial_{t}^{3}v^{i}-\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\partial_{t}^{3}v_{i}
for
i=1,2
. So it becomes

\displaystyle I_{02}^{*}=

\displaystyle 4\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}^{4}\check{q}\partial_{3}\partial_{t}\widetilde{\varphi}(\nabla^{\widetilde{\varphi}}\cdot\partial_{t}^{3}v)\,\mathrm{d}x-4\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}^{4}\check{q}\partial_{3}\partial_{t}\widetilde{\varphi}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\partial_{t}^{3}v_{i}\,\mathrm{d}x

(4.142)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle L}}{{=}}

\displaystyle 4\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}^{4}\check{q}\partial_{t}^{3}(\nabla^{\widetilde{\varphi}}\cdot v)\partial_{3}\partial_{t}\widetilde{\varphi}\,\mathrm{d}x-4\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}^{4}\check{q}\partial_{3}\partial_{t}\widetilde{\varphi}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\partial_{t}^{3}v_{i}\,\mathrm{d}x,

where the first term is controlled by

\left\|\sqrt{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}(q)}\partial_{t}^{4}\check{q}\right\|_{0}\left(\left\|\sqrt{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}(q)}\partial_{t}^{4}\check{q}\right\|_{0}+\left\|\sqrt{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}(q)}\partial_{t}^{3}\partial\check{q}\right\|_{0}+\left\|\sqrt{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}(q)}\partial_{t}^{3}v_{3}\right\|_{0}\right)|\partial_{t}\widetilde{\psi}|_{\infty}

after invoking the continuity equation, and the second term is controlled under time integral after integrating by parts first in
\partial_{t}
and then in
\overline{\partial}{}_{i}
. So we have:

\int_{0}^{T}I_{02}^{*}\,\mathrm{d}t\lesssim~\varepsilon\|\partial_{t}^{3}\overline{\partial}{}\check{q}\|_{0}^{2}+\mathcal{P}_{0}^{\kappa}+\int_{0}^{T}P(E^{\kappa}(t))\,\mathrm{d}t.

(4.143)

Summarizing (
4.116
), (
4.117
), (
4.120
), (
4.122
), (
4.128
)-(
4.131
), (
4.140
), (
4.141
) and (
4.143
), we finally get the control of the Alinhac good unknowns
\mathbf{V}
and
\mathbf{Q}
with respect to
\partial_{t}^{4}
:

\|\mathbf{V}(T)\|_{0}^{2}+\left\|\sqrt{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}(q)}\mathbf{Q}(T)\right\|_{0}^{2}+\left|\sqrt{\sigma}\overline{\nabla}\partial_{t}^{4}\Lambda_{\kappa}\psi(T)\right|_{0}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}\left|\kappa\partial_{t}^{5}\psi\right|_{1}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\lesssim\varepsilon\left(\|\partial_{t}^{2}\partial^{2}\check{q}\|_{0}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}^{3}\partial\check{q}\|_{0}^{2}\right)+\mathcal{P}_{0}^{\kappa}+\int_{0}^{T}P(E^{\kappa}(t))\,\mathrm{d}t.

(4.144)

To recover the energy for
\|\partial_{t}^{4}v\|_{0}^{2}
and
\|\sqrt{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}(q)}\partial_{t}^{4}\check{q}\|_{0}^{2}
, it suffices to invoke (
4.109
) and use the estimate of
|\partial_{t}^{4}\widetilde{\psi}|_{0}
in (
4.126
). Note that the right side of (
4.126
) has been controlled in
\overline{\partial}{}^{4-k}\partial_{t}^{k}
-estimates for
k\leq 3
, so we already have
|\partial_{t}^{4}\widetilde{\psi}|_{0}\leq\mathcal{P}_{0}^{\kappa}+\int_{0}^{T}P(E^{\kappa}(t))\,\mathrm{d}t
and thus

\|\partial_{t}^{4}v(T)\|_{0}^{2}+\left\|\sqrt{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}(q)}\partial_{t}^{4}\check{q}(T)\right\|_{0}^{2}+\left|\sqrt{\sigma}\overline{\nabla}\partial_{t}^{4}\Lambda_{\kappa}\psi(T)\right|_{0}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}\left|\kappa\partial_{t}^{5}\psi\right|_{1}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\lesssim\varepsilon\left(\|\partial_{t}^{2}\partial^{2}\check{q}\|_{0}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}^{3}\partial\check{q}\|_{0}^{2}\right)+\mathcal{P}_{0}^{\kappa}+\int_{0}^{T}P(E^{\kappa}(t))\,\mathrm{d}t.

(4.145)

4.7
A priori estimates for the nonlinear
\kappa
-approximate problem

Now we choose
\varepsilon>0
suitably small and then combine the tangential estimates (
4.98
) and (
4.145
) with div-curl analysis, reduction of pressure and
L^{2}
-estimates in Section
4.1
–Section
4.3
to get the following energy inequality:

E^{\kappa}(T)\leq E^{\kappa}(0)+\int_{0}^{T}P(E^{\kappa}(t))\,\mathrm{d}t.

(4.146)

Since the right-hand side of the energy inequality does not rely on
\kappa^{-1}
, we can use Grönwall’s inequality to prove that there exists some
T_{0}>0
independent of
\kappa>0
such that

\sup_{0\leq t\leq T_{0}}E^{\kappa}(t)\leq P(E^{\kappa}(0)).

(4.147)

We also note that the above energy estimate does not rely on
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}(q)^{-1}
, as a special cancellation structure enjoyed by the Alinhac good unknowns and delicate analysis (
4.130
)-(
4.143
) exclude the only possibility that might make the energy estimates not uniform in Mach number. Therefore, our a priori bound is also uniform in Mach number.

5
Well-posedness of the nonlinear
\kappa
-approximate system

For the nonlinear
\kappa
-approximate problem (
3.11
), we have established the uniform-in-
\kappa
estimates. Once we prove the well-posedness of (
3.11
) for each
fixed

\kappa>0
, we can take the limit
\kappa\rightarrow 0
to prove the local existence of the original system (
1.24
). We would use Picard iteration to construct the solution to (
3.11
) for each fixed
\kappa>0
. We start with
(v^{(0)},\rho^{(0)},\psi^{(0)}):=(\mathbf{0},1,0)
and also define
\psi^{(-1)}:=\psi^{(0)}
. Then we construct the solution by the following iteration scheme: For any
n\geq 0
, given
\{(v^{(k)},\rho^{(k)},\psi^{(k)})\}_{k\leq n}
, we define
(v^{(n+1)},\rho^{(n+1)},\psi^{(n+1)})
to be the solution to the following linear system whose coefficients depend on
(v^{(n)},\rho^{(n)},\psi^{(n)})
and
\psi^{(n-1)}
:

\begin{cases}\rho^{(n)}D_{t}^{\widetilde{\varphi}^{(n)}}v^{(n+1)}+\nabla^{\widetilde{\varphi}^{(n)}}\check{q}^{(n+1)}=-(\rho^{(n)}-1)ge_{3}&~~~\text{in}\,\,[0,T]\times\Omega,\\
\mathcal{F}^{(n)^{\prime}}(q^{(n)})D_{t}^{\widetilde{\varphi}^{(n)}}\check{q}^{(n+1)}+\nabla^{\widetilde{\varphi}^{(n)}}\cdot v^{(n+1)}=\mathcal{F}^{(n)^{\prime}}(q^{(n)})gv_{3}^{(n)}&~~~\text{in}\,[0,T]\times\Omega,\\
q^{(n+1)}=q^{(n+1)}(\rho^{(n+1)}),\check{q}^{(n+1)}=q^{(n+1)}+g\widetilde{\varphi}^{(n)}&~~~\text{in}\,[0,T]\times\Omega,\\
\check{q}^{(n+1)}=g\widetilde{\psi}^{(n)}-\sigma\overline{\nabla}\cdot\left(\frac{\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}^{(n)}}{\sqrt{1+|\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}^{(n)}|^{2}}}\right)+\kappa^{2}(1-\overline{\Delta}{})(v^{(n+1)}\cdot\widetilde{N}^{(n)})&~~~\text{on}~[0,T]\times\Sigma,\\
\partial_{t}\psi^{(n+1)}=v^{(n+1)}\cdot\widetilde{N}^{(n)}&~~~\text{on}~[0,T]\times\Sigma,\\
v^{(n+1)}_{3}=0&~~~\text{on}~[0,T]\times\Sigma_{b},\\
(v^{(n+1)},\rho^{(n+1)},\psi^{(n+1)})|_{t=0}=(v_{0,\kappa},\rho_{0,\kappa},\psi_{0,\kappa}),\end{cases}

(5.1)

where for any
k\leq n+1
,
\varphi^{(k)}(t,x)
is the extension of
\psi^{(k)}
defined by
\varphi^{(k)}(t,x):=x_{3}+\chi(x_{3})\psi^{(k)}
and
\widetilde{\varphi}^{(k)}:=x_{3}+\chi(x_{3})\widetilde{\psi}^{(k)}
is the smoothed version of
\varphi^{(k)}
. The linearized material derivative is defined to be the following linear operator:

\displaystyle D_{t}^{\widetilde{\varphi}^{(n)}}:=\partial_{t}+\overline{v}^{(n)}\cdot\overline{\nabla}+\frac{1}{\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}^{(n)}}(v^{(n)}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{N}}^{(n-1)}-\partial_{t}\varphi^{(n)})\partial_{3},

(5.2)

and the covariant derivatives are defined to be

\displaystyle\partial_{t}^{\widetilde{\varphi}^{(n)}}

\displaystyle=\partial_{t}-\frac{\partial_{t}\varphi^{(n)}}{\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}^{(n)}}\partial_{3},

(5.3)

\displaystyle\nabla^{\widetilde{\varphi}^{(n)}}_{a}

\displaystyle=\partial^{\widetilde{\varphi}^{(n)}}_{a}=\partial_{a}-\frac{\partial_{a}\widetilde{\varphi}^{(n)}}{\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}^{(n)}}\partial_{3},\quad a=1,2,

(5.4)

\displaystyle\nabla^{\widetilde{\varphi}^{(n)}}_{3}

\displaystyle=\partial^{\widetilde{\varphi}^{(n)}}_{3}=\frac{1}{\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}^{(n)}}\partial_{3}.

(5.5)

Remark 5.1
.

Note that the linearized material derivative is no longer equal to
\partial_{t}^{\widetilde{\varphi}^{(n)}}+v^{(n)}\cdot\nabla^{\widetilde{\varphi}^{(n)}}
. Indeed, one has to set the weight of
\partial_{3}
to be
v^{(n)}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{N}}^{(n-1)}-\partial_{t}\varphi^{(n)}
to guarantee both the linearity of this operator and the consistency with the linearized kinematic boundary condition
\partial_{t}\psi^{(n+1)}=v^{(n+1)}\cdot\widetilde{N}^{(n)}
.

Remark 5.2
.

Note that the surface tension term in (
5.1
) is now replaced by a given term instead of being
-\sigma\overline{\nabla}\cdot(\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}^{(n+1)}/|\widetilde{N}^{(n)})
. Under this setting, we can still do energy estimates for
\psi^{(n+1)}
by using the kinematic boundary condition and the viscosity term.

For simplicity of notations, for any
n\geq 0
, we denote
(v^{(n+1)},\rho^{(n+1)},q^{(n+1)},\psi^{(n+1)})
,
(v^{(n)},\rho^{(n)},q^{(n)},\psi^{(n)})
and
\psi^{(n-1)}
by
(v,\rho,q,\psi)
,
(\mathring{v},\mathring{\rho},\mathring{q},\mathring{\psi})
and
\dot{\psi}
. Hence, we need to solve the following linearized version of system (
3.11
) for each fixed
\kappa>0
and then establish an energy estimate to proceed with the iteration scheme.

\begin{cases}\mathring{\rho}D_{t}^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}v+\nabla^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}\check{q}=-(\mathring{\rho}-1)ge_{3},&~~~\text{in}\,\,[0,T]\times\Omega,\\
\mathring{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}(\mathring{q})D_{t}^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}\check{q}+\nabla^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}\cdot v=\mathring{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}(\mathring{q})g\mathring{v}_{3},&~~~\text{in}\,[0,T]\times\Omega,\\
q=q(\rho),\check{q}=q+g\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}&~~~\text{in}\,[0,T]\times\Omega,\\
\check{q}=g\mathring{\widetilde{\psi}}-\sigma\overline{\nabla}\cdot\left(\frac{\overline{\nabla}\mathring{\widetilde{\psi}}}{\sqrt{1+|\overline{\nabla}\mathring{\widetilde{\psi}}|^{2}}}\right)+\kappa^{2}(1-\overline{\Delta}{})(v\cdot\mathring{\widetilde{N}}),&~~~\text{on}~[0,T]\times\Sigma,\\
\partial_{t}\psi=v\cdot\mathring{\widetilde{N}},&~~~\text{on}~[0,T]\times\Sigma,\\
v_{3}=0&~~~\text{on}~[0,T]\times\Sigma_{b},\\
(v,\rho,\psi)|_{t=0}=(v_{0}^{\kappa},\rho_{0}^{\kappa},\psi_{0}^{\kappa}).\end{cases}

(5.6)

Here
\mathring{\mathcal{F}}:=\log\mathring{\rho}
. The linearized material derivative now becomes:

\displaystyle D_{t}^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}:=\partial_{t}+\overline{\mathring{v}}\cdot\overline{\nabla}+\frac{1}{\partial_{3}\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}(\mathring{v}\cdot\dot{\widetilde{\mathbf{N}}}-\partial_{t}\mathring{\varphi})\partial_{3}

(5.7)

and the covariant derivatives with respect to
\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}
are defined to be

\displaystyle\partial^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}_{t}

\displaystyle:=\partial_{t}-\frac{\partial_{t}\mathring{\varphi}}{\partial_{3}\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}\partial_{3},

(5.8)

\displaystyle\nabla^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}_{a}

\displaystyle=\partial^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}_{a}=\partial_{a}-\frac{\partial_{a}\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}{\partial_{3}\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}\partial_{3},\quad a=1,2,

(5.9)

\displaystyle\nabla^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}_{3}

\displaystyle=\partial^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}_{3}=\frac{1}{\partial_{3}\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}\partial_{3},

(5.10)

where
\overline{\mathring{v}}\cdot\overline{\nabla}:=\mathring{v}_{1}\partial_{1}+\mathring{v}_{2}\partial_{2}
. Note that, by the kinematic boundary condition, the normal component in
D_{t}^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}
, namely
(\partial_{3}\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}})^{-1}(\mathring{v}\cdot\dot{\widetilde{\mathbf{N}}}-\partial_{t}\mathring{\varphi})\partial_{3}
vanishes on
\Sigma
.

From now on, we assume the following given quantities are bounded in some time interval
t\in[0,T^{\kappa}]
. This also works as the induction hypothesis for the uniform-in-
n
estimates for (
5.6
):

\displaystyle\|\mathring{\rho}-1\|_{0}^{2}+\sum_{k=0}^{4}\|\partial_{t}^{k}\mathring{v}\|_{4-k}^{2}+\|\dot{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}(\dot{q})\check{q}\|_{0}^{2}+\|\partial\mathring{\check{q}}\|_{3}^{2}+\sum_{k=1}^{3}\|\partial_{t}^{k}\mathring{\check{q}}\|_{4-k}^{2}+\|\dot{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}(\dot{q})\partial_{t}^{4}\mathring{\check{q}}\|_{0}^{2}

(5.11)

\displaystyle+\kappa^{4}|\mathring{\psi}|_{5.5}^{2}+\sum_{k=0}^{3}\kappa^{4}|\partial_{t}^{k+1}\mathring{\psi},\partial_{t}^{k+1}\dot{\psi}|_{5.5-k}^{2}+\kappa^{2}\int_{0}^{t}|\partial_{t}^{5}\mathring{\psi}|_{1}^{2}\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle<\mathring{K}_{0}.

Here, the additional
\frac{1}{2}
-regularity for
\partial_{t}^{j}\mathring{\psi}
and
\partial_{t}^{j}\dot{\psi}
,
j=0,1,2,3,4
is contributed by the artificial viscosity whenever
\kappa>0
is fixed.

5.1
Construction of solution to the linearized approximate system

We can prove that system (
5.6
) is a symmetric hyperbolic system with characteristic boundary conditions. Therefore, we want to use the duality argument developed by Lax-Phillips
[
40
]
to prove the local existence. Before doing this, we have to make sure the boundary conditions are homogeneous.

5.1.1
The homogeneous linearized approximate system

We introduce the variable
\mathring{\mathfrak{h}}
defined by the harmonic extension

\begin{cases}-\Delta\mathring{\mathfrak{h}}=0~~~&\text{ in }\Omega,\\
\mathring{\mathfrak{h}}=g\mathring{\widetilde{\psi}}-\sigma\overline{\nabla}\cdot\left(\frac{\overline{\nabla}\mathring{\widetilde{\psi}}}{\sqrt{1+|\overline{\nabla}\mathring{\widetilde{\psi}}|^{2}}}\right)~~~&\text{ on }\Sigma,\\
\partial_{3}\mathring{\mathfrak{h}}=0~~~&\text{ on }\Sigma_{b},\\
\end{cases}

(5.12)

and define
\underline{q}=\check{q}-\mathring{\mathfrak{h}}
. Then (
5.6
) becomes the following linear hyperbolic system with
homogeneous
boundary conditions:

\begin{cases}\mathring{\rho}D_{t}^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}v+\nabla^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}\underline{q}=-\nabla^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}\mathring{\mathfrak{h}}-(\mathring{\rho}-1)ge_{3},&~~~\text{in}\,\,[0,T]\times\Omega,\\
\mathring{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}(\mathring{q})D_{t}^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}\underline{q}+\nabla^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}\cdot v=\mathring{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}(\mathring{q})({g}\mathring{v}_{3}-D_{t}^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}\mathring{\mathfrak{h}}),&~~~\text{in}\,[0,T]\times\Omega,\\
q=q(\rho),~\underline{q}=q+g\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}-\mathring{\mathfrak{h}}&~~~\text{in}\,[0,T]\times\Omega,\\
\underline{q}=\kappa^{2}(1-\overline{\Delta}{})(v\cdot\mathring{\widetilde{N}}),&~~~\text{on}~[0,T]\times\Sigma,\\
v_{3}=0&~~~\text{on}~[0,T]\times\Sigma_{b},\\
{(v,\rho)|_{t=0}=(v_{0},\rho_{0})}.\end{cases}

(5.13)

Note that the coefficients in (
5.13
) rely on
\dot{\psi},\mathring{\psi},\mathring{v}
, and
\mathring{\rho}
only, all of which are already given. The kinematic boundary condition, namely
\partial_{t}\psi=v\cdot\mathring{\widetilde{N}}=-(\overline{v}\cdot\overline{\nabla})\mathring{\widetilde{\psi}}+v_{3}
on
\Sigma
, is used to define
\psi
after solving
(v,q)
from (
5.13
).

We define
U:=(\underline{q},v_{1},v_{2},v_{3})^{\top}
, then (
5.13
) can be expressed in terms of
U
by

A_{0}(\mathring{U})\partial_{t}U+\sum_{i=1}^{3}A_{i}(\mathring{U})\partial_{i}U=\mathring{f},

(5.14)

where
\mathring{f}:=\left(\mathring{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}(\mathring{q})(g\mathring{v}_{3}-D_{t}^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}\mathring{\mathfrak{h}}),-\partial^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}_{1}\mathring{\mathfrak{h}},-\partial^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}_{2}\mathring{\mathfrak{h}},-\partial^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}_{3}\mathring{\mathfrak{h}}-(\mathring{\rho}-1)g\right)^{\top}
,
A_{0}(\mathring{U})=\text{diag}\left[\mathring{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}(\mathring{q}),\mathring{\rho},\mathring{\rho},\mathring{\rho}\right]
, and

\displaystyle A_{i}(\mathring{U})=\begin{bmatrix}\mathring{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}(\mathring{q})\mathring{v}_{i}&e_{i}^{\top}\\
e_{i}&\mathring{\rho}\mathring{v}_{i}\mathbf{I}_{3}\end{bmatrix}\text{ for }i=1,2,~~A_{3}(\mathring{U})=\frac{1}{\partial_{3}\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}\begin{bmatrix}\mathring{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}(\mathring{q})(\mathring{v}\cdot\dot{\widetilde{\mathbf{N}}}-\partial_{t}\mathring{\varphi})&\mathring{\widetilde{\mathbf{N}}}^{\top}\\
\mathring{\widetilde{\mathbf{N}}}&\mathring{\rho}(\mathring{v}\cdot\dot{\widetilde{\mathbf{N}}}-\partial_{t}\mathring{\varphi})\mathbf{I}_{3}\end{bmatrix}.

Since
(\partial_{t}\mathring{\varphi}-\mathring{v}\cdot\dot{\widetilde{\mathbf{N}}},)|_{\Sigma}=0
and
e_{3}=(0,0,1)^{\top}
is the unit exterior normal vector to
\Sigma
, we know that the boundary matrix, namely the normal projection of the coefficient matrices onto
\Sigma
, is

\sum\limits_{i=1}^{3}A_{i}(\mathring{U})e_{3i}=A_{3}(\mathring{U})=\begin{bmatrix}0&\mathring{\widetilde{N}}^{\top}\\
\mathring{\widetilde{N}}&\mathbf{0}_{3}\end{bmatrix}~~~\text{ on }\Sigma

which is a
4\times 4
matrix of rank 2 (constant rank but not full rank) with one negative eigenvalue, one positive eigenvalue, and two zero eigenvalues. This being said, the system (
5.13
) is a first-order symmetric hyperbolic system with characteristic boundary conditions. The number of boundary conditions should be equal to the number of negative eigenvalues. Therefore, the correct number of boundary conditions for (
5.13
) is indeed equal to 1 which means (
5.13
) is solvable. After solving (
5.13
), we use the kinematic boundary condition to define
\psi
for the next step of the iteration.

5.1.2
Well-posedness in
L^{2}
via
\mu
-regularization

From the duality argument by Lax-Phillips
[
40
]
, we need to prove the following in order to get the well-posedness of (
5.13
) in some function space
X
:

•

We need to establish a priori estimate (without loss of regularity from the source term) for (
5.13
) in
X
.

•

We need to establish a priori estimate (without loss of regularity from the source term) for the dual system of (
5.13
) in
X^{\prime}
.

We choose
X=L^{2}([0,T];L^{2}(\Omega))
whose dual space
X^{\prime}
is just itself. We define
W^{*}=(\underline{q}^{*},w_{1}^{*},w_{2}^{*},w_{3}^{*})^{\top}
to be the dual variables of
U=(\underline{q},v_{1},v_{2},v_{3})^{\top}
. By testing (
5.13
) with
W^{*}
in
L^{2}([0,T];L^{2}(\Omega))
, one can derive the system of
W^{*}
which reads

A_{0}(\mathring{U})\partial_{t}W^{*}+\sum\limits_{i=1}^{3}A_{i}(\mathring{U})\partial_{i}W^{*}+A_{4}(\mathring{U})W^{*}=\mathring{f}^{*}

with boundary condition
\underline{q}^{*}|_{\Sigma}=-\kappa^{2}(1-\overline{\Delta}{})({w^{*}}\cdot\mathring{\widetilde{N}})
, where
A_{4}:=-\partial_{t}A_{0}^{\top}-\sum\limits_{i=1}^{3}\partial_{i}A_{i}^{\top}-(\mathring{\rho}-1)g\mathbf{E}_{44}
with
\mathbf{E}_{44}=\text{diag}[0,0,0,1]
. Note that we do not have the dual variable for
\psi
because
\psi
is completely determined by the original linearized system. That is why we only have one boundary condition for the dual system.

We notice that there is an extra minus sign in the boundary condition for
\underline{q}^{*}
. So, one cannot close the
L^{2}
-type a priori estimate for the dual system even if we can derive that
L^{2}
-type a priori estimate for (
5.13
). To avoid this difficulty, we introduce another viscosity term in the boundary for
\underline{q}
in (
5.13
). That is, we alternatively consider the
\mu
-regularized linear problem for
U=(\underline{q},v_{1},v_{2},v_{3})^{\top}
, which reads

A_{0}(\mathring{U})\partial_{t}U+\sum_{i=1}^{3}A_{i}(\mathring{U})\partial_{i}U=\mathring{f},

(5.15)

with boundary condition

\displaystyle\underline{q}=\kappa^{2}(1-\overline{\Delta}{})(v\cdot\mathring{\widetilde{N}})+\mu(1-\overline{\Delta}{})\partial_{t}(v\cdot\mathring{\widetilde{N}})~~

\displaystyle\text{ on }\Sigma.

(5.16)

Then the dual system of (
5.15
)-(
5.16
) reads

A_{0}(\mathring{U})\partial_{t}W^{*}+\sum\limits_{i=1}^{3}A_{i}(\mathring{U})\partial_{i}W^{*}+A_{4}(\mathring{U})W^{*}=\mathring{f}^{*}

(5.17)

with boundary condition

\displaystyle\underline{q}^{*}=-\kappa^{2}(1-\overline{\Delta}{})(w^{*}\cdot\mathring{\widetilde{N}})+\mu(1-\overline{\Delta}{})\partial_{t}(w^{*}\cdot\mathring{\widetilde{N}})~~

\displaystyle\text{ on }\Sigma,

(5.18)

where
A_{4}:=-\partial_{t}A_{0}^{\top}-\sum\limits_{i=1}^{3}\partial_{i}A_{i}^{\top}-(\mathring{\rho}-1)g\mathbf{E}_{44}
with
\mathbf{E}_{44}=\text{diag}[0,0,0,1]
. Note that we have to integrate by parts once more in
t
variable when deriving the boundary condition for
\underline{q}^{*}
. This is the reason that an extra minus sign appears in front of
\kappa^{2}(1-\overline{\Delta}{})(w^{*}\cdot\mathring{\widetilde{N}})
.

Now we are going to derive the a priori estimates for both (
5.15
) and (
5.17
). For linear system (
5.15
), we test it with
U
in
L^{2}(\Omega)
and use the symmetry of the coefficient matrices to get:

\int_{\Omega}U^{\top}\cdot A_{0}(\mathring{U})U\,\mathrm{d}x=\int_{\Omega}U^{\top}\cdot\mathring{f}-\sum_{i=1}^{3}\int_{\Omega}U^{\top}\cdot\partial_{i}A_{i}(\mathring{U})U\,\mathrm{d}x-\int_{\Sigma}U^{\top}\cdot A_{3}(\mathring{U})U\,\mathrm{d}x,

(5.19)

where the interior integrals are directly controlled by
C(\mathring{K}_{0})\|U\|_{0}^{2}
and the boundary integral reads:

\displaystyle-\int_{\Sigma}U^{\top}\cdot A_{3}(\mathring{U})U\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}=-2\int_{\Sigma}(v\cdot\mathring{\widetilde{N}})\underline{q}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}

(5.20)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\displaystyle-2\kappa^{2}\int_{\Omega}\left((1-\overline{\Delta}{})(v\cdot\mathring{\widetilde{N}})\right)(v\cdot\mathring{\widetilde{N}})\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}-2\mu\int_{\Sigma}\partial_{t}\left((1-\overline{\Delta}{})(v\cdot\mathring{\widetilde{N}})\right)(v\cdot\mathring{\widetilde{N}})\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\mu\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{\Sigma}\left|\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle(v\cdot\mathring{\widetilde{N}})\right|_{0}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}-2\kappa^{2}\left|\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle(v\cdot\mathring{\widetilde{N}})\right|_{0}^{2}.

We define

\mathring{E}_{0}(t):=\|v(t)\|_{0}^{2}+\left\|\sqrt{\mathring{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}(\mathring{q})}\underline{q}(t)\right\|_{0}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}|\kappa(v\cdot\mathring{\widetilde{N}})(\tau)|_{1}^{2}\mathrm{d}\tau+|\sqrt{\mu}(v\cdot\mathring{\widetilde{N}})(t)|_{1}^{2},

then the above analysis shows that

\mathring{E}_{0}(T)-\mathring{E}_{0}(0)\leq C(\mathring{K}_{0})\int_{0}^{T}\mathring{E}_{0}(t)+\sqrt{\mathring{E}_{0}(t)}\|\mathring{f}(t)\|_{0}\,\mathrm{d}t.

(5.21)

and thus by Grönwall’s inequality we finish the
L^{2}
-estimate for (
5.15
). Note that this a priori bound is also uniform in
\mu
.

Next, we show the
L^{2}
-estimate for the dual system (
5.17
). Note that the matrix
A_{4}(\mathring{U})
is still in
L^{\infty}(\Omega)
, so we test (
5.17
) by
W^{*}
and take
L^{2}
-inner product to get

\int_{\Omega}W^{*\top}\cdot A_{0}(\mathring{U})W^{*}\,\mathrm{d}x=\int_{\Omega}W^{*\top}\cdot\mathring{f}^{*}-W^{*\top}\cdot\left(\sum_{i=1}^{3}\partial_{i}A_{i}(\mathring{U})+A_{4}(\mathring{U})+\mathring{\rho}g\mathbf{E}_{44}\right)W^{*}\,\mathrm{d}x-\int_{\Sigma}(W^{*})^{\top}\cdot A_{3}(\mathring{U})W^{*}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime},

(5.22)

where the interior integral is directly controlled by
C(\mathring{K}_{0})\|W^{*}\|_{0}^{2}
, but now there is a sign change in the boundary integral, which reads:

\displaystyle-\int_{\Sigma}(W^{*})^{\top}\cdot A_{3}(\mathring{U})W^{*}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}=-2\int_{\Sigma}(w^{*}\cdot\mathring{\widetilde{N}})\underline{q}^{*}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}

(5.23)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\kappa^{2}\int_{\Omega}\left((1-\overline{\Delta}{})(w^{*}\cdot\mathring{\widetilde{N}})\right)(w^{*}\cdot\mathring{\widetilde{N}})\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}-2\mu\int_{\Sigma}\partial_{t}\left((1-\overline{\Delta}{})(w^{*}\cdot\mathring{\widetilde{N}})\right)(w^{*}\cdot\mathring{\widetilde{N}})\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle-\mu\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{\Sigma}\left|\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle(w^{*}\cdot\mathring{\widetilde{N}})\right|_{0}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}+2\kappa^{2}\left|\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle(w^{*}\cdot\mathring{\widetilde{N}})\right|_{0}^{2}.

One can see that the new viscosity term involving
\mu
controls the term
2\kappa^{2}|\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle(w^{*}\cdot\mathring{\widetilde{N}})|_{0}^{2}
due to the change of sign.

So, if we define

\mathring{E}_{0}^{*}(t)=\|w^{*}(t)\|_{0}^{2}+\left\|\sqrt{\mathring{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}(\mathring{q})}\underline{q}^{*}(t)\right\|_{0}^{2}+\mu\left|(w^{*}\cdot\mathring{\widetilde{N}})(t)\right|_{1}^{2},

then we have

\mathring{E}_{0}^{*}(T)-\mathring{E}_{0}^{*}(0)\lesssim_{\mu^{-1}}C(\mathring{K}_{0})\int_{0}^{T}\mathring{E}_{0}^{*}(t)+\sqrt{\mathring{E}_{0}^{*}(t)}\|\mathring{f}^{*}(t)\|_{0}\,\mathrm{d}t,

(5.24)

and thus Grönwall’s inequality helps us close the
L^{2}
-estimate.

Combining (
5.21
) and (
5.24
), we close the a priori bounds for both linear systems (
5.15
)-(
5.16
) and its dual system (
5.17
)-(
5.18
). Such energy bounds have no regularity loss from their source terms to solutions. Therefore, by the argument in Lax-Phillips
[
40
]
(see also
[
56
, Theorem 5,9]
), for each fixed
\mu>0
, system (
5.17
)-(
5.18
) admits a unique solution
U\in L^{2}([0,T];L^{2}(\Omega))
. Since the energy bound (
5.21
) for (
5.15
)-(
5.16
) is uniform in
\mu
, we can take the limit
\mu\to 0_{+}
to obtain a local-in-time solution of the homogeneous linearized problem (
5.13
). Finally, the modification
\mathring{\mathfrak{h}}
is easily controlled by using the property of the harmonic function

\forall s>-\frac{1}{2},~~\|\mathring{\mathfrak{h}}\|_{s+\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim|\mathring{\mathfrak{h}}|_{s}\leq g|\mathring{\widetilde{\psi}}|_{s}+P(|\overline{\nabla}\mathring{\widetilde{\psi}}|_{s})|\overline{\nabla}^{2}\mathring{\widetilde{\psi}}|_{s},

which implies the local existence for
L^{2}
-(weak)-solution to the linearized
\kappa
-approximate system (
5.6
). By the argument in
[
51
, Section 2.2.3]
(see also
[
56
, Theorem 4, 8]
), the weak solution
U
is actually a strong solution.

5.2
Higher-order estimates for the linearized system

Now we prove higher-order energy estimates for the linearized system (
5.6
).

Proposition 5.1
.

Let

\displaystyle\mathring{E}^{\kappa}(t):=

\displaystyle\|\rho(t)-1\|_{0}^{2}+\sum_{k=0}^{4}\|\partial_{t}^{k}v(t)\|_{4-k}^{2}+\kappa^{2}\int_{0}^{t}\left|\partial_{t}^{k+1}\psi(\tau)\right|_{5-k}^{2}\mathrm{d}\tau

(5.25)

\displaystyle+\|\sqrt{\mathring{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}(\mathring{q})}\check{q}(t)\|_{0}^{2}+\|\partial\check{q}(t)\|_{3}^{2}+\sum_{k=1}^{3}\|\partial_{t}^{k}\check{q}(t)\|_{4-k}^{2}+\|\sqrt{\mathring{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}(\mathring{q})}\partial_{t}^{4}\check{q}(t)\|_{0}^{2}.

Then there exists some
T^{\kappa}>0
depending on
\kappa
and a constant
C(\kappa^{-1},\mathring{K}_{0})>0
, such that

\sup_{0\leq t\leq T^{\kappa}}\mathring{E}^{\kappa}(t)\leq C(\kappa^{-1},\mathring{K}_{0})\mathring{E}^{\kappa}(0).

(5.26)

Apart from that, we have

|\psi(t)|_{5.5}^{2}+\sum_{k=0}^{3}|\partial_{t}^{k+1}\psi(t)|_{5.5-k}^{2}\leq C(\kappa^{-1},\mathring{K}_{0})\mathring{E}^{\kappa}(t),\quad\text{for all}\,\,t\in[0,T^{\kappa}].

(5.27)

5.2.1
L^{2}
-estimate

We define the
L^{2}
-energy for the linearized system (
5.6
) to be

\mathring{E}_{0}^{\kappa}(t):=\|\rho(t)-1\|_{0}^{2}+\|v(t)\|_{0}^{2}+\|\sqrt{\mathring{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}(\mathring{q})}\check{q}(t)\|_{0}^{2}+\kappa^{2}\int_{0}^{t}|\partial_{t}\psi(\tau)|_{1}^{2}\mathrm{d}\tau.

(5.28)

The control of
\mathring{E}_{0}
is identical to the a priori estimate for (
5.15
) when
\mu=0
. Note that the control of
\|\rho-1\|_{0}^{2}
follows from testing the linearized continuity equation
\mathring{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}(\mathring{q})\mathcal{F}^{\prime}(q)^{-1}D_{t}^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}(\rho-1)+\rho(\nabla^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}\cdot v)=0
by
\rho-1
in
L^{2}(\Omega)
. Also one can control the
L^{2}(\Sigma)
norm of
\psi
through
\psi(t)=\psi_{0,\kappa}+\int_{0}^{t}\partial_{t}\psi(\tau)\mathrm{d}\tau.

5.2.2
Div-Curl analysis

To estimate the Sobolev norms of
v
, we invoke the following Hodge decomposition lemma which is exactly from
[
10
, Theorem 1.1]
.

Lemma 5.2

(Hodge elliptic estimates)
.

For any sufficiently smooth vector field
X
and
s\geq 1
, one has

\|X\|_{s}^{2}\lesssim C(|\mathring{\widetilde{\psi}}|_{s+\frac{1}{2}},|\overline{\nabla}\mathring{\widetilde{\psi}}|_{W^{1,\infty}})\left(\|X\|_{0}^{2}+\|\nabla^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}\cdot X\|_{s-1}^{2}+\|\nabla^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}\times X\|_{s-1}^{2}+|X\cdot\mathring{\widetilde{N}}|_{s-\frac{1}{2}}^{2}+|X_{3}|_{H^{s-\frac{1}{2}}(\Sigma_{b})}^{2}\right),

(5.29)

where the constant
C(|\mathring{\widetilde{\psi}}|_{s+\frac{1}{2}},|\overline{\nabla}\mathring{\widetilde{\psi}}|_{W^{1,\infty}})>0
depends linearly on
|\mathring{\widetilde{\psi}}|_{s+\frac{1}{2}}^{2}
.

Applying this lemma to
v
with
s=4
, one has

\|v\|_{4}^{2}\lesssim C(|\mathring{\widetilde{\psi}}|_{4.5},|\overline{\nabla}\mathring{\widetilde{\psi}}|_{W^{1,\infty}})\left(\|v\|_{0}^{2}+\|\nabla^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}\cdot v\|_{3}^{2}+\|\nabla^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}\times v\|_{3}^{2}+|v\cdot\mathring{\widetilde{N}}|_{3.5}^{2}\right).

(5.30)

Now we control the curl term. Taking
\nabla^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}\times
in the first equation of (
5.6
), we get the evolution equation satisfied by
\nabla^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}\times v
:

\mathring{\rho}D_{t}^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}(\nabla^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}\times v)=\mathring{\rho}[\nabla^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}\times,D_{t}^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}]v+\nabla^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}\mathring{\rho}\times(\mathring{\rho}^{-1}\nabla^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}\check{q}),

(5.31)

and taking three derivatives we get

\mathring{\rho}D_{t}^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}\partial^{3}(\nabla^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}\times v)=\partial^{3}\left(\mathring{\rho}[\nabla^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}\times,D_{t}^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}]v)+\nabla^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}\mathring{\rho}\times(\mathring{\rho}^{-1}\nabla^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}\check{q})\right)-[\partial^{3},\mathring{\rho}D_{t}^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}](\nabla^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}\times v).

(5.32)

We expect that the source terms in (
5.32
) only contain
\leq 4
derivatives of
v,\check{q}
and quantities marked with a ring, but there still exists a mismatched term in
([\nabla^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}\times,D_{t}^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}]v)^{i}=\epsilon^{ijk}\nabla^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}_{j}\mathring{v}^{l}\nabla^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}_{l}v_{k}+\epsilon^{ijk}\nabla^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}_{j}\partial_{t}(\varphi-\dot{\widetilde{\varphi}})\partial^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}_{3}v_{k}
. The contribution of
\dot{\widetilde{\psi}}
is controlled by
\mathring{K}_{0}
. So, the standard
L^{2}
-estimate for the
\partial^{3}
-differentiated evolution equation of
\nabla^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}\times v
and Reynold transport formula (
A.9
) gives

\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|\nabla^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}\times v\|_{3}^{2}\leq P(\mathring{K}_{0})(\|v\|_{4}^{2}+\|\check{q}\|_{4}\|v\|_{4}+|\partial_{t}\psi|_{4}\|\partial v\|_{\infty}).

(5.33)

Finally, using the linearized continuity equation, we can control the divergence

\|\nabla^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}\cdot v\|_{3}^{2}\leq\left\|\mathring{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}(\mathring{q})D_{t}^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}\check{q}\right\|_{3}^{2}+\left\|\mathring{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}(\mathring{q})g\mathring{v}_{3}\right\|_{3}^{2}.

(5.34)

The div-curl analysis for the time derivatives is proceeded similarly. First, the div-curl analysis for
\|\partial_{t}^{k}v\|_{4-k}^{2}
,
1\leq k\leq 3
yields

\|\partial_{t}^{k}v\|_{4-k}^{2}\lesssim C(|\mathring{\widetilde{\psi}}|_{4.5-k},|\overline{\nabla}\mathring{\widetilde{\psi}}|_{W^{1,\infty}})\left(\|\partial_{t}^{k}v\|_{0}^{2}+\|\nabla^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}\cdot\partial_{t}^{k}v\|_{3-k}^{2}+\|\nabla^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}\times\partial_{t}^{k}v\|_{3-k}^{2}+|\partial_{t}^{k}v\cdot\mathring{\widetilde{N}}|_{3.5-k}^{2}\right).

(5.35)

We replace
\partial^{3}
by
\partial_{t}^{k}\partial^{3-k}
for
0\leq k\leq 3
in (
5.32
) to get the evolution equation:

\mathring{\rho}D_{t}^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}\left(\partial^{3-k}\partial_{t}^{k}(\nabla^{\widetilde{\varphi}}\times v)\right)=\partial_{t}^{k}\partial^{3-k}\left(\mathring{\rho}[\nabla^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}\times,D_{t}^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}]v)+\nabla^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}\mathring{\rho}\times(\mathring{\rho}^{-1}\nabla^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}\check{q})\right)-[\partial_{t}^{k}\partial^{3-k},\mathring{\rho}D_{t}^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}](\nabla^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}\times v),

(5.36)

and thus

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\frac{1}{2}\|\partial_{t}^{k}(\nabla^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}\times v)\|_{3-k}^{2}\leq P(\mathring{E}^{\kappa}(t)).

(5.37)

Now, since the leading order term in the commutator
[\partial_{t}^{k},\nabla^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}\times]v
should be
\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}\partial_{t}^{k-1}\partial_{3}v
, we have

\|\nabla^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}\times\partial_{t}^{k}v\|_{3-k}^{2}\leq C(\mathring{K}_{0})\left(\mathring{E}^{\kappa}(0)+\int_{0}^{T}\mathring{E}^{\kappa}(t)\,\mathrm{d}t\right).

(5.38)

As for divergence, by taking
\partial_{t}^{k}
,
1\leq k\leq 3
in the continuity equation, we get

\nabla^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}\cdot\partial_{t}^{k}v=-\partial_{t}^{k}(\mathring{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}(\mathring{q})D_{t}^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}\check{q}+\mathring{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}(\mathring{q})g\mathring{v}_{3})+[\nabla^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}\cdot,\partial_{t}^{k}]v\stackrel{{\scriptstyle L}}{{=}}-\mathring{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}(\mathring{q})(\partial_{t}^{k}D_{t}^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}\check{q}+g\partial_{t}^{k}\mathring{v}_{3})+(\partial_{3}\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}})^{-1}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{k}\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}\partial_{3}v.

Parallel to the analysis for (
4.25
), since
\|\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{k}\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}\|_{3-k}\leq\mathring{K}_{0}
thanks to (
5.11
), we have
\|\nabla^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}\cdot\partial_{t}^{k}v\|_{3-k}
is reduced to the control of
\|\mathring{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}(\mathring{q})\partial_{t}^{k+1}\check{q}\|_{3-k}
and
\|\mathring{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}(\mathring{q})\partial_{t}^{k}\check{q}\|_{4-k}
at the top order. Thus,

\|\nabla^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}\cdot\partial_{t}^{k}v\|_{3-k}^{2}\leq(C(\mathring{K}_{0})+1)\left(\left\|\mathring{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}(q)\partial_{t}^{k+1}\check{q}\right\|_{3-k}^{2}+\left\|\mathring{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}(q)\partial_{t}^{k}\check{q}\right\|_{4-k}^{2}\right).

(5.39)

5.2.3
Estimates for
\psi
and normal traces

The normal trace terms in (
5.30
) and (
5.35
) can be directly controlled by applying boundary elliptic estimates to the linearized viscous surface tension equation
\kappa^{2}(1-\overline{\Delta}{})(v\cdot\mathring{\widetilde{N}})=q-\sigma\mathcal{H}(\overline{\nabla}\mathring{\widetilde{\psi}},\overline{\nabla}^{2}\mathring{\widetilde{\psi}})
. We start with controlling
|v\cdot\mathring{\widetilde{N}}|_{3.5}
:

|v\cdot\mathring{\widetilde{N}}|_{3.5}^{2}\leq\kappa^{-2}\left(|q|_{1.5}^{2}+\sigma|\overline{\nabla}^{2}\mathring{\widetilde{\psi}}|_{1.5}^{2}P(|\overline{\nabla}\mathring{\widetilde{\psi}}|_{1.5})\right)\leq\kappa^{-2}P(\mathring{K}_{0})\|\check{q}\|_{2}^{2}.

(5.40)

Taking time derivatives in the kinematic boundary condition, we obtain:

\partial_{t}^{k}v\cdot\mathring{\widetilde{N}}=\partial_{t}^{k+1}\psi-\sum_{j=1}^{k}\binom{k}{j}\partial_{t}^{k-j}\overline{v}\cdot\partial_{t}^{j}\overline{\nabla}\mathring{\widetilde{\psi}},

and thus

|\partial_{t}v\cdot\mathring{\widetilde{N}}|_{2.5}\leq|\partial_{t}^{2}\psi|_{2.5}+|\overline{v}\cdot\overline{\nabla}\partial_{t}\mathring{\widetilde{\psi}}|_{2.5}\leq|\partial_{t}^{2}\psi|_{2.5}+\|v_{\kappa,0}\|_{3}^{2}+P(\mathring{K}_{0})\int_{0}^{T}\|\partial_{t}\overline{v}(t)\|_{3}\,\mathrm{d}t.

(5.41)

Then we take a time derivative in the linearized viscous surface tension equation to get

\kappa(1-\overline{\Delta}{})\partial_{t}^{2}\psi=\partial_{t}q-\sigma\partial_{t}\mathcal{H}(\overline{\nabla}\mathring{\widetilde{\psi}},\overline{\nabla}^{2}\mathring{\widetilde{\psi}}),

which implies
|\partial_{t}^{2}\psi|_{2.5}\leq\|\partial_{t}q\|_{1}+P(\mathring{K}_{0})
. Repeatedly, we can take more time derivatives to obtain

|\partial_{t}^{k}v\cdot\mathring{\widetilde{N}}|_{3.5-k}^{2}\leq|\partial_{t}^{k+1}\psi|_{3.5-k}^{2}+\mathcal{P}_{0}^{\kappa}+P(\mathring{K}_{0})\int_{0}^{T}\mathring{E}^{\kappa}(t)\,\mathrm{d}t,

(5.42)

and then
|\partial_{t}^{k+1}\psi|_{3.5-k}
is controlled via boundary elliptic estimates:

\displaystyle|\partial_{t}^{3}\psi|_{1.5}^{2}\approx

\displaystyle~|\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{-\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{t}^{3}\psi|_{2}^{2}\leq|\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{-\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{t}^{2}\check{q}|_{0}^{2}+P(\mathring{K}_{0})\leq\|\partial_{t}^{2}\check{q}\|_{1}^{2}+P(\mathring{K}_{0})\leq\|\partial_{t}^{2}\check{q}(0)\|_{1}^{2}+P(\mathring{K}_{0})+\int_{0}^{T}\mathring{E}^{\kappa}(t)\,\mathrm{d}t,

(5.43)

\displaystyle|\partial_{t}^{4}\psi|_{0.5}\approx

\displaystyle~|\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{-\frac{3}{2}}\partial_{t}^{4}\psi|_{2}\leq|\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{-\frac{3}{2}}\partial_{t}^{3}\check{q}|_{0}+P(\mathring{K}_{0})\leq\|\partial_{t}^{3}\check{q}\|_{1}+P(\mathring{K}_{0}),

(5.44)

where the leading order term
\|\partial\partial_{t}^{3}\check{q}\|_{0}
on the RHS of (
5.44
) will be further reduced through the reduction scheme shown in the upcoming subsection.

5.2.4
Reduction of pressure

We start with
\|\check{q}\|_{4}
. From the linearized momentum equation, we know

\displaystyle-(\partial_{3}\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}})^{-1}\partial_{3}q=

\displaystyle~(\mathring{\rho}-1)g+\mathring{\rho}D_{t}^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}v_{3},

\displaystyle-\partial_{i}q=

\displaystyle~(\partial_{3}\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}})^{-1}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}\partial_{3}q+\mathring{\rho}D_{t}^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}v_{i},~~i=1,2,

and thus we have the following estimates after taking
\partial^{3}
and using
D_{t}^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}=(\partial_{t}+\overline{\mathring{v}}\cdot\overline{\nabla})+(\partial_{3}\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}})^{-1}(\mathring{v}\cdot\mathring{\widetilde{\mathbf{N}}}-\partial_{t}\mathring{\varphi})\partial_{3}
to get

\|\check{q}\|_{4}\lesssim_{\mathring{K}_{0}}\|\check{q}\|_{0}+\|\mathcal{T}v\|_{3}+\|\mathring{\rho}-1\|_{3},

(5.45)

where
\mathcal{T}
denotes a tangential derivative, including
\partial_{t},\overline{\partial}{}
and
\omega(x)\partial_{3}
for some weight function
\omega
that vanishes on
\Sigma
and is approximately equal to
|x_{3}|
near
\Sigma
. Replacing
\partial^{3}
by
\partial^{3-k}\partial_{t}^{k}
, we know the estimate of
\|\partial_{t}^{k}\partial^{4-k}\check{q}\|_{0}
is reduced to the estimate of
\|\partial_{t}^{k}\mathcal{T}v\|_{3-k}
. Combining this with the div-curl analysis in Subsection
5.2.2
we can reduce the top order mixed norms
\|\partial_{t}^{k}\partial^{4-k}v\|_{0}
and
\|\partial_{t}^{k}\partial^{4-k}\check{q}\|_{0}
to
\|\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}v\|_{0}
,
|\alpha|=4
, and
\left\|\sqrt{\mathring{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}(\mathring{q})}\partial_{t}^{4}\check{q}\right\|_{0}
, all of which are part of the tangential energy.

5.2.5
Control of full time derivatives

From the reduction procedures for
\check{q}
and the div-curl analysis for
v
, we know a spatial derivative of
\check{q}
is reduced to a tangential derivative of
v
, and the divergence of
v
is reduced to
\mathring{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}(\mathring{q})\partial_{t}\check{q}
. Repeatedly, it remains to control
\sqrt{\mathring{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}(\mathring{q})}\partial_{t}^{4}\check{q}
and
\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}v
with
|\alpha|=4
in
L^{2}(\Omega)
. Here we only present the proof for the estimate with full-time derivatives which is parallel to Section
4.6
, and the mixed space-time tangential estimates are easier. We introduce the Alinhac good unknowns
\mathring{\mathbf{V}},\mathring{\mathbf{Q}}
for the
\partial_{t}^{4}
-differentiated linearized system (
5.6
):

\mathring{\mathbf{V}}:=\partial_{t}^{4}v-\partial_{t}^{4}\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}\partial^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}_{3}v,~~~\mathring{\mathbf{Q}}:=\partial_{t}^{4}\check{q}-\partial_{t}^{4}\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}\partial^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}_{3}\check{q}

(5.46)

Similar to the arguments in Section
4.6
, when
f=v_{i}
and
\check{q}
, the following identity holds:

\partial_{t}^{4}(\nabla^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}_{i}f)=\nabla^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}_{i}\mathring{\mathbf{F}}+\mathring{\mathfrak{C}}_{i}(f),

(5.47)

where
\mathring{\mathfrak{C}}_{i}(f):=\partial^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}_{3}\partial^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}_{i}f\partial_{t}^{4}\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}+\mathring{\mathfrak{C}}^{\prime}_{i}(f)
. Also,

\displaystyle\mathring{\mathfrak{C}}_{i}^{\prime}(f)=

\displaystyle-\left[\partial_{t}^{4},\frac{\partial_{i}\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}{\partial_{3}\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}},\partial_{3}f\right]-\partial_{3}f\left[\partial_{t}^{4},\partial_{i}\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}},\frac{1}{\partial_{3}\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}\right]-\partial_{i}\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}\partial_{3}f\left[\partial_{t}^{3},\frac{1}{(\partial_{3}\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}})^{2}}\right]\partial_{t}\partial_{3}\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}},~~i=1,2

(5.48)

\displaystyle\mathring{\mathfrak{C}}_{3}^{\prime}(f)=

\displaystyle\left[\partial_{t}^{4},\frac{1}{\partial_{3}\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}},\partial_{3}f\right]+\partial_{3}f\left[\partial_{t}^{3},\frac{1}{(\partial_{3}\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}})^{2}}\right]\partial_{t}\partial_{3}\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}.

(5.49)

Then we take
\partial_{t}^{4}
to the first two equations of (
5.6
) to obtain:

\displaystyle\mathring{\rho}D_{t}^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}\mathring{\mathbf{V}}_{i}+\nabla^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}_{i}\mathring{\mathbf{Q}}=

\displaystyle~\mathring{\mathcal{R}}^{1}_{i},

(5.50)

\displaystyle\mathring{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}(\mathring{q})D_{t}^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}\mathring{\mathbf{Q}}+\nabla^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}\cdot\mathring{\mathbf{V}}=

\displaystyle~\mathring{\mathcal{R}}^{2}-\mathring{\mathfrak{C}}_{i}(v^{i}),

(5.51)

where

\displaystyle\mathring{\mathcal{R}}^{1}_{i}:=

\displaystyle-[\partial_{t}^{4},\mathring{\rho}]D_{t}^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}v_{i}-\mathring{\rho}\left(\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}(v_{i})+\mathring{\mathfrak{e}}(v_{i})\right)-\mathring{\mathfrak{C}}_{i}(\check{q})-\partial_{t}^{4}\mathring{\rho}g\delta_{3i},

(5.52)

\displaystyle\mathring{\mathcal{R}}^{2}:=

\displaystyle-[\partial_{t}^{4},\mathring{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}(\mathring{q})]D_{t}^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}\check{q}-\mathring{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}(\mathring{q})\left(\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}(\check{q})+\mathring{\mathfrak{e}}(\check{q})\right)+\partial_{t}^{4}(\mathring{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}(\mathring{q})g\mathring{v}_{3}),

(5.53)

and the commutators
\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}(f),\mathring{\mathfrak{e}}(f)
are defined in the same way as in (
4.39
) and (
4.40
) by replacing
\mathcal{T}^{\alpha},\overline{\partial}{},\widetilde{\varphi}
respectively with
\partial_{t}^{4},\partial_{t},\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}
. The last two terms in (
4.39
) vanish because
\partial_{t}^{4}
commutes with
\partial_{3}
. Specifically, we have:

\displaystyle\partial_{t}^{4}D_{t}^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}f

\displaystyle=D_{t}^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}\mathring{\mathbf{F}}+\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}(f)+\mathring{\mathfrak{e}}(f),

(5.54)

where
\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}(f):=(D_{t}^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}\partial^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}_{3}f)\partial_{t}^{4}\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}+\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}^{\prime}(f)
, and

\displaystyle\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}^{\prime}(f)=

\displaystyle[\partial_{t}^{4},\overline{\mathring{v}}]\cdot\overline{\partial}{}f+\left[\partial_{t}^{4},\frac{1}{\partial_{3}\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}(\mathring{v}\cdot\dot{\widetilde{\mathbf{N}}}-\partial_{t}\mathring{\varphi}),\partial_{3}f\right]+\left[\partial_{t}^{4},\mathring{v}\cdot\dot{\widetilde{\mathbf{N}}}-\partial_{t}\mathring{\varphi},\frac{1}{\partial_{3}\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}\right]\partial_{3}f+\frac{1}{\partial_{3}\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}[\partial_{t}^{4},\mathring{v}]\cdot\dot{\widetilde{\mathbf{N}}}\partial_{3}f

\displaystyle-4(\mathring{v}\cdot\dot{\widetilde{\mathbf{N}}}-\partial_{t}\mathring{\varphi})\partial_{3}f\left[\partial_{t}^{3},\frac{1}{(\partial_{3}\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}})^{2}}\right]\partial_{t}\partial_{3}\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}},

(5.55)

\mathring{\mathfrak{e}}(f):=\partial_{t}^{5}(\dot{\widetilde{\varphi}}-\mathring{\varphi})\partial^{\widetilde{\varphi}}_{3}f.

(5.56)

Analogous to Lemma
4.4
, the following estimates hold.

Lemma 5.3
.

Let
\mathring{\mathbf{F}}:=\partial_{t}^{4}f-\partial^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}_{3}f\partial_{t}^{4}\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}
be the Alinhac good unknowns associated with the smooth function
f
. Assume
\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}\geq c_{0}>0
, and let
\mathring{\mathfrak{C}}(f)
,
\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}(f)
, and
\mathring{\mathfrak{e}}(f)
be the remainder terms defined as above. Then

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}^{4}f\|_{0}\leq

\displaystyle~\|\mathring{\mathbf{F}}\|_{0}+c_{0}^{-1}\|\partial_{3}f\|_{\infty}|\partial_{t}^{4}\widetilde{\psi}|_{0},

(5.57)

\displaystyle\|\mathring{\mathfrak{C}}_{i}(f)\|_{0}\leq

\displaystyle~P\left(c_{0}^{-1},|\overline{\nabla}\mathring{\widetilde{\psi}}|_{\infty},\sum_{k=1}^{3}|\overline{\nabla}\partial_{t}^{k}\mathring{\widetilde{\psi}}|_{3-k}\right)\cdot\left(\|\partial f\|_{\infty}+\sum_{k=1}^{3}\|\partial_{t}^{k}f\|_{4-k}\right),\quad i=1,2,3,

(5.58)

\displaystyle\|\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}(f)\|_{0}\leq

\displaystyle~P\left(c_{0}^{-1},|\overline{\nabla}\mathring{\widetilde{\psi}}|_{\infty},\sum_{k=1}^{3}|\overline{\nabla}\partial_{t}^{k}\mathring{\widetilde{\psi}},\overline{\nabla}\partial_{t}^{k}\dot{\widetilde{\psi}}|_{3-k}\right)\cdot\left(\|\partial f\|_{\infty}+\sum_{k=1}^{3}\|\partial_{t}^{k}f\|_{4-k}\right),

(5.59)

\displaystyle\|\mathring{\mathfrak{e}}(f)\|_{0}\leq

\displaystyle~(|\partial_{t}^{5}\psi|_{0}+|\partial_{t}^{5}\dot{\widetilde{\psi}}|_{0})\|\partial f\|_{\infty}.

(5.60)

Next, we introduce the boundary conditions for
\mathring{\mathbf{V}},\mathring{\mathbf{Q}}
. The
\partial_{t}^{4}
-differentiated linearized kinematic boundary condition now reads:

\partial_{t}^{5}\psi+(\overline{v}\cdot\overline{\nabla})\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\mathring{\widetilde{\psi}}-\mathring{\mathbf{V}}\cdot\mathring{\widetilde{N}}=\mathring{\mathcal{S}}_{1},\quad\text{on}\,\,\Sigma,

(5.61)

where

\displaystyle\mathring{\mathcal{S}}_{1}:=\partial_{3}v\cdot\mathring{\widetilde{N}}\partial_{t}^{4}\mathring{\widetilde{\psi}}+\sum_{1\leq j\leq 3}\binom{4}{j}\partial_{t}^{j}v\cdot\partial_{t}^{4-j}\mathring{\widetilde{N}}.

(5.62)

Also, since
\mathring{\mathbf{Q}}|_{\Sigma}=\partial_{t}^{4}\check{q}-\partial^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}_{3}\check{q}\partial_{t}^{4}\mathring{\widetilde{\psi}}
, the boundary condition of
\mathring{\mathbf{Q}}
on
\Sigma
reads:

\displaystyle\mathring{\mathbf{Q}}=-\sigma\partial_{t}^{4}\overline{\nabla}\cdot\left(\frac{\overline{\nabla}\mathring{\widetilde{\psi}}}{\sqrt{1+|\overline{\nabla}\mathring{\widetilde{\psi}}|^{2}}}\right)+\kappa^{2}(1-\overline{\Delta}{})\partial_{t}^{5}\psi-\partial_{3}\check{q}\partial_{t}^{4}\mathring{\widetilde{\psi}}+g\partial_{t}^{4}\mathring{\widetilde{\psi}}.

(5.63)

Invoking (
A.9
), we have

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega}\mathring{\rho}|\mathring{\mathbf{V}}|^{2}\partial_{3}\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}\,\mathrm{d}x=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega}|\mathring{\mathbf{V}}|^{2}\left((D_{t}^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}\mathring{\rho}+\mathring{\rho}\nabla^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}\cdot\mathring{v})\partial_{3}\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}+\mathring{\rho}\mathring{M}\right)\,\mathrm{d}x

(5.64)

\displaystyle+\int_{\Omega}\mathring{\mathbf{Q}}(\nabla^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}\cdot\mathring{\mathbf{V}})\partial_{3}\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}\,\mathrm{d}x-\int_{\Sigma}\mathring{\mathbf{Q}}(\mathring{\mathbf{V}}\cdot\mathring{\widetilde{N}})\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}+\int_{\Omega}\mathring{\mathbf{V}}\cdot\mathring{\mathcal{R}}^{1}\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}\,\mathrm{d}x,

where
\mathring{M}:=\partial_{t}\partial_{3}(\dot{\widetilde{\varphi}}-\mathring{\varphi})+\partial_{3}(\partial_{t}+\overline{\mathring{v}}\cdot\overline{\nabla})(\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}-\dot{\widetilde{\varphi}})
represents the mismatched terms involving tangential smoothing in (
A.9
). The first integral on the RHS can be directly controlled by
P(\mathring{K}_{0})
because all these quantities are already given. Moreover, the last integral is directly controlled by
P(\mathring{K}_{0})\|\mathring{\mathbf{V}}\|_{0}\sqrt{\mathring{E}^{\kappa}(t)}
. For the second term in (
5.64
), we invoke (
5.51
) to get the estimates parallel to (
4.117
):

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\mathring{\mathbf{Q}}(\nabla^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}\cdot\mathring{\mathbf{V}})\partial_{3}\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}\,\mathrm{d}x

(5.65)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\underbrace{-\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}^{4}\check{q}\mathring{\mathfrak{C}}_{i}(v^{i})\partial_{3}\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}\,\mathrm{d}x}_{=:\mathring{I}_{0}}+\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}^{4}\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}\partial^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}_{3}\check{q}\mathring{\mathfrak{C}}_{i}(v^{i})\partial_{3}\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}\,\mathrm{d}x-\int_{\Omega}\mathring{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}(\mathring{q})D_{t}^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}\mathring{\mathbf{Q}}~\mathring{\mathbf{Q}}\partial_{3}\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}\,\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Omega}\mathring{\mathcal{R}}^{2}\mathring{\mathbf{Q}}\partial_{3}\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\mathring{I}_{0}-\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left\|\sqrt{\mathring{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}(\mathring{q})}\mathbf{Q}\right\|_{0}^{2}+\left\|\sqrt{\mathring{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}(\mathring{q})}\partial_{t}^{4}\check{q}\right\|_{0}^{2}(\|\nabla^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}\cdot\mathring{v}\|_{\infty}+\|\mathring{M}\|_{\infty})

\displaystyle+\|\mathring{\mathfrak{C}}_{i}(v^{i})\|_{0}\|\partial\check{q}\|_{\infty}|\partial_{t}^{4}\mathring{\widetilde{\psi}}|_{0}+\left\|\sqrt{\mathring{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}(\mathring{q})}\mathbf{Q}\right\|_{0}\left\|\sqrt{\mathring{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}(\mathring{q})}^{-1}\mathring{\mathcal{R}}^{2}\right\|_{0}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\mathring{I}_{0}-\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left\|\sqrt{\mathring{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}(\mathring{q})}\mathbf{Q}\right\|_{0}^{2}+P(\mathring{K}_{0})\mathring{E}^{\kappa}(t),

where we note that all terms in
\mathring{\mathcal{R}}^{2}
come with
\mathring{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}(\mathring{q})
and thus the control of
\sqrt{\mathring{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}(\mathring{q})}^{-1}\mathring{\mathcal{R}}^{2}
is still uniform in
\mathring{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}(\mathring{q})
.

Now it remains to control the boundary integral. Compared with the nonlinear system, the estimate for the linearized system is easier as the surface tension term now becomes a given term. Plugging (
5.61
) and (
5.63
) into the boundary integral, we get

\displaystyle-\int_{\Sigma}\mathring{\mathbf{Q}}(\mathring{\mathbf{V}}\cdot\mathring{\widetilde{N}})\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}=

\displaystyle-\int_{\Sigma}\partial_{t}^{4}\overline{\nabla}\cdot(\overline{\nabla}\mathring{\widetilde{\psi}}/|\mathring{\widetilde{N}}|)\partial_{t}^{5}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}-\kappa^{2}\int_{\Sigma}\partial_{t}^{4}(1-\overline{\Delta}{})\partial_{t}\psi\cdot\partial_{t}^{5}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}

(5.66)

\displaystyle-\int_{\Sigma}g\partial_{t}^{4}\mathring{\widetilde{\psi}}\partial_{t}^{5}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}+\int_{\Sigma}\partial_{3}\check{q}\partial_{t}^{4}\mathring{\widetilde{\psi}}\partial_{t}^{5}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}

\displaystyle-\int_{\Sigma}\mathbf{Q}(\overline{\mathring{v}}\cdot\overline{\nabla})\partial_{t}^{4}\mathring{\widetilde{\psi}}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}+\int_{\Sigma}\mathring{\mathbf{Q}}\mathring{\mathcal{S}}_{1}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime},

where the second term gives us the boundary energy

-\kappa^{2}\int_{\Sigma}\partial_{t}^{4}(1-\overline{\Delta}{})\partial_{t}\psi\cdot\partial_{t}^{5}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}=\kappa^{2}\int_{\Sigma}\left|\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle\partial_{t}^{5}\psi\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}.

(5.67)

We note that the first, the third, and the fourth terms in (
5.66
) can all be directly controlled under the time integral, i.e.,

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Sigma}\partial_{t}^{4}\overline{\nabla}\cdot(\overline{\nabla}\mathring{\widetilde{\psi}}/|\mathring{\widetilde{N}}|)\partial_{t}^{5}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}\,\mathrm{d}t\lesssim

\displaystyle~\varepsilon|\partial_{t}^{5}\psi|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{x^{\prime}}^{1}}^{2}+P(|\overline{\nabla}\mathring{\widetilde{\psi}}|_{\infty})|\overline{\nabla}\partial_{t}^{4}\mathring{\widetilde{\psi}}|_{0}^{2}\leq_{\kappa^{-1}}\varepsilon\mathring{E}^{\kappa}(T)+P(\mathring{K}_{0})

(5.68)

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Sigma}(g-\partial_{3}\check{q})\partial_{t}^{4}\mathring{\widetilde{\psi}}\partial_{t}^{5}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}\,\mathrm{d}t\leq

\displaystyle~\varepsilon|\partial_{t}^{5}\psi|_{L_{t}^{2}L_{x^{\prime}}^{2}}^{2}+|\partial_{t}^{4}\mathring{\widetilde{\psi}}|_{0}^{2}(1+\|\partial\check{q}\|_{L_{t}^{2}L_{x}^{\infty}}^{2})\leq_{\kappa^{-1}}\varepsilon\mathring{E}^{\kappa}(T)+P(\mathring{K}_{0})\int_{0}^{T}\mathring{E}^{\kappa}(t)\,\mathrm{d}t.

(5.69)

Further, the fifth term is controlled directly by invoking (
3.5
):

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Sigma}\mathbf{Q}(\overline{\mathring{v}}\cdot\overline{\nabla})\partial_{t}^{4}\mathring{\widetilde{\psi}}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}\,\mathrm{d}t=

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{T}\sigma\int_{\Sigma}\partial_{t}^{4}\overline{\nabla}\cdot(\overline{\nabla}\mathring{\widetilde{\psi}}/|\mathring{\widetilde{N}}|)(\overline{\mathring{v}}\cdot\overline{\nabla})\partial_{t}^{4}\mathring{\widetilde{\psi}}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}\,\mathrm{d}t+\kappa^{2}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Sigma}(1-\overline{\Delta}{})\partial_{t}^{5}\psi(\overline{\mathring{v}}\cdot\overline{\nabla})\partial_{t}^{4}\mathring{\widetilde{\psi}}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}\,\mathrm{d}t

(5.70)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Sigma}(g-\partial_{3}\check{q})\partial_{t}^{4}\mathring{\widetilde{\psi}}(\overline{\mathring{v}}\cdot\overline{\nabla})\partial_{t}^{4}\mathring{\widetilde{\psi}}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\kappa^{-1}}

\displaystyle\varepsilon|\partial_{t}^{5}\psi|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{x^{\prime}}^{1}}^{2}+P(\mathring{K}_{0})\int_{0}^{T}\mathring{E}^{\kappa}(t)\,\mathrm{d}t.

It remains to analyze the last integral in (
5.66
) which will be canceled with
\mathring{I}_{0}
defined in (
5.65
). Following the analysis in (
4.130
)–(
4.140
), we have

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma}\mathring{\mathbf{Q}}\mathring{\mathcal{S}}_{1}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}=

\displaystyle~4\int_{\Sigma}\partial_{t}^{4}\check{q}\partial_{t}^{3}v\cdot\partial_{t}\mathring{\widetilde{N}}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}+\text{ controllable terms},

(5.71)

\displaystyle\mathring{I}_{0}=

\displaystyle-4\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}^{4}\check{q}\partial_{t}\mathring{\widetilde{\mathbf{N}}}_{i}\partial_{3}\partial_{t}^{3}v^{i}\,\mathrm{d}x+\text{ controllable terms},

(5.72)

and then we add them together and use the divergence theorem to get

\displaystyle 4\int_{\Sigma}\partial_{t}^{4}\check{q}\partial_{t}^{3}v\cdot\partial_{t}\mathring{\widetilde{N}}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}-4\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}^{4}\check{q}\partial_{t}\mathring{\widetilde{\mathbf{N}}}_{i}\partial_{3}\partial_{t}^{3}v^{i}\,\mathrm{d}x

(5.73)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{\Omega}(\partial_{t}^{3}\partial_{3}\check{q}\partial_{t}\mathring{\widetilde{\mathbf{N}}}+\partial_{t}^{3}\check{q}\partial_{t}\partial_{3}\mathring{\widetilde{\mathbf{N}}})\cdot\partial_{t}^{3}v\,\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}^{3}\partial_{3}\check{q}\partial_{t}(\partial_{t}\mathring{\widetilde{\mathbf{N}}}\cdot\partial_{t}^{3}v)+\partial_{t}^{3}\check{q}\partial_{t}(\partial_{t}\partial_{3}\mathring{\widetilde{\mathbf{N}}}\cdot\partial_{3}v)\,\mathrm{d}x,

whose time integral can be easily bounded by
\varepsilon\|\partial_{t}^{3}\partial_{3}\check{q}\|_{0}^{2}+\mathring{E}^{\kappa}(0)+P(\mathring{K}_{0})\int_{0}^{T}\mathring{E}^{\kappa}(t)\,\mathrm{d}t
. Hence, we get the control of boundary integral

-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Sigma}\mathring{\mathbf{Q}}(\mathring{\mathbf{V}}\cdot\mathring{\widetilde{N}})\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}\,\mathrm{d}t+\kappa^{2}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Sigma}\left|\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle\partial_{t}^{5}\widetilde{\psi}\right|_{0}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\leq\varepsilon\|\partial_{t}^{3}\partial_{3}\check{q}\|_{0}^{2}+\mathring{E}^{\kappa}(0)+P(\mathring{K}_{0})\int_{0}^{T}\mathring{E}^{\kappa}(t)\,\mathrm{d}t.

(5.74)

Combining this with (
5.64
), (
5.65
) and the definition of Alinhac good unknowns we get the estimates for the full-time derivatives

\|\partial_{t}^{4}v(t)\|_{0}^{2}+\left\|\sqrt{\mathring{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}(\mathring{q})}\partial_{t}^{4}\check{q}\right\|_{0}^{2}+\kappa^{2}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Sigma}\left|\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle\partial_{t}^{5}\widetilde{\psi}\right|_{0}^{2}\mathrm{d}\tau\leq\varepsilon\|\partial_{t}^{3}\partial_{3}\check{q}\|_{0}^{2}+\mathring{E}^{\kappa}(0)+P(\mathring{K}_{0})\int_{0}^{t}\mathring{E}^{\kappa}(t)\mathrm{d}\tau.

(5.75)

This, together with div-curl analysis gives us the energy inequality of
\mathring{E}^{\kappa}(t)
after choosing
\varepsilon>0
suitably small:

\mathring{E}^{\kappa}(t)\leq_{\kappa^{-1}}\mathring{E}^{\kappa}(0)+P(\mathring{K}_{0})\int_{0}^{t}\mathring{E}^{\kappa}(\tau)\mathrm{d}\tau,

(5.76)

which implies that there exists some
T^{\kappa}>0
such that

\sup_{0\leq t\leq T^{\kappa}}\mathring{E}^{\kappa}(t)\leq C(\kappa^{-1},\mathring{K}_{0})\mathring{E}^{\kappa}(0).

Therefore, the uniform-in-
n
estimates for (
5.6
) are proven by induction.

5.2.6
Regularity of
\psi
and its time derivatives

The regularity of
\partial_{t}^{k+1}\psi~(0\leq k\leq 3)
can be enhanced to
H^{5.5-k}
by the boundary elliptic estimates once we close the energy estimates for
\mathring{E}^{\kappa}(t)
. Note that the boundary condition gives

\kappa^{2}(1-\overline{\Delta}{})\partial_{t}\psi=\check{q}-g\mathring{\widetilde{\psi}}+\sigma\mathcal{H}(\overline{\nabla}\mathring{\widetilde{\psi}},\overline{\nabla}^{2}\mathring{\widetilde{\psi}}),

thus, by (
5.11
) and the elliptic estimate, it holds that

|\partial_{t}^{k+1}\psi|_{5.5-k}\leq\kappa^{-2}\left(\sigma P(|\overline{\nabla}\mathring{\widetilde{\psi}}|_{\infty})|\partial_{t}^{k}\overline{\nabla}^{2}\mathring{\widetilde{\psi}}|_{3.5-k}+|\partial_{t}^{k}q|_{3.5-k}+P(\mathring{K}_{0})\right)\leq C(\kappa^{-1},\mathring{K}_{0})\mathring{E}^{\kappa}.

(5.77)

Moreover,
|\psi|_{5.5}
is controlled by

|\psi|_{5.5}\leq|\psi_{0,\kappa}|_{5.5}+\int_{0}^{T}|\partial_{t}\psi(t)|_{5.5}\,\mathrm{d}t.

(5.78)

5.3
Picard iteration

So far, we have established the local existence and the uniform-in-
n
estimates for the linearized system (
5.1
) for each fixed
\kappa>0
, namely

\begin{cases}\rho^{(n)}D_{t}^{\widetilde{\varphi}^{(n)}}v^{(n+1)}+\nabla^{\widetilde{\varphi}^{(n)}}\check{q}^{(n+1)}=-(\rho^{(n)}-1)ge_{3}&~~~\text{in }[0,T]\times\Omega,\\
\mathcal{F}^{(n)^{\prime}}(q^{(n)})D_{t}^{\widetilde{\varphi}^{(n)}}\check{q}^{(n+1)}+\nabla^{\widetilde{\varphi}^{(n)}}\cdot v^{(n+1)}=\mathcal{F}^{(n)^{\prime}}(q^{(n)})gv_{3}^{(n)}&~~~\text{in }[0,T]\times\Omega,\\
q^{(n+1)}=q^{(n+1)}(\rho^{(n+1)}),\check{q}^{(n+1)}=q^{(n+1)}+g\widetilde{\varphi}^{(n)}&~~~\text{in }[0,T]\times\Omega,\\
\check{q}^{(n+1)}=g\widetilde{\psi}^{(n)}-\sigma\overline{\nabla}\cdot\left(\frac{\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}^{(n)}}{\sqrt{1+|\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}^{(n)}|^{2}}}\right)+\kappa^{2}(1-\overline{\Delta}{})(v^{(n+1)}\cdot\widetilde{N}^{(n)})&~~~\text{on }[0,T]\times\Sigma,\\
\partial_{t}\psi^{(n+1)}=v^{(n+1)}\cdot\widetilde{N}^{(n)}&~~~\text{on }[0,T]\times\Sigma,\\
v_{3}^{(n+1)}=0&~~~\text{on}~[0,T]\times\Sigma_{b},\\
(v^{(n+1)},\rho^{(n+1)},\psi^{(n+1)})|_{t=0}=(v_{0}^{\kappa},\rho_{0}^{\kappa},\psi_{0}^{\kappa}),\end{cases}

(5.79)

where
\psi^{(n)},\varphi^{(n)},D_{t}^{\widetilde{\varphi}^{(n)}},\nabla^{\widetilde{\varphi}^{(n)}}
are defined in (
5.2
)-(
5.5
). Now it suffices to prove that, for each fixed
\kappa>0
, the sequence
\{(v^{(n)},\check{q}^{(n)},\psi^{(n)})\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}
has a strongly convergent subsequence. Once we prove this, the limit of that subsequence becomes the solution to the nonlinear
\kappa
-approximate system (
3.11
) for this chosen
\kappa
.

For a function sequence
\{f^{(n)}\}
we define
[f]^{(n)}:=f^{(n+1)}-f^{(n)}
and then we find that
\{([v]^{(n)},[\check{q}]^{(n)},[\psi]^{(n)})\}
satisfies the following linear system

\begin{cases}\rho^{(n)}D_{t}^{\widetilde{\varphi}^{(n)}}[v]^{(n)}+\nabla^{\widetilde{\varphi}^{(n)}}[\check{q}]^{(n)}=-\mathring{f}_{v}^{(n)}&~~~\text{in }[0,T]\times\Omega,\\
\mathcal{F}^{(n)^{\prime}}(q^{(n)})D_{t}^{\widetilde{\varphi}^{(n)}}[\check{q}]^{(n)}+\nabla^{\widetilde{\varphi}^{(n)}}\cdot[v]^{(n)}=-\mathring{f}_{q}^{(n)}&~~~\text{in }[0,T]\times\Omega,\\
[\check{q}]^{(n)}=[q]^{(n)}+g[\widetilde{\varphi}]^{(n-1)}&~~~\text{in }[0,T]\times\Omega,\\
[\check{q}]^{(n)}=g[\widetilde{\psi}]^{(n-1)}-\sigma[\mathcal{H}]^{{(n-1)}}+\kappa^{2}(1-\overline{\Delta}{})([v]^{(n)}\cdot\widetilde{N}^{(n)})+\kappa^{2}(1-\overline{\Delta}{})(v^{(n)}\cdot[\widetilde{N}]^{(n-1)}),&~~~\text{on }[0,T]\times\Sigma,\\
\partial_{t}[\psi]^{(n)}=[v]^{(n)}\cdot\widetilde{N}^{(n)}+(v^{(n)}\cdot[\widetilde{N}]^{(n-1)}),&~~~\text{on }[0,T]\times\Sigma,\\
[v_{3}^{(n)}]=0&~~~\text{on}~[0,T]\times\Sigma_{b},\\
([v]^{(n)},[\rho]^{(n)},[\psi]^{(n)})|_{t=0}=(\mathbf{0},0,0),\end{cases}

(5.80)

where
\mathring{f}_{v}^{(n)}
and
\mathring{f}_{q}^{(n)}
are defined by

\displaystyle\mathring{f}_{v}^{(n)}:=

\displaystyle~[\rho]^{(n-1)}\partial_{t}v^{(n)}+[\rho\overline{v}]^{(n-1)}\cdot\overline{\nabla}v^{(n)}+[\rho V_{\mathring{\mathbf{N}}}]^{(n-1)}\partial_{3}v^{(n)}+[\rho]^{(n-1)}ge_{3}+\partial_{3}\check{q}^{(n)}[A_{i3}]^{(n-1)},

(5.81)

\displaystyle\mathring{f}_{q}^{(n)}:=

\displaystyle~[\mathcal{F}^{\prime}(q)]^{(n-1)}(\partial_{t}\check{q}^{(n)}-gv_{3}^{(n-1)})+[\mathcal{F}^{\prime}(q)\overline{v}]^{(n-1)}\cdot\overline{\nabla}\check{q}^{(n)}+[\mathcal{F}^{\prime}(q)V_{\mathring{\mathbf{N}}}]^{(n-1)}\partial_{3}\check{q}^{(n)}

(5.82)

\displaystyle-\mathcal{F}^{(n)^{\prime}}(q^{(n)})g[v_{3}]^{(n-1)}+\partial_{3}v_{i}^{(n)}[A_{i3}]^{(n-1)},

and

\displaystyle V_{\mathring{\mathbf{N}}}^{(n)}:=~\frac{1}{\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}^{(n)}}(v^{(n)}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{N}}^{(n-1)}-\partial_{t}\varphi^{(n)}),~A^{(n)}_{13}:=-\frac{\partial_{1}\widetilde{\varphi}^{(n)}}{\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}^{(n)}},~A^{(n)}_{23}:=-\frac{\partial_{2}\widetilde{\varphi}^{(n)}}{\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}^{(n)}},~A^{(n)}_{33}:=\frac{1}{\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}^{(n)}},

\displaystyle[\mathcal{H}]^{(n-1)}:=\mathcal{H}(\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}^{(n)})-\mathcal{H}(\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}^{(n-1)}),~\mathcal{H}(\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}):=-\overline{\nabla}\cdot\left(\frac{\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}}{1+|\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}|^{2}}\right).

For
n\geq 1
, we define the energy of (
5.80
)
[E]^{(n)}
to be the following quantity

\displaystyle{}^{(n)}(t):=\sum_{k=0}^{3}\|\partial_{t}^{k}[v]^{(n)}(t)\|_{3-k}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}^{k}[\check{q}]^{(n)}(t)\|_{3-k}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\left|\partial_{t}^{k+1}[\psi]^{(n)}(\tau)\right|_{4-k}^{2}\mathrm{d}\tau+|[\psi]^{(n)}(t)|_{4}^{2}

(5.83)

It suffices to control
[E]^{(n)}(t)
and use
([v]^{(n)},[\rho]^{(n)},[\psi]^{(n)})|_{t=0}=(\mathbf{0},0,0)
to show that
[E]^{(n)}(t)\leq\frac{1}{4}([E]^{(n-1)}(t)+[E]^{(n-2)}(t))
in some time interval
[0,T^{\kappa}_{1}]
. The estimates for
[E]^{(n)}(t)
are parallel to Section
5.2
, so we will not go into every detail but only list the sketch of the proof.

5.3.1
Div-curl analysis for
[v]^{(n)}

By Lemma
5.2
, we have the following inequalities for
k=0,1,2
:

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}^{k}[v]^{(n)}\|_{3-k}^{2}\leq

\displaystyle~C(\mathring{K}_{0})\left(\|\partial_{t}^{k}[v]^{(n)}\|_{0}^{2}+\|\nabla^{\widetilde{\varphi}^{(n)}}\times\partial_{t}^{k}[v]^{(n)}\|_{2-k}^{2}+\|\nabla^{\widetilde{\varphi}^{(n)}}\cdot\partial_{t}^{k}[v]^{(n)}\|_{2-k}^{2}+|\partial_{t}^{k}[v]^{(n)}\cdot\widetilde{N}^{(n)}|_{2.5-k}^{2}\right).

(5.84)

The estimates for
L^{2}(\Omega)
norms follow in the same way as Section
5.2.1
so we do not repeat here. For the curl part, we take
\nabla^{\widetilde{\varphi}^{(n)}}\times
in the first equation of (
5.80
) to get

\rho^{(n)}D_{t}^{\widetilde{\varphi}^{(n)}}(\nabla^{\widetilde{\varphi}^{(n)}}\times[v]^{(n)})=-\nabla^{\widetilde{\varphi}^{(n)}}\times\mathring{f}_{v}^{(n)}-\nabla^{\widetilde{\varphi}^{(n)}}\rho^{(n)}\times D_{t}^{\widetilde{\varphi}^{(n)}}[v]^{(n)}+\rho^{(n)}[\nabla^{\widetilde{\varphi}^{(n)}}\times,D_{t}^{\widetilde{\varphi}^{(n)}}][v]^{(n)},

(5.85)

where
([\nabla^{\widetilde{\varphi}^{(n)}}\times,D_{t}^{\widetilde{\varphi}^{(n)}}][v]^{(n)})^{i}=\epsilon^{ijk}\nabla_{j}^{\widetilde{\varphi}^{(n)}}v_{l}^{(n)}\nabla_{l}^{\widetilde{\varphi}^{(n)}}[v]_{k}^{(n)}+\epsilon^{ijk}\nabla_{j}^{\widetilde{\varphi}^{(n)}}\partial_{t}(\widetilde{\varphi}^{(n)}-\widetilde{\varphi}^{(n-1)})\partial_{3}[v]_{k}^{(n)}
and
\nabla^{\widetilde{\varphi}^{(n)}}\times\mathring{f}_{v}^{(n)}
contains at most two derivatives of
v^{(n)},\varphi^{(n)},\varphi^{(n-1)},\varphi^{(n-2)}
. Taking
\partial^{2}
, we have

\rho^{(n)}D_{t}^{\widetilde{\varphi}^{(n)}}(\partial^{2}\nabla^{\widetilde{\varphi}^{(n)}}\times[v]^{(n)})=\partial^{2}(\text{RHS of }\eqref{curllv eq0})-[\partial^{2},\rho^{(n)}D_{t}^{\widetilde{\varphi}^{(n)}}](\nabla^{\widetilde{\varphi}^{(n)}}\times[v]^{(n)}).

(5.86)

Based on the analysis above, we find that the leading-order terms of
[v]^{(n)},[v]^{(n-1)}
must be linear in
[v]^{(n)},[v]^{(n-1)}
respectively. Using Reynold transport formula (
A.9
) for the linearized system, the curl part can be directly controlled as in (
5.33
):

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{\widetilde{\varphi}^{(n)}}\times[v]^{(n)}(T)\|_{2}^{2}\leq

\displaystyle~C(\mathring{K}_{0})\left(\|\underbrace{\nabla^{\widetilde{\varphi}^{(n)}}\times[v]^{(n)}(0)}_{=0}\|_{2}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}P(\mathring{E}^{(n)},\mathring{E}^{(n-1)},\mathring{E}^{(n-2)})[E]^{(n)}(t)\,\mathrm{d}t\right)

(5.87)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~C(\mathring{K}_{0})\int_{0}^{T}[E]^{(n)}(t)+[\mathring{E}]^{(n-1)}(t)+[\mathring{E}]^{(n-2)}(t)\,\mathrm{d}t.

Similarly, replacing
\partial^{2}
by
\partial^{2-k}\partial_{t}^{k}
for
k=1,2
, we get

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{\widetilde{\varphi}^{(n)}}\times\partial_{t}^{k}[v]^{(n)}(T)\|_{2-k}^{2}\leq~C(\mathring{K}_{0})\int_{0}^{T}[E]^{(n)}(t)+[\mathring{E}]^{(n-1)}(t)+[\mathring{E}]^{(n-2)}(t)\,\mathrm{d}t.

(5.88)

As for the divergence, the second equation in (
5.80
) gives

\|\nabla^{\widetilde{\varphi}^{(n)}}\cdot[v]^{(n)}\|_{2}^{2}\leq\|\mathcal{F}^{(n)^{\prime}}(q^{(n)})D_{t}^{\widetilde{\varphi}^{(n)}}[\check{q}]^{(n)}\|_{2}^{2}+\|\mathring{f}_{q}\|_{2}^{2}\leq P(\mathring{K}_{0})\|\mathcal{F}^{(n)^{\prime}}(q^{(n)})\mathcal{T}[\check{q}]^{(n)}\|_{2}^{2},

(5.89)

where
\mathcal{T}=\partial_{t}
or
\overline{\partial}{}
or
\omega\partial_{3}
for a bounded weight function
\omega
vanishing on
\Sigma
. Therefore, the divergence is then reduced to the tangential derivatives of
[\check{q}]
. Similarly, the divergence of
\partial_{t}^{k}[v]^{(n)}
is reduced to
\partial_{t}^{k}\mathcal{T}\check{q}
.

Next, the normal traces are still controlled by using boundary elliptic estimates. Note that the Dirichlet boundary condition for
[\check{q}]^{(n)}
can be written as

-\kappa^{2}(1-\overline{\Delta}{})([v]^{(n)}\cdot\widetilde{N}^{(n)})=-[q]^{(n)}-\sigma\left(\mathcal{H}(\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}^{(n)})-\mathcal{H}(\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}^{(n-1)})\right)+\kappa^{2}(1-\overline{\Delta}{})(v^{(n)}\cdot[\widetilde{N}]^{(n-1)}),

(5.90)

and thus

|[v]^{(n)}\cdot\widetilde{N}^{(n)}|_{2.5}^{2}\leq_{\kappa^{-1}}\|[q]^{(n)}\|_{1}^{2}+P(\mathring{K}_{0})+|\overline{v}^{(n)}\cdot\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}^{(n-1)}|_{2.5}^{2}+|v_{3}^{(n)}|_{2.5}^{2}\leq\|[q]^{(n)}\|_{1}^{2}+P(\mathring{K}_{0}).

(5.91)

Similarly, we have for
k=1,2

|\partial_{t}^{k}[v]^{(n)}\cdot\widetilde{N}^{(n)}|_{2.5-k}^{2}\leq_{\kappa^{-1}}\|\partial_{t}^{k}[q]^{(n)}\|_{1}^{2}+P(\mathring{K}_{0}).

(5.92)

5.3.2
Reduction of pressure
[\check{q}]^{(n)}

This is similar to the arguments in Section
5.2.4
. We first consider the third component of the first equation in (
5.80
):

(\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}^{(n)})^{-1}\partial_{3}[\check{q}]^{(n)}=-\rho^{(n)}D_{t}^{\widetilde{\varphi}^{(n)}}[v]^{(n)}+\mathring{f}_{v}^{(n)},

(5.93)

which means the control of
\partial_{3}[\check{q}]^{(n)}
is reduced to
\mathcal{T}[v]^{(n)}
. Then by considering the first and second components, we can further reduce the control of
\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\check{q}~(i=1,2)
to
\partial_{3}\check{q}
and
\mathcal{T}v
since
\nabla^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}_{i}=\overline{\partial}{}_{i}-\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}\partial^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}_{3}
. Therefore, combining the div-curl analysis and reduction procedures for
[\check{q}]^{(n)}
, it suffices to control
\partial_{t}^{2}\overline{\partial}{}[\check{q}]^{(n)}
and
\partial_{t}^{3}[\check{q}]^{(n)}
.

5.3.3
Tangential estimates for full-time derivatives

Again we only show the control of
\partial_{t}^{3}[\check{q}]^{(n)}
by introducing the Alinhac good unknowns:

\displaystyle[\mathbf{V}]^{(n)}:=\mathring{\mathbf{V}}^{(n+1)}-\mathring{\mathbf{V}}^{(n)}=\partial_{t}^{3}[v]^{(n)}-\partial_{t}^{3}\widetilde{\varphi}^{(n)}\partial_{3}^{\widetilde{\varphi}^{(n)}}[v]^{(n)}-\partial_{t}^{3}\widetilde{\varphi}^{(n)}\partial_{3}^{[\widetilde{\varphi}]^{(n-1)}}v^{(n)}-\partial_{t}^{3}[\widetilde{\varphi}]^{(n-1)}\partial_{3}^{\widetilde{\varphi}^{(n-1)}}v^{(n)},

(5.94)

\displaystyle[\mathbf{Q}]^{(n)}:=\mathring{\mathbf{Q}}^{(n+1)}-\mathring{\mathbf{Q}}^{(n)}=\partial_{t}^{3}[\check{q}]^{(n)}-\partial_{t}^{3}\widetilde{\varphi}^{(n)}\partial_{3}^{\widetilde{\varphi}^{(n)}}[\check{q}]^{(n)}-\partial_{t}^{3}\widetilde{\varphi}^{(n)}\partial_{3}^{[\widetilde{\varphi}]^{(n-1)}}\check{q}^{(n)}-\partial_{t}^{3}[\widetilde{\varphi}]^{(n-1)}\partial_{3}^{\widetilde{\varphi}^{(n-1)}}\check{q}^{(n)}.

(5.95)

For a function
f
and its associated Alinhac good unknown
\mathbf{F}
, we have

\displaystyle\partial_{t}^{3}(\partial_{i}^{\widetilde{\varphi}^{(n)}}[f]^{(n)}+\partial_{i}^{[\widetilde{\varphi}]^{(n-1)}}f^{(n)})=

\displaystyle~\partial_{i}^{\widetilde{\varphi}^{(n)}}[\mathbf{F}]^{(n)}+[\mathfrak{C}]_{i}^{(n)}(f),

\displaystyle\partial_{t}^{3}(D_{t}^{\widetilde{\varphi}^{(n)}}[f]^{(n)}+D_{t}^{[\widetilde{\varphi}]^{(n-1)}}f^{(n)})=

\displaystyle~D_{t}^{\widetilde{\varphi}^{(n)}}[\mathbf{F}]^{(n)}+[\mathfrak{D}]^{(n)}(f)+[\mathfrak{e}]^{(n)}(f)

with

\displaystyle[\mathfrak{C}]_{i}^{(n)}(f)=\mathfrak{C}_{i}^{(n)}(f^{(n+1)})-\mathfrak{C}_{i}^{(n-1)}(f^{(n)})+\text{lower-order controllable terms},

\displaystyle[\mathfrak{D}]_{i}^{(n)}(f)=\mathfrak{D}^{(n)}(f^{(n+1)})-\mathfrak{D}^{(n-1)}(f^{(n)})+\text{lower-order controllable terms},

\displaystyle[\mathfrak{e}]_{i}^{(n)}(f)=\mathfrak{e}^{(n)}(f^{(n+1)})-\mathfrak{e}^{(n-1)}(f^{(n)})+\text{lower-order controllable terms},

where
\mathfrak{C}_{i}^{(n)}(f^{(m)}),\mathfrak{D}^{(n)}(f^{(m)})
and
\mathfrak{e}^{(n)}(f^{(m)})
are defined by replacing
\partial_{t}^{4},\partial_{t}^{5}
by
\partial_{t}^{3},\partial_{t}^{4}
, replacing the coefficient 4 in
\mathring{\mathfrak{D}}
by 3 and setting
\mathring{\varphi}=\varphi^{(n)}
,
\dot{\varphi}=\varphi^{(n-1)}
,
f^{(n+1)}=f
and
f^{(n)}=\mathring{f}
in (
5.48
)-(
5.56
).

The Alinhac good unknowns
[\mathbf{V}]^{(n)},[\mathbf{Q}]^{(n)}
satisfy the following linear system:

\displaystyle\rho^{(n)}D_{t}^{\widetilde{\varphi}^{(n)}}[\mathbf{V}]^{(n)}+\nabla^{\widetilde{\varphi}^{(n)}}[\mathbf{Q}]^{(n)}

\displaystyle=-\mathfrak{C}^{(n)}(\check{q}^{(n+1)})+\mathfrak{C}^{(n-1)}(\check{q}^{(n)})+[\mathring{\mathcal{R}}]_{v},

(5.96)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{{(n)}^{\prime}}(q^{(n)})D_{t}^{\widetilde{\varphi}^{(n)}}[\mathbf{Q}]^{(n)}+\nabla^{\widetilde{\varphi}^{(n)}}\cdot\mathbf{V}^{(n)}

\displaystyle=-\mathfrak{C}_{i}^{(n)}(v_{i}^{(n+1)})+\mathfrak{C}_{i}^{(n-1)}(v_{i}^{(n)})+[\mathring{\mathcal{R}}]_{q},

(5.97)

where
[R]
terms consist of
\partial_{t}^{3}\mathring{f}
terms in (
5.80
) and the omitted commutator terms in the definition of Alinhac good unknowns
[\mathbf{V}],[\mathbf{Q}]
and they are controllable in
L^{2}(\Omega)
.

\displaystyle\|[\mathring{\mathcal{R}}]\|_{0}^{2}\leq C(\mathring{K}_{0})([E]^{(n)}(t)+[E]^{(n-1)}(t)+[E]^{(n-2)}(t)).

(5.98)

The boundary conditions now become:

\displaystyle[\mathbf{Q}]^{(n)}

\displaystyle=g\partial_{t}^{3}[\widetilde{\psi}]^{(n-1)}+\sigma\partial_{t}^{3}(\mathcal{H}(\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}^{(n)})-\mathcal{H}^{(}\overline{\nabla}\widetilde{\psi}^{(n-1)}))-\kappa^{2}(1-\overline{\Delta}{})\partial_{t}^{4}[\psi]^{(n)}

\displaystyle+\partial_{t}^{3}\widetilde{\psi}^{(n)}\partial_{3}[\check{q}]^{(n)}+\partial_{t}^{3}[\widetilde{\psi}]^{(n-1)}\partial_{3}\check{q}^{(n)}

(5.99)

\displaystyle[\mathbf{V}]^{(n)}\cdot\widetilde{N}^{(n)}

\displaystyle=\partial_{t}^{4}[\psi]^{(n)}+[\overline{v}]^{(n)}\cdot\overline{\nabla}\partial_{t}^{3}\widetilde{\psi}^{(n)}+(\overline{v}\cdot\overline{\nabla})\partial_{t}^{3}[\widetilde{\psi}]^{(n-1)}+\partial_{t}^{3}\overline{v}^{(n)}\cdot\overline{\nabla}[\widetilde{\psi}]^{(n-1)}

\displaystyle-(\partial_{3}[v]^{(n)}\cdot\widetilde{N}^{(n)})\partial_{t}^{3}\widetilde{\psi}^{(n)}+(\partial_{3}v^{(n)}\cdot\widetilde{N}^{(n)})\partial_{t}^{3}[\widetilde{\psi}]^{(n-1)}+[\partial_{t}^{3},\widetilde{N}^{(n)}\cdot,v^{(n+1)}]-[\partial_{t}^{3},\widetilde{N}^{(n-1)}\cdot,v^{(n)}].

(5.100)

Following the analysis in Section
5.2.5
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left(\int_{\Omega}\rho^{(n)}|[\mathbf{V}]^{(n)}|^{2}\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}^{(n)}\,\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Omega}\mathcal{F}^{{(n)}^{\prime}}(q^{(n)})|[\mathbf{Q}]^{(n)}|^{2}\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}^{(n)}\,\mathrm{d}x\right)+\kappa^{2}\int_{0}^{T}\left|\partial_{t}^{4}[\psi]^{(n)}\right|_{1}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t

(5.101)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(\mathring{K}_{0})\left([\mathring{E}]^{(n)}(0)+\int_{0}^{T}[\mathring{E}]^{(n)}(t)+[\mathring{E}]^{(n-1)}(t)+[\mathring{E}]^{(n-2)}(t)\,\mathrm{d}t\right)

\displaystyle-\int_{\Sigma}[\mathring{\mathbf{Q}}]^{(n)}[\partial_{t}^{3},\widetilde{\mathbf{N}}^{\cdot},v^{(n-1)}]\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}+\int_{\Omega}[\mathring{\mathbf{Q}}]^{(n)}\mathfrak{C}_{i}^{(n-1)}(v_{i}^{(n)})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}^{(n)}

where the last line is analyzed in the same way as in (
5.72
