\displaystyle~|\widehat{\partial_{t}^{2}\mathfrak{H}}|_{L^{2}}|\langle\eta\rangle^{-1-\delta}|_{L_{\eta}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}|\langle\eta\rangle^{2+\delta}\hat{\psi}(\eta)|_{L_{\eta}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle~C(|\overline{\nabla}\psi,\overline{\nabla}\psi_{t}|_{L^{\infty}})|\overline{\nabla}\psi_{tt}|_{0}|\psi|_{2+\delta}\lesssim P(\mathfrak{E}_{4}(t)).

(7.78)

The last step is to control
\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}(\partial_{t}^{2}R_{\mathfrak{H}})
and it suffices to control
|\partial_{t}^{2}R_{\mathfrak{H}}|_{1}
. This step is actually a refinement of
[
2
, Lemma 3.25]
. Recall that the mean curvature is given by
\mathcal{H}=-\overline{\nabla}\cdot F(\overline{\nabla}\psi)
with
F(x):=\frac{x}{\sqrt{1+x^{2}}}
and
F(0)=0
. We expand
F
into 2nd-order term to get

F(a)=0+F^{\prime}(a)a+\frac{F^{\prime\prime}(\zeta)}{2}a^{2}=T_{F^{\prime}(a)}a+T_{a}(F^{\prime}(a))+R(a,F^{\prime}(a))+\frac{F^{\prime\prime}(\zeta)}{2}a^{2}.

Let
a=\overline{\nabla}\eta
and then
T_{F^{\prime}(a)}a
is exactly the term
T_{\mathfrak{H}}\psi
defined in Lemma
7.9
and

\displaystyle R_{\mathfrak{H}}=

\displaystyle T_{\overline{\nabla}\psi}\left(\frac{Id}{\sqrt{1+|\overline{\nabla}\psi|^{2}}}-\frac{\overline{\nabla}\psi\otimes\overline{\nabla}\psi}{(\sqrt{1+|\overline{\nabla}\psi|^{2}})^{3}}\right)+R(\overline{\nabla}\psi,F^{\prime}(\overline{\nabla}\psi))+(\overline{\nabla}\psi)^{\top}\cdot\mathbf{M}(\overline{\nabla}\psi)\cdot(\overline{\nabla}\psi)

where
\mathbf{M}(\overline{\nabla}\psi)
is a
2\times 2
matrix depending on
\overline{\nabla}\psi
. Thus, the leading-order part in the last two terms of
\partial_{t}^{2}R_{\mathfrak{H}}
must have the form
(\overline{\nabla}\psi_{tt}+\overline{\nabla}\psi_{t}\cdot\overline{\nabla}\psi_{t})C^{\prime}(\overline{\nabla}\psi)
for some continuous function
C^{\prime}(\cdot)
, while the first term is controlled by either using Lemma
D.1
or following the same way as in the control of
[\partial_{t}^{2},T_{\mathfrak{H}}]\psi
. We conclude the result as follows

\displaystyle\sigma|\partial_{t}^{2}R_{\mathfrak{H}}|_{0}\lesssim C(|\overline{\nabla}\psi,\overline{\nabla}\psi_{t}|_{L^{\infty}})(|\sigma\overline{\nabla}\psi_{tt}|_{L^{2}}+|\sqrt{\sigma}\overline{\nabla}\psi_{t}|_{L^{\infty}}|\sqrt{\sigma}\overline{\nabla}\psi_{t}|_{0})\leq P(\mathfrak{E}_{4}(t)).

(7.79)

Now, we have finished all estimates and the proof of improved incompressible limit ends here.

Appendix A
The Reynold transport theorems

Below, the formulas involving
\widetilde{\varphi},\widetilde{\psi}
are used for the nonlinear
\kappa
-problem (
3.11
) and the formulas involving
\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}},\mathring{\widetilde{\psi}}
are used for the linearized
\kappa
-problem (
5.6
).

Lemma A.1
.

Let
f,g
be smooth functions defined on
[0,T]\times\Omega
. Then:

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\Omega}fg\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}\,dx=\int_{\Omega}(\partial^{\widetilde{\varphi}}_{t}f)g\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}\,dx+\int_{\Omega}f(\partial^{\widetilde{\varphi}}_{t}g)\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}\,dx+\int_{x_{3}=0}fg\partial_{t}\psi\,dx^{\prime}+\int_{\Omega}fg\partial_{3}\partial_{t}(\widetilde{\varphi}-\varphi)\,dx,

(A.1)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\Omega}fg\partial_{3}\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}\,dx=\int_{\Omega}(\partial^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}_{t}f)g\partial_{3}\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}\,dx+\int_{\Omega}f(\partial^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}_{t}g)\partial_{3}\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}\,dx+\int_{x_{3}=0}fg\partial_{t}\mathring{\psi}\,dx^{\prime}+\int_{\Omega}fg\partial_{3}\partial_{t}(\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}-\mathring{\varphi})\,dx.

(A.2)

Proof.

In view of (
3.12
),

LHS of
(
A.1
)

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega}(\partial_{t}f)g\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}\,\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Omega}f(\partial_{t}g)\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}\,\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Omega}fg\partial_{3}\partial_{t}\widetilde{\varphi}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega}fg\partial_{3}\partial_{t}\widetilde{\varphi}\,\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Omega}(\partial^{\widetilde{\varphi}}_{t}f)g\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}\,\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Omega}f(\partial^{\widetilde{\varphi}}_{t}g)\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}\,\mathrm{d}x+\overbrace{\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}\varphi(\partial_{3}f)g\,\mathrm{d}x}^{i}+\overbrace{\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}\varphi(\partial_{3}g)f\,\mathrm{d}x}^{ii}.

Integrating
\partial_{3}
in
ii
by parts, we have

\displaystyle ii=\int_{x_{3}=0}fg\partial_{t}\psi\,dx^{\prime}-\int_{x_{3}=-b}fg\underbrace{\partial_{t}\varphi}_{=\partial_{t}(-b)=0}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}-\int_{\Omega}fg\partial_{3}\partial_{t}\varphi\,\mathrm{d}x-i.

This concludes the proof of (
A.1
). Moreover, in light of (
5.8
),

LHS of
(
A.2
)

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega}(\partial_{t}f)g\partial_{3}\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}\,\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Omega}f(\partial_{t}g)\partial_{3}\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}\,\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Omega}fg\partial_{3}\partial_{t}\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega}fg\partial_{3}\partial_{t}\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}\,\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Omega}(\partial^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}_{t}f)g\partial_{3}\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}\,\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Omega}f(\partial^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}_{t}g)\partial_{3}\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}\,\mathrm{d}x+\overbrace{\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}\mathring{\varphi}(\partial_{3}f)g\,\mathrm{d}x}^{\mathring{i}}+\overbrace{\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}\mathring{\varphi}(\partial_{3}g)f\,\mathrm{d}x}^{\mathring{ii}}.

Integrating
\partial_{3}
in
\mathring{ii}
by parts, we have

\displaystyle\mathring{ii}=\int_{x_{3}=0}fg\partial_{t}\mathring{\psi}\,dx^{\prime}-\int_{\Omega}fg\partial_{3}\partial_{t}\mathring{\varphi}\,\mathrm{d}x-\mathring{i},

and thus (
A.2
) follows.
∎

Lemma A.2

(
Integration by parts for covariant derivatives
)
.

Let
f,g
be defined as in Lemma
A.1
. Then:

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}(\partial^{\widetilde{\varphi}}_{i}f)g\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}\,\mathrm{d}x=-\int_{\Omega}f(\partial^{\widetilde{\varphi}}_{i}g)\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}\,\mathrm{d}x+\int_{x_{3}=0}fg\widetilde{N}_{i}\,dx^{\prime},

(A.3)

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}(\partial^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}_{i}f)g\partial_{3}\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}\,\mathrm{d}x=-\int_{\Omega}f(\partial^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}_{i}g)\partial_{3}\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}\,\mathrm{d}x+\int_{x_{3}=0}fg\mathring{\widetilde{N}}_{i}\,dx^{\prime}.

(A.4)

Proof.

(
A.3
) follows from the fact that
\partial^{\widetilde{\varphi}}_{i}
is the covariant spatial derivative and
\partial_{3}\widetilde{\psi}\,\mathrm{d}x
is the associated volume element. (
A.4
) follows from a parallel argument.
∎

Let
D_{t}^{\widetilde{\varphi}}
be the smoothed material derivative defined in (
3.15
). Then the following theorem holds.

Theorem A.3

(
Reynold transport theorem for nonlinear
\kappa
-problem
)
.

Let
f
be a smooth function defined on
[0,T]\times\Omega
. Then:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\Omega}\rho|f|^{2}\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}\,\mathrm{d}x=\int_{\Omega}\rho(D_{t}^{\widetilde{\varphi}}f)f\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}\,\mathrm{d}x+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega}\rho|f|^{2}\partial_{3}\partial_{t}(\widetilde{\varphi}-\varphi)\,\mathrm{d}x.

(A.5)

Proof.

First, we express

\int_{\Omega}\rho(D_{t}^{\widetilde{\varphi}}f)f\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}\,\mathrm{d}x=\int_{\Omega}\rho(\partial^{\widetilde{\varphi}}_{t}f)f\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}\,\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Omega}\rho(v\cdot\nabla^{\widetilde{\varphi}}f)f\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}\,\mathrm{d}x.

Invoking (
A.1
), we have

\int_{\Omega}\rho(\partial^{\widetilde{\varphi}}_{t}f)f\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}\,\mathrm{d}x=\partial_{t}\int_{\Omega}\rho|f|^{2}\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}\,\mathrm{d}x-\int_{\Omega}\partial^{\widetilde{\varphi}}_{t}(\rho f)f\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}\,\mathrm{d}x-\int_{x_{3}=0}\rho|f|^{2}\partial_{t}\psi\,dx^{\prime}-\int_{\Omega}\rho|f|^{2}\partial_{3}\partial_{t}(\widetilde{\varphi}-\varphi)\,\mathrm{d}x,

and this indicates that

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\rho(\partial^{\widetilde{\varphi}}_{t}f)f\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}\,\mathrm{d}x=\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\Omega}\rho|f|^{2}\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}\,\mathrm{d}x\overbrace{-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega}(\partial^{\widetilde{\varphi}}_{t}\rho)|f|^{2}\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}\,\mathrm{d}x}^{A}\overbrace{-\frac{1}{2}\int_{x_{3}=0}\rho|f|^{2}\partial_{t}\psi\,dx^{\prime}}^{C}-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega}\rho|f|^{2}\partial_{3}\partial_{t}(\widetilde{\varphi}-\varphi)\,\mathrm{d}x.

(A.6)

Furthermore, invoking (
A.3
), we have

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\rho(v\cdot\nabla^{\widetilde{\varphi}}f)f\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}\,\mathrm{d}x=\int_{\Omega}\nabla^{\widetilde{\varphi}}\cdot(\rho vf)f\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}\,\mathrm{d}x-\int_{\Omega}\nabla^{\widetilde{\varphi}}\cdot(\rho v)|f|^{2}\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=-\int_{\Omega}\rho f(v\cdot\nabla^{\widetilde{\varphi}}f)\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}\,\mathrm{d}x+\int_{x_{3}=0}\rho|f|^{2}v\cdot\widetilde{N}\,dx^{\prime}-\int_{\Omega}\nabla^{\widetilde{\varphi}}\cdot(\rho v)|f|^{2}\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}\,\mathrm{d}x,

and thus

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\rho(v\cdot\nabla^{\widetilde{\varphi}}f)f\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}\,\mathrm{d}x=\overbrace{\frac{1}{2}\int_{x_{3}=0}\rho|f|^{2}v\cdot\widetilde{N}\,dx^{\prime}}^{D}\overbrace{-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega}\nabla^{\widetilde{\varphi}}\cdot(\rho v)|f|^{2}\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}\,\mathrm{d}x}^{B}.

(A.7)

We have
A+B=C+D=0
thanks to the second and fifth equations of (
3.11
), respectively. Hence, (
A.5
) follows after adding (
A.6
) and (
A.7
) up.
∎

Theorem
A.3
leads to the following two corollaries. The first one records the integration by parts formula for
D_{t}^{\widetilde{\varphi}}
.

Corollary A.4

(
Reynold transport theorem for nonlinear
\kappa
-problem
)
.

It holds that

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\Omega}fg\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}\,\mathrm{d}x=\int_{\Omega}(D_{t}^{\widetilde{\varphi}}f)g\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}\,\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Omega}f(D_{t}^{\widetilde{\varphi}}g)\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}\,\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Omega}(\nabla^{\widetilde{\varphi}}\cdot v)fg\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}\,\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Omega}fg\partial_{3}\partial_{t}(\widetilde{\varphi}-\varphi)\,\mathrm{d}x.

(A.8)

Proof.

Given (
A.1
), we have

\int_{\Omega}(\partial^{\widetilde{\varphi}}_{t}f)g\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}\,\mathrm{d}x=\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\Omega}fg\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}\,\mathrm{d}x-\int_{\Omega}f(\partial^{\widetilde{\varphi}}_{t}g)\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}\,\mathrm{d}x-\int_{x_{3}=0}fg\partial_{t}\psi\,dx^{\prime}-\int_{\Omega}fg\partial_{3}\partial_{t}(\widetilde{\varphi}-\varphi)\,\mathrm{d}x,

Also, (
A.3
) yields

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}(v\cdot\nabla^{\widetilde{\varphi}}f)g\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}\,\mathrm{d}x=\int_{\Omega}\nabla^{\widetilde{\varphi}}\cdot(vf)g\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}\,\mathrm{d}x-\int_{\Omega}(\nabla^{\widetilde{\varphi}}\cdot v)fg\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=-\int_{\Omega}f(v\cdot\nabla^{\widetilde{\varphi}}g)\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}\,\mathrm{d}x+\int_{x_{3}=0}fg(v\cdot\widetilde{N})\,dx^{\prime}-\int_{\Omega}(\nabla^{\widetilde{\varphi}}\cdot v)fg\partial_{3}\widetilde{\varphi}\,\mathrm{d}x.

Then we obtain (
A.8
) by adding these up.
∎

The second corollary concerns the transport theorem as well as the integration by parts formula for the linearized material derivative
D_{t}^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}
, defined in (
5.7
).

Corollary A.5

(
Reynold transport theorem for linearized
\kappa
-problem
)
.

Let
D_{t}^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}:=\partial_{t}+(\overline{\mathring{v}}\cdot\overline{\nabla})+\frac{1}{\partial_{3}\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}(\mathring{v}\cdot\dot{\widetilde{\mathbf{N}}}-\partial_{t}\mathring{\varphi})\partial_{3}
be the linearized material derivative defined in (
5.7
). Then:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\Omega}\mathring{\rho}|f|^{2}\partial_{3}\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}\,\mathrm{d}x=

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\mathring{\rho}(D_{t}^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}f)f\partial_{3}\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}\,\mathrm{d}x+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega}\left(D_{t}^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}\mathring{\rho}+\mathring{\rho}\nabla^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}\cdot\mathring{v}\right)|f|^{2}\partial_{3}\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}\,dx

(A.9)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega}\mathring{\rho}|f|^{2}\left(\partial_{3}\partial_{t}(\dot{\widetilde{\varphi}}-\mathring{\varphi})+\partial_{3}(\partial_{t}+\overline{\mathring{v}}\cdot\overline{\nabla})(\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}-\dot{\widetilde{\varphi}})\right)\,\mathrm{d}x.

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\Omega}|f|^{2}\partial_{3}\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}\,\mathrm{d}x=

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}(D_{t}^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}f)f\partial_{3}\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}\,\mathrm{d}x+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega}\nabla^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}\cdot\mathring{v}|f|^{2}\partial_{3}\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}\,\mathrm{d}x

(A.10)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega}|f|^{2}\left(\partial_{3}\partial_{t}(\dot{\widetilde{\varphi}}-\mathring{\varphi})+\partial_{3}(\partial_{t}+\overline{\mathring{v}}\cdot\overline{\nabla})(\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}-\dot{\widetilde{\varphi}})\right)\,\mathrm{d}x.

Proof.

It suffices to show (
A.9
) only since the proof of (
A.10
) follows by setting
\mathring{\rho}=1
. We write the first term on the RHS of (
A.9
) as

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\mathring{\rho}(D_{t}^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}f)f\partial_{3}\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}\,\mathrm{d}x=

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\mathring{\rho}(\partial_{t}f)f\partial_{3}\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}\,\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Omega}\mathring{\rho}\left((\overline{\mathring{v}}\cdot\overline{\nabla})f\right)f\partial_{3}\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}\,\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Omega}\mathring{\rho}\left((\mathring{v}\cdot\dot{\widetilde{\mathbf{N}}}-\partial_{t}\mathring{\varphi})\partial_{3}f\right)f\,\mathrm{d}x,

(A.11)

and then integrate
\partial_{t}
,
\overline{\nabla}
and
\partial_{3}
by parts respectively in these terms to get:

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\mathring{\rho}(D_{t}^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}f)f\partial_{3}\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}\,\mathrm{d}x=

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega}\mathring{\rho}|f|^{2}\partial_{3}\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}\,\mathrm{d}x-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega}\left(\partial_{t}\mathring{\rho}+\overline{\mathring{v}}\cdot\overline{\nabla}\mathring{\rho}+\frac{1}{\partial_{3}\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}(\mathring{v}\cdot\dot{\widetilde{\mathbf{N}}}-\partial_{t}\mathring{\varphi})\partial_{3}\mathring{\rho}\right)|f|^{2}\partial_{3}\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}\,\mathrm{d}x

(A.12)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega}\mathring{\rho}(\overline{\nabla}\cdot\overline{\mathring{v}})|f|^{2}\partial_{3}\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}\,\mathrm{d}x-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega}\mathring{\rho}|f|^{2}(\partial_{t}+\overline{\mathring{v}}\cdot\overline{\nabla})\partial_{3}\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega}\mathring{\rho}\partial_{3}(-(\overline{\mathring{v}}\cdot\overline{\nabla})\dot{\widetilde{\varphi}}+\mathring{v}_{3}-\partial_{t}\mathring{\varphi})|f|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x,

where we used
\mathring{v}\cdot\dot{\widetilde{\mathbf{N}}}=-(\overline{\mathring{v}}\cdot\overline{\nabla})\dot{\widetilde{\varphi}}+\mathring{v}_{3}
in the last line.
We find that the second integral in the first line is
\int_{\Omega}D_{t}^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}\mathring{\rho}|f|^{2}\partial_{3}\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}\,\mathrm{d}x
. Also, the term in the last line can be written as

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega}\mathring{\rho}\partial_{3}(-(\overline{\mathring{v}}\cdot\overline{\nabla})\dot{\widetilde{\varphi}}+\mathring{v}_{3}-\partial_{t}\mathring{\varphi})|f|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x

(A.13)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega}\mathring{\rho}|f|^{2}\left(\frac{1}{\partial_{3}\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}\partial_{3}v_{3}-\frac{\overline{\partial}{}_{1}\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}{\partial_{3}\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}\partial_{3}\mathring{v}_{1}-\frac{\overline{\partial}{}_{2}\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}{\partial_{3}\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}\partial_{3}\mathring{v}_{2}\right)\partial_{3}\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega}\mathring{\rho}|f|^{2}\partial_{3}\overline{\mathring{v}}\cdot\overline{\nabla}(\dot{\widetilde{\varphi}}-\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}})\,\mathrm{d}x+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega}\mathring{\rho}|f|^{2}(\partial_{t}\partial_{3}\mathring{\varphi}+(\overline{\mathring{v}}\cdot\overline{\nabla})\partial_{3}\dot{\widetilde{\varphi}})\,\mathrm{d}x.

The first term on the RHS together with the third term in (
A.12
)
contributes to

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega}\mathring{\rho}(\nabla^{\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}}\cdot\mathring{v})|f|^{2}\partial_{3}\mathring{\widetilde{\varphi}}\,\mathrm{d}x

in
(
A.9
). Meanwhile, the terms in the last line of (
A.13
) together with the fourth term in (
A.12
) give the terms in (
A.9
) with mismatches.
∎

Appendix B
Construction of initial data for the original system

This section aims to construct the initial data for Theorem
1.2
and Theorem
1.3
satisfying the compatibility conditions

(D_{t}^{\varphi})^{j}q|_{\{t=0\}\times\Sigma}=(D_{t}^{\varphi})^{j}(\sigma\mathcal{H})|_{\{t=0\}\times\Sigma},\quad\partial_{t}^{j}v^{3}|_{\{t=0\}\times\Sigma_{b}}=0,\quad j=0,1,2,3.

Since
D_{t}^{\varphi}|_{\Sigma}=\partial_{t}+\overline{v}\cdot\overline{\partial}{}
and
\mathcal{H}=-\overline{\nabla}\cdot\left(\frac{\overline{\nabla}\psi}{\sqrt{1+|\overline{\nabla}\psi|^{2}}}\right)
, we rewrite the compatibility conditions in terms of
\check{q}
as

(\partial_{t}+\overline{v}\cdot\overline{\partial}{})^{j}\check{q}|_{\{t=0\}\times\Sigma}=(\partial_{t}+\overline{v}\cdot\overline{\partial}{})^{j}\left({-\sigma}\overline{\nabla}\cdot\frac{\overline{\nabla}\psi}{\sqrt{1+|\overline{\nabla}\psi|^{2}}}+g\psi\right)\Bigg|_{\{t=0\}\times\Sigma},\quad j=0,1,2,3.

(B.1)

Here, we use the modified pressure
\check{q}
since we want
\partial\check{q}_{0}\in L^{2}(\Omega)
for the sake of convenience.
Such compatibility conditions are required to show that
E(t)
(defined as (
1.33
)), and
E^{\lambda,\sigma}(t)
(defined as (
1.39
)) are bounded at
t=0
by adapting the arguments in
[
18
, Section 4.3]
.

B.1
Formal construction

We shall adapt the method developed in
[
18
]
to construct smooth data
(\psi_{0},v_{0},\check{q}_{0})
that satisfies (
B.1
).
We first describe the method formally which serves as a good guideline. The key difference, however, is that in
[
18
]
we constructed the initial data in Lagrangian coordinates, where (
B.1
) has a different formulation.

By identifying
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{\lambda}(q)=\lambda^{2}
without loss of generality, and since
\partial_{1}\varphi|_{\Sigma}=\partial_{1}\psi
,
\partial_{2}\varphi|_{\Sigma}=\partial_{2}\psi
,
\partial_{3}\varphi|_{\Sigma}=1
,
the momentum and continuity equations reduce respectively to

\displaystyle\rho(\partial_{t}+\overline{v}\cdot\overline{\partial}{})v+\nabla^{\varphi}\check{q}=-g(\rho-1)e_{3},

\displaystyle~~\text{on}\,\,\Sigma

(B.2)

\displaystyle\lambda^{2}(\partial_{t}+\overline{v}\cdot\overline{\partial}{})\check{q}+\text{div}\,v=\partial_{1}\psi\partial_{3}v^{1}+\partial_{2}\psi\partial_{3}v^{2}+\lambda^{2}gv^{3},

\displaystyle~~\text{on}\,\,\Sigma,

(B.3)

where
\nabla^{\varphi}q=(\partial_{1}q-\partial_{1}\psi\partial_{3}q,\partial_{2}q-\partial_{2}\psi\partial_{3}q,\partial_{3}q)^{\top}
and
\text{div}\,v=\partial\cdot v
. By ignoring the terms contributed by the denominator, we have

\mathcal{H}\sim-\overline{\Delta}{}\psi
. Invoking the kinematic boundary condition
\partial_{t}\psi=v\cdot N
, we have

(\partial_{t}+\overline{v}\cdot\overline{\partial}{})\psi=v^{3},\quad\text{on }\,\Sigma,

we obtain from the zeroth compatibility condition
\check{q}\sim-\sigma\overline{\Delta}{}\psi
that

\displaystyle(\partial_{t}+\overline{v}\cdot\overline{\partial}{})\check{q}\sim-\sigma\overline{\Delta}{}v^{3},\quad\text{on }\,\Sigma,

(B.4)

which is the first compatibility condition.
Since the continuity equation (
B.3
) implies
\lambda^{2}(\partial_{t}+\overline{v}\cdot\overline{\partial}{})\check{q}\sim-\text{div}\,v
, we can deduce from (
B.4
) that:

\text{div}\,v\sim\sigma\lambda^{2}\overline{\Delta}{}v^{3},\quad\text{on}\,\,\Sigma.

(B.5)

Furthermore, the momentum equation (
B.2
) implies
(\partial_{t}+\overline{v}\cdot\overline{\partial}{})v^{3}\sim-\partial_{3}\check{q}
, and thus the second compatibility condition on
\check{q}
becomes:

(\partial_{t}+\overline{v}\cdot\overline{\partial}{})^{2}\check{q}\sim-\sigma(\partial_{t}+\overline{v}\cdot\overline{\partial}{})\overline{\Delta}{}v^{3}\sim\sigma\overline{\Delta}{}\partial_{3}\check{q},\quad\text{on}\,\,\Sigma.

(B.6)

Taking
\partial_{t}+\overline{v}\cdot\overline{\partial}{}
to the continuity equation to obtain
\lambda^{2}(\partial_{t}+\overline{v}\cdot\overline{\partial}{})^{2}\check{q}\sim-\text{div}\,(\partial_{t}+\overline{v}\cdot\overline{\partial}{})v\sim\Delta\check{q}
, and this gives

\partial_{3}^{2}\check{q}\sim\sigma\lambda^{2}\overline{\Delta}{}\partial_{3}\check{q}-\overline{\Delta}{}\check{q},\quad\text{on}\,\,\Sigma.

(B.7)

Finally, we derive from the third compatibility condition on
\check{q}
that

\displaystyle(\partial_{t}+\overline{v}\cdot\overline{\partial}{})^{3}\check{q}\sim\sigma\overline{\Delta}{}\partial_{3}(\partial_{t}+\overline{v}\cdot\overline{\partial}{})\check{q}\sim\sigma\lambda^{-2}\overline{\Delta}{}\partial_{3}\text{div}\,v,\quad\text{on}\,\,\Sigma,

(B.8)

together with the relation
\lambda^{2}(\partial_{t}+\overline{v}\cdot\overline{\partial}{})^{3}\check{q}\sim\Delta(\partial_{t}+\overline{v}\cdot\overline{\partial}{})\check{q}\sim\lambda^{-2}\Delta\text{div}\,v
obtained by taking
(\partial_{t}+\overline{v}\cdot\overline{\partial}{})^{2}
to the continuity equation that

\displaystyle\Delta\text{div}\,v\sim\sigma\lambda^{2}\overline{\Delta}{}\partial_{3}\text{div}\,v,\quad\text{on}\,\,\Sigma.

(B.9)

In other words,

\displaystyle\partial_{3}^{3}v\sim\sigma\lambda^{2}\overline{\Delta}{}\partial_{3}\text{div}\,v-\Delta\partial_{1}v-\Delta\partial_{2}v-\overline{\Delta}{}\partial_{3}v,\quad\text{on}\,\,\Sigma.

(B.10)

Therefore, the first order compatibility condition on
\check{q}
yields an “identity in terms of
v
” (
B.5
), the second order compatibility condition on
\check{q}
yields an “identity in terms of
q
” (
B.7
), and lastly, the third order compatibility condition on
\check{q}
yields an “identity in terms of
v
” again (
B.10
).

We construct our data by the following iterative procedure. To begin with, let
(\xi_{0},\textbf{w}_{0},p_{0})
be the generic smooth
localized
incompressible data that verifies the zeroth order compatibility condition
\check{p}_{0}=-\sigma\overline{\nabla}\cdot\frac{\overline{\nabla}\xi_{0}}{\sqrt{1+|\overline{\nabla}\xi_{0}|^{2}}}+g\xi_{0}
on
\Sigma
. In the first step, we fixed a smooth function
\psi_{0}
which represents the moving interface, and constructed the data satisfying the first compatibility condition. Given (
B.5
), we shall need to construct the appropriate velocity vector field denoted by
\textbf{u}_{0}=(\textbf{u}_{0}^{1},\textbf{u}_{0}^{2},\textbf{u}_{0}^{3})
. We achieve this by setting
\textbf{u}_{0}^{1}=\textbf{w}_{0}^{1}
,
\textbf{u}_{0}^{2}=\textbf{w}_{0}^{2}
, and construct
\textbf{u}_{0}^{3}
by solving a poly-harmonic equation of order 2:

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\Delta^{2}\textbf{u}_{0}^{3}=\Delta^{2}\textbf{w}_{0}^{3},\quad&\text{in}\,\,\Omega,\\
\textbf{u}_{0}^{3}=\textbf{w}_{0}^{3},\quad\partial_{3}\textbf{u}_{0}^{3}\sim-\partial_{1}\textbf{w}_{0}^{1}-\partial_{2}\textbf{w}_{0}^{2}+\sigma\lambda^{2}\overline{\Delta}{}\textbf{w}_{0}^{3},\quad&\text{on}\,\,\Sigma,\\
\textbf{u}_{0}^{3}=\textbf{w}_{0}^{3},\quad\partial_{3}\textbf{u}_{0}^{3}=\partial_{3}\textbf{w}_{0}^{3}\quad&\text{on}\,\,\Sigma_{b}.\end{cases}

(B.11)

In particular, the boundary condition
\partial_{3}\textbf{u}_{0}^{3}\sim-\partial_{1}\textbf{w}_{0}^{1}-\partial_{2}\textbf{w}_{0}^{2}+\sigma\lambda^{2}\overline{\Delta}{}\textbf{w}_{0}^{3}
is derived from (
B.5
).

In the second step, we construct the data verifying the second compatibility condition. We shall construct
\check{q}_{0}
here because of (
B.7
). This is achieved by solving a poly-harmonic equation of order 3:

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\Delta^{3}\check{q}_{0}=\Delta^{3}\check{p}_{0},\quad&\text{in}\,\,\Omega,\\
\check{q}_{0}=\check{p}_{0},\quad\partial_{3}\check{q}_{0}=\partial_{3}\check{p}_{0},\quad&\text{on}\,\,\Sigma,\\
\partial_{3}^{2}\check{q}_{0}\sim\sigma\lambda^{2}\overline{\Delta}{}\partial_{3}\check{p}_{0}-\overline{\Delta}{}\check{p}_{0},\quad&\text{on}\,\,\Sigma,\\
\partial_{3}^{j}\check{q}_{0}=0~~(0\leq j\leq 2),\quad&\text{on}\,\,\Sigma_{b}.\end{cases}

(B.12)

It can be seen that the boundary condition
\partial_{3}^{2}\check{q}_{0}\sim\sigma\lambda^{2}\overline{\Delta}{}\partial_{3}\check{p}_{0}
is a consequence of (
B.7
).

In the third (and final) step, we construct the data verifying the compatibility conditions up to order
3
with a fixed smooth function representing the moving interface still denoted by
\psi_{0}
. Since
q_{0}
has been constructed, we need only to construct
v_{0}=(v_{0}^{1},v_{0}^{2},v_{0}^{3})
by setting
\textbf{w}_{0}^{1}=v_{0}^{1},\textbf{w}_{0}^{2}=v_{0}^{2}
, and solving the following order 4 poly-harmonic equation for
v_{0}^{3}
:

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\Delta^{4}v_{0}^{3}=\Delta^{4}\textbf{u}_{0}^{3},\quad&\text{in}\,\,\Omega,\\
v_{0}^{3}=\textbf{u}_{0}^{3},\quad\partial_{3}v_{0}^{3}\sim-\partial_{1}\textbf{u}_{0}^{1}-\partial_{2}\textbf{u}_{0}^{2}+\sigma\lambda^{2}\overline{\Delta}{}\textbf{u}_{0}^{3}\quad&\text{on}\,\,\Sigma,\\
\partial_{3}^{2}v_{0}^{3}\sim-\partial_{3}\partial_{1}\textbf{u}_{0}^{1}-\partial_{3}\partial_{2}\textbf{u}_{0}^{2}+\sigma\lambda^{2}\overline{\Delta}{}\partial_{3}\textbf{u}_{0}^{3},\quad&\text{on}\,\,\Sigma,\\
\partial_{3}^{3}v_{0}^{3}=-\Delta\partial_{1}\textbf{u}_{0}^{1}-\Delta\partial_{2}\textbf{u}_{0}^{2}+\sigma\lambda^{2}\overline{\Delta}{}\partial_{3}\text{div}\,\textbf{u}_{0}-\overline{\Delta}{}\partial_{3}\textbf{u}_{0}^{3},\quad&\text{on}\,\,\Sigma,\\
\partial_{3}^{j}v_{0}^{3}=\partial_{3}^{j}\textbf{u}_{0}^{3}~~(0\leq j\leq 3)\quad&\text{on}\,\,\Sigma_{b}.\end{cases}

(B.13)

The second and third boundary conditions arise from (
B.5
), whereas the fourth boundary condition is derived from (
B.10
).

B.2
The full construction procedure

We shall repeat the method introduced in Subsection
B.1
with detailed boundary conditions generated by the compatibility conditions. We will use
\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}
to denote generic non-negative continuous functions. Apart from this, we will set

0\leq k^{\prime}\leq 1,\quad 0\leq k\leq 2,\quad 0\leq l\leq 3,

throughout.

By invoking the commutator

\displaystyle[\overline{\partial}{}^{s},\partial_{t}+\overline{v}\cdot\overline{\partial}{}]=[\overline{\partial}{}^{s},\overline{v}]\cdot\overline{\partial}{},

(B.14)

and since it holds on
\Sigma
that

(\partial_{t}+\overline{v}\cdot\overline{\partial}{})\psi=v^{3},\quad\check{q}=-\sigma\left(\frac{\overline{\Delta}{}\psi}{|N|}-\frac{\overline{\partial}{}\psi\cdot\overline{\partial}{}\overline{\nabla}\psi}{|N|^{3}}\right)+g\psi,\quad|N|=\sqrt{1+|\overline{\nabla}\psi|^{2}},

the first compatibility condition on
\check{q}
reads:

\displaystyle(\partial_{t}+\overline{v}\cdot\overline{\partial}{})\check{q}=\sigma\mathcal{P}(\frac{1}{|N|},\overline{\partial}{}^{k}\psi,\overline{\partial}{}^{k}\overline{v},\overline{\partial}{}^{k}v^{3}),\quad\text{on}\,\,\Sigma.

(B.15)

In addition, the continuity equation (
B.3
) gives

\displaystyle\lambda^{2}(\partial_{t}+\overline{v}\cdot\overline{\partial}{})\check{q}=-\text{div}\,v+\overline{\partial}{}\psi\cdot\partial_{3}\overline{v}+\lambda^{2}gv^{3},\quad\text{on}\,\,\Sigma.

(B.16)

Hence, we combine (
B.15
) and (
B.16
) to get

\text{div}\,v=\sigma\lambda^{2}\mathcal{P}(|N|^{-1},\overline{\partial}{}^{k}\psi,\overline{\partial}{}^{k}\overline{v},\overline{\partial}{}^{k}v^{3},\partial_{3}\overline{v}),\quad\text{on}\,\,\Sigma.

(B.17)

and the equation used to determine
\textbf{u}_{0}^{3}
is

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\Delta^{2}\textbf{u}_{0}^{3}=\Delta^{2}\textbf{w}_{0}^{3},\quad&\text{in}\,\,\Omega,\\
\textbf{u}_{0}^{3}=\textbf{w}_{0}^{3},\quad&\text{on}\,\,\Sigma\cup\Sigma_{b},\\
\partial_{3}\textbf{u}_{0}^{3}=-\partial_{1}\textbf{w}_{0}^{1}-\partial_{2}\textbf{w}_{0}^{2}+\sigma\lambda^{2}\mathcal{P}(|N_{0}|^{-1},\overline{\partial}{}^{k}\psi_{0},\overline{\partial}{}^{k}\overline{\mathbf{w}}_{0},\overline{\partial}{}^{k}\textbf{w}_{0}^{3},\partial_{3}\overline{\mathbf{w}}_{0}),\quad&\text{on}\,\,\Sigma,\\
\partial_{3}\textbf{u}_{0}^{3}=\partial_{3}\textbf{w}_{0}^{3}\quad&\text{on}\,\,\Sigma_{b}.\end{cases}

(B.18)

whose rough version is given by (
B.11
). Let
s_{0}\geq 8
. The poly-harmonic estimate yields

\displaystyle\|\textbf{u}_{0}^{3}-\textbf{w}_{0}^{3}\|_{s_{0}}\lesssim\underbrace{\|\Delta^{2}(\textbf{u}_{0}^{3}-\textbf{w}_{0}^{3})\|_{s_{0}-4}}_{=0}+\underbrace{|\textbf{u}_{0}^{3}-\textbf{w}_{0}^{3}|_{s_{0}-0.5}}_{=0}+|\partial_{3}(\textbf{u}_{0}^{3}-\textbf{w}_{0}^{3})|_{s_{0}-1.5}\leq\lambda^{2}C(|\psi_{0}|_{s},\|\textbf{w}_{0}\|_{s}),

(B.19)

for some
s>s_{0}
, and hence
\|\textbf{u}_{0}^{3}-\textbf{w}_{0}^{3}\|_{s_{0}}\to 0
as
\lambda\to 0
.

We construct
\check{q}_{0}
using the second-order compatibility condition in the next stage. Owing to (
B.2
), the identities

\displaystyle\rho(\partial_{t}+\overline{v}\cdot\overline{\partial}{})\overline{v}+\overline{\partial}{}\check{q}=\overline{\partial}{}\psi\partial_{3}\check{q},\quad\text{and}\,\,\rho(\partial_{t}+\overline{v}\cdot\overline{\partial}{})v_{3}+\partial_{3}\check{q}=-g(\rho-1),

(B.20)

hold on
\Sigma
, and we view
\rho=\rho(\check{q})
here and throughout. Taking
\partial_{t}+\overline{v}\cdot\overline{\partial}{}
to (
B.15
) and invoking (
B.14
), we have

\displaystyle(\partial_{t}+\overline{v}\cdot\overline{\partial}{})^{2}\check{q}=\sigma\mathcal{P}(\rho^{-1},|N|^{-1},\overline{\partial}{}^{l}\psi,\overline{\partial}{}^{k}\overline{v},\overline{\partial}{}^{k}v^{3},\overline{\partial}{}^{l}\check{q},\overline{\partial}{}^{k}\partial_{3}\check{q}),\quad\text{on}\,\,\Sigma.

(B.21)

Moreover,
by taking
\partial_{t}+\overline{v}\cdot\overline{\partial}{}
to the continuity equation (
B.3
), we get

\displaystyle\lambda^{2}(\partial_{t}+\overline{v}\cdot\overline{\partial}{})^{2}\check{q}=-\text{div}\,(\partial_{t}+\overline{v}\cdot\overline{\partial}{})v+[\text{div}\,,(\partial_{t}+\overline{v}\cdot\overline{\partial}{})]v+(\partial_{t}+\overline{v}\cdot\overline{\partial}{})(\overline{\partial}{}\psi\cdot\partial_{3}\overline{v}+\lambda^{2}gv^{3}),

(B.22)

where
[\text{div}\,,(\partial_{t}+\overline{v}\cdot\overline{\partial}{})]v=\partial_{i}\overline{v}\cdot\overline{\partial}{}v^{i}
,

\displaystyle-\text{div}\,(\partial_{t}+\overline{v}\cdot\overline{\partial}{})\overline{v}=\partial^{\tau}(\rho^{-1}\partial_{\tau}\check{q})-\partial^{\tau}(\rho^{-1}\partial_{\tau}\psi\partial_{3}\check{q})+\underbrace{\partial_{3}(\rho^{-1}\partial_{3}\check{q})}_{=\rho^{-1}\partial_{3}^{2}\check{q}+\partial_{3}\rho^{-1}\partial_{3}\check{q}}+g\partial_{3}\Big(\rho^{-1}(\rho-1)\Big),~~~~~\tau=1,2,

(B.23)

and

\displaystyle(\partial_{t}+\overline{v}\cdot\overline{\partial}{})(\overline{\partial}{}\psi\cdot\partial_{3}\overline{v}+\lambda^{2}gv^{3})=\overline{\partial}{}v^{3}\cdot\partial_{3}\overline{v}+\overline{\partial}{}\psi\cdot\partial_{3}(-\rho^{-1}\overline{\partial}{}\check{q}+\rho^{-1}\overline{\partial}{}\psi\partial_{3}\check{q})

\displaystyle-\overline{\partial}{}\overline{v}\cdot\overline{\partial}{}\psi\cdot\partial_{3}\overline{v}-\overline{\partial}{}\psi\cdot\partial_{3}\overline{v}\cdot\partial_{3}\overline{v}+\lambda^{2}g(-\rho^{-1}\partial_{3}\check{q}-g\rho^{-1}(\rho-1)).

(B.24)

Since the third term on the RHS of (
B.2
) contributes to
\rho^{-1}|\overline{\partial}{}\psi|^{2}\partial_{3}^{2}\check{q}
, it holds that

\displaystyle\lambda^{2}(\partial_{t}+\overline{v}\cdot\overline{\partial}{})^{2}\check{q}=\rho^{-1}(1+|\overline{\partial}{}\psi|^{2})\partial_{3}^{2}\check{q}+\mathcal{Q}(\rho^{-1},|N|^{-1},\overline{\partial}{}^{k}\psi,\overline{\partial}{}^{k^{\prime}}\partial_{3}v,\overline{\partial}{}^{k^{\prime}}\partial_{3}\check{q}),\quad\text{on}\,\,\,\Sigma.

(B.25)

Therefore, we combine (
B.21
) and (
B.25
) to get

\displaystyle\rho^{-1}(1+|\overline{\partial}{}\psi|^{2})\partial_{3}^{2}q=\sigma\lambda^{2}\mathcal{P}(\rho^{-1},|N|^{-1},\overline{\partial}{}^{l}\psi,\overline{\partial}{}^{k}\overline{v},\overline{\partial}{}^{k}v^{3},\overline{\partial}{}^{l}q,\overline{\partial}{}^{k}\partial_{3}q)+\mathcal{Q}(\rho^{-1},|N|^{-1},\overline{\partial}{}^{k}\psi,\overline{\partial}{}^{k^{\prime}}\partial_{3}v,\overline{\partial}{}^{k^{\prime}}\partial_{3}q),\quad\text{on}\,\,\Sigma,

(B.26)

and we set
\check{q}_{0}
by solving

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\Delta^{3}\check{q}_{0}=\Delta^{3}\check{p}_{0},\quad&\text{in}\,\,\Omega,\\
\check{q}_{0}=\check{p}_{0},\quad\partial_{3}\check{q}_{0}=\partial_{3}\check{p}_{0},\quad&\text{on}\,\,\Sigma,\\
\partial_{3}^{2}\check{q}_{0}=\rho_{0}(1+|\overline{\partial}{}\psi_{0}|^{2})^{-1}\bigg(\sigma\lambda^{2}\mathcal{P}(\rho_{0}^{-1},|N_{0}|^{-1},\overline{\partial}{}^{l}\psi_{0},\overline{\partial}{}^{k}\overline{\mathbf{u}}_{0},\overline{\partial}{}^{k}\textbf{u}_{0}^{3},\overline{\partial}{}^{l}\check{p}_{0},\overline{\partial}{}^{k}\partial_{3}\check{p}_{0})\\
+\mathcal{Q}(\rho_{0}^{-1},|N_{0}|^{-1},\overline{\partial}{}^{k}\psi_{0},\overline{\partial}{}^{k^{\prime}}\partial_{3}\textbf{u}_{0},\overline{\partial}{}^{k^{\prime}}\partial_{3}\check{p}_{0})\bigg),\quad&\text{on}\,\,\Sigma,\\
\partial_{3}^{j}\check{q}_{0}=0~~(0\leq j\leq 2)\quad&\text{on}\,\,\Sigma_{b}.\end{cases}

(B.27)

whose rough version is (
B.12
). Also, the poly-harmonic estimate implies

\displaystyle\|\check{q}_{0}\|_{s_{0}}\lesssim\|\Delta^{3}\check{p}_{0}\|_{s_{0}-6}+|\check{p}_{0}|_{s_{0}-0.5}+|\partial_{3}\check{p}_{0}|_{s_{0}-1.5}+|\partial_{3}^{2}\check{q}_{0}|_{s-2.5}\leq\lambda^{2}C_{1}(|\psi_{0}|_{s},\|\textbf{u}_{0}\|_{s},\|\check{p}_{0}\|_{s})+C_{2}(|\psi_{0}|_{s},\|\textbf{u}_{0}\|_{s},\|\check{p}_{0}\|_{s}),

(B.28)

for some
s>s_{0}
.

Finally, we construct
v_{0}^{3}
using the third-order compatibility condition in the last stage. We obtain

\displaystyle(\partial_{t}+\overline{v}\cdot\overline{\partial}{})^{3}\check{q}=\sigma\mathcal{P}(\rho^{-1},|N|^{-1},\overline{\partial}{}^{l}\psi,\overline{\partial}{}^{l}\overline{v},\overline{\partial}{}^{l}v^{3},\partial^{l}\check{q})\left(\lambda^{-2}\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\psi+\lambda^{-2}\overline{\partial}{}^{4}v+\lambda^{-2}\overline{\partial}{}^{l}\partial_{3}v+\lambda^{-2}\overline{\partial}{}^{k}\partial_{3}^{2}v\right),\quad\text{on}\,\,\Sigma,

(B.29)

by taking
(\partial_{t}+\overline{v}\cdot\overline{\partial}{})
to (
B.21
). Further, taking
(\partial_{t}+\overline{v}\cdot\overline{\partial}{})
to (
B.25
) to get

\displaystyle\lambda^{2}(\partial_{t}+\overline{v}\cdot\overline{\partial}{})^{3}\check{q}=-\lambda^{-2}\rho^{-1}(1+|\overline{\partial}{}\psi|^{2})\partial_{3}^{2}\text{div}\,v+\mathcal{Q}(\rho^{-1},|N|^{-1},\overline{\partial}{}^{l}\psi,\overline{\partial}{}^{l}v,\overline{\partial}{}^{k}\partial_{3}v,\overline{\partial}{}^{k^{\prime}}\partial_{3}^{2}v,\overline{\partial}{}^{l}\check{q}),\quad\text{on}\,\,\,\Sigma.

(B.30)

Therefore, we combine (
B.29
) and (
B.30
) to obtain

\displaystyle\rho^{-1}(1+|\overline{\partial}{}\psi|^{2})\partial_{3}^{2}\text{div}\,v=\sigma\lambda^{2}\mathcal{P}(\rho^{-1},|N|^{-1},\overline{\partial}{}^{l}\psi,\overline{\partial}{}^{l}\overline{v},\overline{\partial}{}^{l}v^{3},\partial^{l}\check{q})\left(\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\psi+\overline{\partial}{}^{4}v+\overline{\partial}{}^{l}\partial_{3}v+\overline{\partial}{}^{k}\partial_{3}^{2}v\right)

(B.31)

\displaystyle+\lambda^{2}\mathcal{Q}(\rho^{-1},|N|^{-1},\overline{\partial}{}^{l}\psi,\overline{\partial}{}^{l}\psi,\overline{\partial}{}^{l}v,\overline{\partial}{}^{k}\partial_{3}v,\overline{\partial}{}^{k^{\prime}}\partial_{3}^{2}v,\overline{\partial}{}^{l}\check{q}),\quad\text{on}\,\,\,\Sigma,

and we set
v_{0}^{3}
by solving

\begin{cases}\Delta^{4}v_{0}^{3}=\Delta^{4}\textbf{u}_{0}^{3},\quad&\text{in}\,\,\Omega,\\
v_{0}^{3}=\textbf{u}_{0}^{3},\quad&\text{on}\,\,\Sigma,\\
\partial_{3}v_{0}^{3}=-\partial_{1}\textbf{u}_{0}^{1}-\partial_{2}\textbf{u}_{0}^{2}+\sigma\lambda^{2}\mathcal{P}(|N_{0}|^{-1},\overline{\partial}{}^{k}\psi_{0},\overline{\partial}{}^{k}\textbf{u}_{0},\overline{\partial}{}^{k}\textbf{u}_{0}^{3},\partial_{3}\overline{\mathbf{u}}_{0}),\quad 0\leq k\leq 2,\quad&\text{on}\,\,\Sigma,\\
\partial_{3}^{2}v_{0}^{3}=-\partial_{1}\partial_{3}\textbf{u}_{0}^{1}-\partial_{2}\partial_{3}\textbf{u}_{0}^{2}+\sigma\lambda^{2}\partial_{3}\mathcal{P}(|N_{0}|^{-1},\overline{\partial}{}^{k}\psi_{0},\overline{\partial}{}^{k}\textbf{u}_{0},\overline{\partial}{}^{k}\textbf{u}_{0}^{3},\partial_{3}\overline{\mathbf{u}}_{0}),\quad&\text{on}\,\,\Sigma,\\
\partial_{3}^{3}v_{0}^{3}=\rho_{0}(1+|\overline{\partial}{}\psi_{0}|^{2})^{-1}\bigg(\sigma\lambda^{2}\mathcal{P}(\rho_{0}^{-1},|N_{0}|^{-1},\overline{\partial}{}^{l}\psi_{0},\overline{\partial}{}^{l}\overline{\mathbf{u}}_{0},\overline{\partial}{}^{l}\textbf{u}_{0}^{3},\partial^{l}\check{q}_{0})\left(\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\psi_{0}+\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\textbf{u}_{0}+\overline{\partial}{}^{l}\partial_{3}\textbf{u}_{0}+\overline{\partial}{}^{k}\partial_{3}^{2}\textbf{u}_{0}\right)\\
+\lambda^{2}\mathcal{Q}(\rho_{0}^{-1},|N_{0}|^{-1},\overline{\partial}{}^{l}\psi_{0},\overline{\partial}{}^{l}\psi,\overline{\partial}{}^{l}\textbf{u}_{0},\overline{\partial}{}^{k}\partial_{3}\textbf{u}_{0},\overline{\partial}{}^{k^{\prime}}\partial_{3}^{2}\textbf{u}_{0},\overline{\partial}{}^{l}\check{q}_{0})\bigg)-\rho_{0}^{-1}(1+|\overline{\partial}{}\psi_{0}|^{2})\partial_{3}^{2}(\partial_{1}\textbf{u}_{0}^{1}+\partial_{2}\textbf{u}_{0}^{2}),\quad&\text{on}\,\,\Sigma,\\
\partial_{3}^{j}v_{0}^{3}=\partial_{3}^{j}\textbf{u}_{0}^{3}~~(0\leq j\leq 3)\quad&\text{on}\,\,\Sigma_{b}.\end{cases}

(B.32)

whose rough version is (
B.13
). By the poly-harmonic estimate, we have

\displaystyle\|v_{0}^{3}-\textbf{u}_{0}^{3}\|_{s_{0}}\lesssim\|\Delta^{4}(v_{0}^{3}-\textbf{u}_{0}^{3})\|_{s_{0}-8}+|v_{0}^{3}-\textbf{u}_{0}^{3}|_{s_{0}-0.5}+|\partial^{3}(v_{0}^{3}-\textbf{u}_{0}^{3})|_{s_{0}-1.5}+|\partial_{3}^{2}(v_{0}^{3}-\textbf{u}_{0}^{3})|_{s_{0}-2.5}+|\partial^{3}(v_{0}^{3}-\textbf{u}_{0}^{3})|_{s_{0}-3.5}.

(B.33)

The first two terms on the RHS are
0
. Invoking (
B.19
), (
B.28
), we have, for some
s,s^{\prime}
satisfying
s>s^{\prime}>s_{0}
, that

|v_{0}^{3}-\textbf{u}_{0}^{3}|_{s_{0}-0.5}+|\partial^{3}(v_{0}^{3}-\textbf{u}_{0}^{3})|_{s_{0}-1.5}\leq\lambda^{2}C(|\psi_{0}|_{s^{\prime}},\|\textbf{u}_{0}\|_{s^{\prime}})\leq\lambda^{2}C(|\psi_{0}|_{s},\|\textbf{w}_{0}\|_{s}),

and

|\partial_{3}^{2}(v_{0}^{3}-\textbf{u}_{0}^{3})|_{s_{0}-2.5}\leq\lambda^{2}C(|\psi_{0}|_{s^{\prime}},\|\textbf{u}_{0}\|_{s^{\prime}},\|\check{q}_{0}\|_{s^{\prime}})\leq\lambda^{2}C(|\psi_{0}|_{s},\|\textbf{w}_{0}\|_{s},\|\check{p}_{0}\|_{s}).

Thus,

\displaystyle\|v_{0}^{3}-\textbf{u}_{0}^{3}\|_{s_{0}}\leq\lambda^{2}C(|\psi_{0}|_{s},\|\textbf{w}_{0}\|_{s},\|\check{p}_{0}\|_{s}).

(B.34)

In particular, since we have set
\textbf{w}_{0}^{\tau}=\textbf{u}_{0}^{\tau}=v_{0}^{\tau}
,
\tau=1,2
, we deduce from (
B.19
) and (
B.34
) that

\displaystyle\|v_{0}-\textbf{w}_{0}\|_{s_{0}}\leq\|v_{0}^{3}-\textbf{u}_{0}^{3}\|_{s_{0}}+\|\textbf{u}_{0}^{3}-\textbf{w}_{0}^{3}\|_{s_{0}}=O(\lambda^{2}).

(B.35)

In addition, we deduce from
\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot\textbf{w}_{0}=0
and (
B.35
) that

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot v_{0}\|_{C^{1}}=O(\lambda^{2}).

(B.36)

Apart from these, it can be seen from (
B.27
) and (
B.32
) that
\|v_{0}\|_{s_{0}}
and
\|\check{q}_{0}\|_{s_{0}}
are uniform in both
\sigma
and
\lambda
. This allows us to take the zero surface tension and incompressible limits at the same time.

Appendix C
Construction of initial data for the nonlinear
\kappa
-approximate system

The construction of smooth initial data for the
\kappa
-problem (
3.11
) is parallel to what has been done in the previous section and thus we shall only sketch the details. We will set

0\leq k^{\prime}\leq 1,\quad 0\leq k\leq 2,\quad 0\leq l\leq 3,\quad 0\leq m\leq 4,\quad 0\leq n\leq 5

in the sequel.

Let
(\psi_{0},v_{0},q_{0})
be the smooth initial data constructed in the previous section. Our goal is to construct
(\psi_{\kappa,0},v_{\kappa,0},q_{\kappa,0})
that satisfies the
\kappa
-compatibility conditions up to the third order:

\displaystyle(\partial_{t}+\overline{v}\cdot\overline{\partial}{})^{j}q|_{\{t=0\}\times\Sigma}=\sigma(\partial_{t}+\overline{v}\cdot\overline{\partial}{})^{j}\mathcal{H}|_{\{t=0\}\times\Sigma}

\displaystyle+\kappa^{2}(\partial_{t}+\overline{v}\cdot\overline{\partial}{})^{j}\left((1-\overline{\Delta}{})(-\partial_{1}\widetilde{\psi}v^{1}-\partial_{2}\widetilde{\psi}v^{2}+v^{3})\right)|_{\{t=0\}\times\Omega},\quad

\displaystyle j=0,1,2,3,

(C.1)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}^{j}v^{3}|_{\{t=0\}\times\Sigma_{b}}=0,\quad

\displaystyle j=0,1,2,3.

(C.2)

Setting
\psi_{\kappa,0}=\psi_{0}
, we need only to compute the last term on the RHS to formulate the poly-harmonic equations for
q_{\kappa,0}
and
v_{\kappa,0}
.
Since

[(1-\overline{\Delta}{}),\partial_{t}+\overline{v}\cdot\overline{\partial}{}]=-[\overline{\Delta}{},\overline{v}]\cdot\overline{\partial}{},

we have, when
j=1
:

\displaystyle(\partial_{t}+\overline{v}\cdot\overline{\partial}{})\Big((1-\overline{\Delta}{})(-\overline{\partial}{}\widetilde{\psi}\cdot\overline{v}+v^{3})\Big)=\mathcal{R}(\overline{\partial}{}^{l}\psi,\overline{\partial}{}^{l}v,\overline{\partial}{}^{l}\widetilde{\psi},\overline{\partial}{}^{l}\widetilde{v}^{3},\overline{\partial}{}^{l}\check{q},\overline{\partial}{}^{k}\partial_{3}\check{q}),~~\text{on}\,\,\Sigma.

(C.3)

This implies that the equation used to determine
\mathbf{u}_{\kappa,0}^{3}
is

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\Delta^{2}\mathbf{u}_{\kappa,0}^{3}=\Delta^{2}v_{0}^{3},\quad&\text{in}\,\,\Omega,\\
\mathbf{u}_{\kappa,0}^{3}=v_{0}^{3},\quad&\text{on}\,\,\Sigma,\\
\partial_{3}\mathbf{u}_{\kappa,0}^{3}=-\partial_{1}v_{0}^{1}-\partial_{2}v_{0}^{2}+\sigma\lambda^{2}\mathcal{P}(|N_{0}|^{-1},\overline{\partial}{}^{k}\psi_{0},\overline{\partial}{}^{k}v_{0},\overline{\partial}{}^{k}v_{0}^{3},\partial_{3}\overline{v}_{0})\\
+\kappa^{2}\lambda^{2}\mathcal{R}(\overline{\partial}{}^{l}\psi_{0},\overline{\partial}{}^{l}v_{0},\overline{\partial}{}^{l}\widetilde{\psi}_{0},\overline{\partial}{}^{l}\widetilde{v}_{0}^{3},\overline{\partial}{}^{l}\check{q}_{0},\overline{\partial}{}^{k}\partial_{3}\check{q}_{0}),\quad&\text{on}\,\,\Sigma,\\
\partial_{3}^{j}v_{\kappa,0}^{3}=\partial_{3}^{j}\textbf{u}_{0}^{3}~~(0\leq j\leq 1)\quad&\text{on}\,\,\Sigma_{b}.\end{cases}

(C.4)

which is parallel to (
B.18
).

Then, when
j=2
, we have

\displaystyle(\partial_{t}+\overline{v}\cdot\overline{\partial}{})^{2}\Big((1-\overline{\Delta}{})(-\overline{\partial}{}\widetilde{\psi}\cdot\overline{v}+v^{3})\Big)=(\partial_{t}+\overline{v}\cdot\overline{\partial}{})\mathcal{R}(\overline{\partial}{}^{l}\psi,\overline{\partial}{}^{l}v,\overline{\partial}{}^{l}\widetilde{\psi},\overline{\partial}{}^{l}\widetilde{v}^{3},\overline{\partial}{}^{l}\check{q},\overline{\partial}{}^{k}\partial_{3}\check{q})

\displaystyle=\mathcal{R}(\overline{\partial}{}^{l}\psi,\overline{\partial}{}^{l}\widetilde{\psi},\overline{\partial}{}^{l}v^{3},\overline{\partial}{}^{l}\widetilde{v}^{3},\overline{\partial}{}^{m}\check{q},\overline{\partial}{}^{l}\partial_{3}\check{q},\lambda^{-2}\overline{\partial}{}^{4}v,\lambda^{-2}\overline{\partial}{}^{l}\partial_{3}v,\lambda^{-2}\overline{\partial}{}^{k}\partial_{3}^{2}v,\lambda^{-2}\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\psi),\quad\text{on}\,\,\Sigma,

(C.5)

where the power of
\lambda^{-1}
does not exceed
2
.
Thus, we determine
q_{\kappa,0}
by solving

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\Delta^{3}\check{q}_{\kappa,0}=\Delta^{3}\check{q}_{0},\quad&\text{in}\,\,\Omega,\\
\check{q}_{\kappa,0}=\check{q}_{0},\quad\partial_{3}\check{q}_{\kappa,0}=\partial_{3}\check{q}_{0},\quad&\text{on}\,\,\Sigma,\\
\partial_{3}^{2}\check{q}_{\kappa,0}=\rho_{0}(1+|\overline{\partial}{}\psi_{0}|^{2})^{-1}\bigg(\sigma\lambda^{2}\mathcal{P}(\rho_{0}^{-1},|N_{0}|^{-1},\overline{\partial}{}^{l}\psi_{0},\overline{\partial}{}^{k}\overline{\mathbf{u}}_{\kappa,0},\overline{\partial}{}^{k}\mathbf{u}_{\kappa,0}^{3},\overline{\partial}{}^{l}\check{q}_{0},\overline{\partial}{}^{k}\partial_{3}\check{q}_{0})\\
+\mathcal{Q}(\rho_{0}^{-1},|N_{0}|^{-1},\overline{\partial}{}^{k}\psi_{0},\overline{\partial}{}^{k^{\prime}}\partial_{3}\mathbf{u}_{\kappa,0},\overline{\partial}{}^{k^{\prime}}\partial_{3}\check{q}_{0})\\
+\kappa^{2}\lambda^{2}\mathcal{R}(\overline{\partial}{}^{l}\psi_{0},\overline{\partial}{}^{l}\widetilde{\psi}_{0},\overline{\partial}{}^{l}\mathbf{u}_{\kappa,0}^{3},\overline{\partial}{}^{l}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{\kappa,0}^{3},\overline{\partial}{}^{m}\check{q}_{0},\overline{\partial}{}^{l}\partial_{3}\check{q}_{0},\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\mathbf{u}_{\kappa,0},\overline{\partial}{}^{l}\partial_{3}\mathbf{u}_{\kappa,0},\overline{\partial}{}^{k}\partial_{3}^{2}\mathbf{u}_{\kappa,0},\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\psi_{0})\bigg),\quad&\text{on}\,\,\Sigma,\\
\partial_{3}^{j}\check{q}_{0}^{3}=0~~(0\leq j\leq 2)\quad&\text{on}\,\,\Sigma_{b}.\end{cases}

(C.6)

Finally, when
j=3
, we have

\displaystyle(\partial_{t}+\overline{v}\cdot\overline{\partial}{})^{3}\Big((1-\overline{\Delta}{})(-\overline{\partial}{}\widetilde{\psi}\cdot\overline{v}+v^{3})\Big)=\mathcal{R}(\overline{\partial}{}^{m}\psi,\overline{\partial}{}^{m}\widetilde{\psi},\overline{\partial}{}^{m}v^{3},\overline{\partial}{}^{m}\widetilde{v}^{3},\overline{\partial}{}^{n}\check{q},\overline{\partial}{}^{m}\partial_{3}\check{q},\lambda^{-2}\overline{\partial}{}^{5}v,\lambda^{-2}\overline{\partial}{}^{m}\partial_{3}v,\lambda^{-2}\overline{\partial}{}^{l}\partial_{3}^{2}v,\lambda^{-2}\overline{\partial}{}^{5}\psi),\quad\text{on}\,\,\Sigma,

(C.7)

where the power of
\lambda^{-1}
does not exceed
4
. Therefore, we construct
v_{\kappa,0}^{3}
by solving

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\Delta^{4}v_{\kappa,0}^{3}=\Delta^{4}\mathbf{u}_{\kappa,0}^{3},\hskip 9.24994pt&\text{in}\,\,\Omega,\\
v_{\kappa,0}^{3}=\mathbf{u}_{\kappa,0}^{3},\hskip 9.24994pt&\text{on}\,\,\Sigma,\\
\partial_{3}v_{\kappa,0}^{3}=-\partial_{1}\mathbf{u}_{\kappa,0}^{1}-\partial_{2}\mathbf{u}_{\kappa,0}^{2}+\sigma\lambda^{2}\mathcal{P}(|N_{0}|^{-1},\overline{\partial}{}^{k}\psi_{0},\overline{\partial}{}^{k}\mathbf{u}_{\kappa,0},\overline{\partial}{}^{k}\mathbf{u}_{\kappa,0}^{3},\partial_{3}\overline{\mathbf{u}}_{\kappa,0})\\
+\kappa^{2}\lambda^{2}\mathcal{R}(\overline{\partial}{}^{l}\psi_{0},\overline{\partial}{}^{l}\mathbf{u}_{\kappa,0},\overline{\partial}{}^{l}\widetilde{\psi}_{0},\overline{\partial}{}^{l}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{\kappa,0}^{3},\overline{\partial}{}^{l}\check{q}_{\kappa,0},\overline{\partial}{}^{k}\partial_{3}\check{q}_{\kappa,0}),\hskip 9.24994pt&\text{on}\,\,\Sigma,\\
\partial_{3}^{2}v_{\kappa,0}^{3}=-\partial_{1}\partial_{3}\mathbf{u}_{\kappa,0}^{1}-\partial_{2}\partial_{3}\mathbf{u}_{\kappa,0}^{2}+\sigma\lambda^{2}\partial_{3}\mathcal{P}(|N_{0}|^{-1},\overline{\partial}{}^{k}\psi_{0},\overline{\partial}{}^{k}\mathbf{u}_{\kappa,0},\overline{\partial}{}^{k}\mathbf{u}_{\kappa,0}^{3},\partial_{3}\overline{\mathbf{u}}_{\kappa,0})\\
+\kappa^{2}\lambda^{2}\partial_{3}\mathcal{R}(\overline{\partial}{}^{l}\psi_{0},\overline{\partial}{}^{l}\mathbf{u}_{\kappa,0},\overline{\partial}{}^{l}\widetilde{\psi}_{0},\overline{\partial}{}^{l}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{\kappa,0}^{3},\overline{\partial}{}^{l}\check{q}_{\kappa,0},\overline{\partial}{}^{k}\partial_{3}\check{q}_{\kappa,0}),\hskip 9.24994pt&\text{on}\,\,\Sigma,\\
\partial_{3}^{3}v_{\kappa,0}^{3}=\rho_{0}(1+|\overline{\partial}{}\psi_{0}|^{2})^{-1}\bigg(\sigma\lambda^{2}\mathcal{P}(\rho_{0}^{-1},|N_{0}|^{-1},\overline{\partial}{}^{l}\psi_{0},\overline{\partial}{}^{l}\overline{\mathbf{u}}_{\kappa,0},\overline{\partial}{}^{l}\mathbf{u}_{\kappa,0}^{3},\partial^{l}\check{q}_{0})\left(\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\psi_{0}+\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\mathbf{u}_{\kappa,0}+\overline{\partial}{}^{3}\partial_{3}\mathbf{u}_{\kappa,0}+\overline{\partial}{}^{2}\partial_{3}^{2}\mathbf{u}_{\kappa,0}\right)\\
+\lambda^{2}\mathcal{Q}(\rho_{0}^{-1},|N_{0}|^{-1},\overline{\partial}{}^{l}\psi_{0},\overline{\partial}{}^{l}\textbf{u}_{0},\overline{\partial}{}^{k}\partial_{3}\textbf{u}_{0},\overline{\partial}{}^{k^{\prime}}\partial_{3}^{2}\textbf{u}_{0},\overline{\partial}{}^{l}\check{q}_{\kappa,0})\\
+\mathcal{R}(\overline{\partial}{}^{m}\psi_{0},\overline{\partial}{}^{m}\widetilde{\psi}_{0},\overline{\partial}{}^{m}\mathbf{u}_{\kappa,0}^{3},\overline{\partial}{}^{m}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{\kappa,0}^{3},\overline{\partial}{}^{n}\check{q}_{\kappa,0},\overline{\partial}{}^{m}\partial_{3}\check{q}_{\kappa,0},\overline{\partial}{}^{5}\mathbf{u}_{\kappa,0},\overline{\partial}{}^{m}\partial_{3}\mathbf{u}_{\kappa,0},\overline{\partial}{}^{l}\partial_{3}^{2}\mathbf{u}_{\kappa,0},\overline{\partial}{}^{5}\psi_{0})\bigg)\\
-\rho_{0}^{-1}(1+|\overline{\partial}{}\psi_{0}|^{2})\partial_{3}^{2}(\partial_{1}\mathbf{u}_{\kappa,0}^{1}+\partial_{2}\mathbf{u}_{\kappa,0}^{2}),\hskip 9.24994pt&\text{on}\,\,\Sigma,\\
\partial_{3}^{j}v_{\kappa,0}^{3}=\partial_{3}^{j}\textbf{u}_{0}^{3}~~(0\leq j\leq 3)\hskip 9.24994pt&\text{on}\,\,\Sigma_{b}.\end{cases}

(C.8)

Let
\lambda>0
be fixed. Invoking the poly-harmonic estimate subsequently to (
C.4
), (
C.6
), and (
C.8
), we obtain that
\|v_{\kappa,0}\|_{s_{0}}
and
\|\check{q}_{\kappa,0}\|_{s_{0}}
are bounded for some
s_{0}\geq 8
.
Thus, the energy
E^{\kappa}(t)
(defined as (
4.1
)) is bounded at
t=0
.
In addition,

\displaystyle\|v_{\kappa,0}-v_{0}\|_{s_{0}},~\text{and}~\|\check{q}_{\kappa,0}-\check{q}\|_{s_{0}}\rightarrow 0,\quad\text{as}\,\,\,\kappa\to 0.

Appendix D
Paraproducts and the Dirichlet-to-Neumann operator

D.1
Bony’s paraproduct decomposition

We already introduce the paradifferential operator in Section
7.2
. Here we present the relations between paradifferential operators and paraproducts. The cutoff function
\tilde{\chi}(\xi,\eta)
in the definition of
T_{a}u
is

\tilde{\chi}(\xi,\eta)=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\Theta_{k-3}(\xi)\vartheta(\eta),

where
\Theta(\xi)=1
when
|\xi|\leq 1
and
\Theta(\xi)=0
when
|\xi|\geq 2
and

\Theta_{k}(\xi):=\Theta(\frac{\xi}{2}),~~k\in\mathbb{Z},\quad\vartheta_{0}=\Theta,\quad\vartheta_{k}:=\Theta_{k}-\Theta_{k-1},~~k\geq 1.

Based on this, we can introduce the Littlewood-Paley projections
\mathbf{P}_{k}
and
\mathbf{P}_{\leq k}
as follows

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathbf{P}_{k}u}(\xi):=\vartheta_{k}(\xi)\hat{u}(\xi),~~\forall k\geq 0,\quad\mathbf{P}_{k}u:=0~~\forall k<0,\quad\mathbf{P}_{\leq k}u:=\sum_{l\leq k}\mathbf{P}_{l}u.

When the symbol
a(x,\xi)
(in the paradifferential operator
T_{a}
) does not depend on
\xi
, we can take
\psi(\eta)\equiv 1
and then we have

T_{a}u=\sum_{k}\mathbf{P}_{\leq k-3}a(\mathbf{P}_{k}u)

which is the usual Bony’s paraproduct. In general, the well-known Bony’s paraproduct decomposition is

au=T_{a}u+T_{u}a+R(u,a),\quad R(u,a)=\sum_{|k-l|\leq 2}(\mathbf{P}_{k}a)(\mathbf{P}_{l}u).

We have the following estimates for the remainder
R(u,a)

Lemma D.1

(
[
2
, Section 2.3]
)
.

For
s\in\mathbb{R},~r<d/2,~\delta>0
, we have

|T_{a}u|_{H^{s}}\lesssim\min\{|a|_{L^{\infty}}|u|_{H^{s}},|a|_{H^{r}}|u|_{H^{s+\frac{d}{2}-r}},|a|_{H^{\frac{d}{2}}}|u|_{H^{s+\delta}}\}

and for any
s>0,s_{1},s_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
satisfying
s_{1}+s_{2}=s+\frac{d}{2}
, we have

|R(u,a)|_{H^{s}}\lesssim|a|_{H^{s_{1}}}|u|_{H^{s_{2}}}.

D.2
Basic properties of the Dirichlet-to-Neumann operator

Let the space dimension
d=3
for simplicity. Given a function
f:\Sigma=\mathbb{T}^{2}\to\mathbb{R}
, we define the Dirichlet-to-Neumann (DtN) operator (with respect to
\psi
and region
\Omega^{\pm}
) by

\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}f:=\mp N\cdot\nabla^{\varphi}(\mathcal{E}_{\psi}^{\pm}f)|_{\Sigma},\quad-\Delta^{\varphi}(\mathcal{E}_{\psi}^{\pm}f)=0\text{ in }\Omega^{\pm},~~\mathcal{E}_{\psi}^{\pm}f|_{\Sigma}=f,~~\partial_{3}(\mathcal{E}_{\psi}^{\pm}f)|_{\Sigma^{\pm}}=0.

Here the Laplacian operator is defined by
\Delta^{\varphi}:=\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot\nabla^{\varphi}=\partial_{i}(\bm{E}^{ij}\partial_{j})
with

\bm{E}=\frac{1}{\partial_{3}\varphi}\begin{bmatrix}\partial_{3}\varphi&0&-\overline{\partial}{}_{1}\varphi\\
0&\partial_{3}\varphi&-\overline{\partial}{}_{2}\varphi\\
-\overline{\partial}{}_{1}\varphi&-\overline{\partial}{}_{2}\varphi&\frac{1+|\overline{\nabla}\varphi|^{2}}{\partial_{3}\varphi}\end{bmatrix}=\frac{1}{\partial_{3}\varphi}\bm{P}\bm{P}^{\top},~~\bm{P}:=\begin{bmatrix}\partial_{3}\varphi&0&0\\
0&\partial_{3}\varphi&0\\
-\overline{\partial}{}_{1}\varphi&-\overline{\partial}{}_{2}\varphi&1\end{bmatrix},

and
\varphi(t,x):=x_{3}+\chi(x_{3})\psi(t,x^{\prime})
is defined as the extension of
\psi
into
\Omega^{\pm}
. The choice of
\chi(x_{3})
is slightly different from
[
2
,
3
,
5
]
, but it does not introduce any substantial difference because the expression of
\Delta^{\varphi}
is still written to be
\Delta^{\varphi}:=\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot\nabla^{\varphi}=\partial_{i}(\bm{E}^{ij}\partial_{j})
and we have
\Delta^{\varphi}\varphi=0
in
\Omega^{\pm}
. The DtN operators satisfy the following estimates and we refer to
[
63
, Appendix A.4]
for the proof.

Lemma D.2

(Sobolev estimates for DtN operators)
.

For
s>2+\frac{d}{2},~-\frac{1}{2}\leq r\leq s-1
and
\psi\in H^{s}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, we have

|\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}f|_{r}\leq C(|\psi|_{s})|f|_{r+1}.

Lemma D.3

(Remainder estimates for DtN operators)
.

For
s>2+\frac{d}{2}
and
\psi\in H^{s}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, we have

\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}f=T_{\Lambda}f+R_{\Lambda}^{\psi}(f)

with
\Lambda
defined in Proposition
7.8
. The remainder
R_{\Lambda}^{\psi}(f)
satisfies

|R_{\Lambda}^{\psi}(f)|_{r}\leq C(|\psi|_{s+\frac{1}{2}})|f|_{r}.

Lemma D.4

(Sobolev estimates for the inverse of the DtN operator)
.

For
s>2+\frac{d}{2},~-\frac{1}{2}\leq r\leq s-1
and
\psi\in H^{s}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, we have

|(\mathfrak{N}_{\psi})^{-1}f|_{r+1}\leq C(|\psi|_{s})|f|_{r}.

Lemma D.5

(Commutator estimate for the DtN operator and its square root)
.

For
s>2+\frac{d+1}{2}
and
\psi\in H^{s}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, we have

|[\mathfrak{N}_{\psi},a]f|_{r-1}\leq C(|\psi|_{s})|a|_{r+1}|f|_{r}\quad\forall 0<r\leq s-\frac{1}{2},

and

|[(\mathfrak{N}_{\psi})^{\frac{1}{2}},a]f|_{r-\frac{1}{2}}\leq C(|\psi|_{s})|a|_{r+1}|f|_{r}\quad\forall-\frac{1}{2}<r\leq s-1.
